# Changelog

All notable changes to Pool Plant are recorded here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the game uses [semantic versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html): see "Versions and releases" in the README for what each kind of change means here. Add each change under Unreleased as it is made; a release moves those entries under its own version and date.

## [Unreleased]

- Museum Hall 9, The whole plant, audited against its walls (2026-10-31): the pool stood 0.3 m into the approach from Hall 8's archway and the hypochlorite store 0.8 m into the way on to Hall 0; the pool (with the sample tapping and injection points) and the chemistry row stand clear of both. Both pumps faced the north wall; each turns to face the room, its suction looping round it, the pipework still under 180 m. The entrance panel and the RESET sign hung 40 mm off the east wall, DC1 10 mm off the south, and the desk stood 0.29 m off it; all stand flush. The boiler and the filter stood a few centimetres through the north wall, and the sample tube, hung free, sagged through the floor and the south wall; it is laid square and clipped as the dosing tubes are. A walls test checks wall items flush, every mesh inside the hall and above its floor, nothing across or before an archway, no overlaps, and every machine facing the room.
- Museum Hall 4, The chemistry of pools, review layout updated (2026-10-31): the readout board alone centred on the right (west) wall, the Balance Beam hung opposite on the left wall as its match (scaled to the board's width and height, at its height), its balance-index words moved off it onto a lectern before it; the ideal pool ahead and right of the desk beside the tanks' stand, its display toward the desk, with an ideal balancing tank behind it, the pump beyond in the far right corner, the injection tee between them and the dosing pot at the east end of the manifold strip; the LAB TIME display on the archways' wall between them, under the hall's title.
- Museum exit signs: the hanging signs face into the hall from the way out's wall, their arrows towards it as their readers see it. Hall 4's had faced along its long axis, across its way out, their backs to most of the hall. A test checks every hanging sign's reader faces the way out and its arrow points to it.
- Museum Hall 4, The chemistry of pools, laid out again for the owner's review (2026-10-31): the chemical tanks stand at the back, under the courtyard windows and centred on them, a metre out from the wall (a clear service space behind for the dosing manifold), lifted 0.6 m on a braced steel stand with the bund on its deck as a drip tray; the dosing desk 2 m in front of them on their centre line, its operator looking past it at the raised tanks; the teleprinter on the operator's right, RESET on the left with its "Don't worry" sign on a lectern just behind it; the pump with its valves and flowmeter in the far right corner, facing the room; the analyser's readout board and the Balance Beam side by side, as one group, centred on the right-hand (west) wall; the mimic (The map) on the back wall behind the tanks, centred on them, its foot over their tops as read from the desk and its head under the windows' sills. Temporary, for the review: the pipework is left out of the scene (`PIPES_DEFERRED`) and the station lecterns are parked out of bounds (`LECTERNS_PARKED`), until the hall is re-plumbed.
- Museum Hall 9, The whole plant, bound in full to its own model (2026-10-31): the binding test passes. P1's starter binds to the pool pump; the pump's gauges, the gas and filter gauges (which had named their machines), the thermometer board's TI1 to TI4 and the pool's TI5, the sample cell's isolating valve, needle valve, flow switch, probes and tap, the day tanks' sight gauges, DC1 (the Chlorella CX-7), the DPD test kit, the annunciator, the water panel and the station clock each to its own instrument or fitting. Three instruments in the model were never drawn and now are, working: SG1, a sight glass on the backwash waste that murks with what it carries; FS1, a paddle flow switch on the exchanger's feed, its lamp lit while made; and TC1, the pool thermostat on that feed, its dial setting the heating and its CALL lamp lit while calling. The thermometer board (M9-QB1), the mimic panel (M9-MP1) and the control desk (M9-DS1) join the model as instruments, their parts bound through them. The fault pedestal and scoreboard, the TIME buttons, the RESET pedestal, the entrance panel, the RESET sign and the lecterns are the exhibit's own controls and words, marked decorative.
- Museum Hall 3, Safe water, audited against its walls (2026-10-31): the mimic, the chemistry board, the entrance panel and the RESET sign hung 40 mm off the south wall, and the DC1 pair and its settings plate 10 and 12 mm off the north; all hang flush. The fault pedestal stood through The tanks' lectern; it stands beside its own (When it goes wrong), clear of the columns. The archways were already clear and every machine faces the room. A walls test checks wall items flush, every mesh inside the hall and above its floor, nothing across or before an archway, no overlaps, and every machine facing the room.
- Museum Hall 3, Safe water, bound in full to its own model (2026-10-31): the binding test passes. Pipe markings bind to their links, P1's local station to the pool pump; the sample cell's isolating valve and flow regulator to their fittings, its flow switch, probes and sample tap, the day tanks' sight gauges, the DC1 pair and its settings plate, the DPD test kit, the meters and the chart recorder each to its own instrument. The chemistry board (M3-QB1) and mimic panel (M3-MP1) join the model as instruments, their parts bound through them. The swimmers' dial, the fault pedestal, the plant time display and the RESET pedestal are the exhibit's controls of the simulation, marked decorative.
- Museum Hall 4, The chemistry of pools, laid out anew (the owner, 2026-10-31): the dosing desk stands front and centre before the arrival archway, its working side toward the visitor, the ten tanks a metre behind it, the teleprinter on its right; the ideal pool against the west wall between the Balance Beam and the readout board, with the pump, dosing pot and injection tee between it and the tanks, all re-plumbed; the mimic on the east wall; RESET in the middle of the north wall. The entrance panel is gone. The lecterns stand by what they describe, numbered along the way round (desk, tanks, swimmers, readout, challenges).
- Museum Hall 4, The chemistry of pools, bound in full to its own model (2026-10-31): the binding test passes. The dosing header DH1 is drawn as a manifold under the tanks' outlets; each tank's line has its solenoid valve, energised while the desk doses that tank; the pump's suction now enters its suction nozzle and its discharge leaves the top flange, over the pump to the injection tee, with its lever valves (which work: shut one and the flowmeter, gauges and mimic show nothing circulating), non-return valve and flowmeter FT1. The desk, mimic panel, teleprinter and Balance Beam join the model as instruments; the LAB TIME clock, RESET pedestal, the desk's TIME and CHALLENGE buttons and the tanks' bund are the exhibit's, marked decorative.
- Museum, Hall 7: the furan resin specimen has black grout (the owner, 2026-10-30), its plaque now "Furan grouts are generally black. So is this one."; the specimens are drawn one instanced mesh per grout colour in use (grey, the 1970s' avocado, black).
- Museum Hall 5, A Day in the Life, fixed after the owner's audit (2026-10-31): the photographs hang evenly round all four walls in date order (north, east up to the way on to Hall 6, south, west), 1.0 to 1.3 m apart, where two walls were crowded and the west bare; the entrance panel no longer hangs in the doorway from Hall 4 (it is on the north wall); the RESET pedestal and its sign stand beside the bin; the clock is gone; the "Inside a skimmer" cut-away is on a lectern by the skimmer at the water's edge, facing into the room, its diagram no longer running under its caption; the opening checks sheet and the No running sign hang in gaps of their own on the south wall, clear of every bay; the brass timeline rail runs round all four walls, broken at both doorways (it ran across the way on to Hall 6); everything on the walls is flush (it hung 40 mm off them); and the two columns that stood on the painted pool's edge are left out. Tests check wall items against the doorways, windows, safety fittings and each other, even spacing on every wall, and the rail against the openings.
- Museum, Hall 9 has three courtyard windows on its west wall, evenly from the middle, where it had two (the owner, 2026-10-31: "would benefit from some more windows").
- Museum Hall 6, Grundflow's Amazing World of Pumps, redesigned (the owner, 2026-10-31, replacing the two-pump ruling): six of the game's own pumps, smallest to largest along the route from the north archway to the south, the DMX 1602, DDS 7.5-16, DMH 21-10 and PERI 30 dosing pumps and the NKC 125-250 and NKC 150-315 end-suction pumps; two on the north wall, two on the west (window) wall and two on the south wall, clear of the archways, each facing into the hall with its RUN stand and plaque, drawing from a trough at the foot of its wall (the one ideal reservoir CW1) and filling the test tank through the overhead header HD1, its fill line never above the header. The east wall keeps the mimic and panels; the exhibit is no longer turned half round. The entrance panel's ratio is now about 140,000. A test keeps every mesh inside the hall's walls.
- Museum Hall 1, Warm water, audited against its walls, archways and the facing rule (2026-10-31): the gas main, both pumps, the exchanger and the pool now stand with their fronts (gauges, nameplates, displays) turned out from the street wall into the room, where they faced along the wall or into it; the pool pump's suction line loops round to its suction end, the exchanger's frame plate faces south toward the tees, and the pipes are re-routed by the router. The pump's local station moves off the front of the pump, the pump and pool placards before their machines, and the losses board beyond them. The east wall's mimic, clock, time display, placards, RESET sign and gas burn display hang flush on the wall (they stood 40 mm off it). A walls test (`hall-1-walls.test.ts`) checks wall items flush, every mesh inside the hall, nothing across or before an archway, nothing standing through anything else, and every machine facing the room.
- Museum Hall 1, Warm water, bound in full to its own model (2026-10-31): the binding test passes. Each pipe's engraved plates and flow arrows bind to its link; P2's local control station to the pool pump; the pump's suction and discharge gauges, the gas pressure gauge, the four line thermometers, the pool's big temperature display, the flow switch and the thermostat (and its stem) each to its own instrument (they were bound to their machines, or not at all). The gas burn display (M1-EC1), the losses board with its SWIMMERS dial (M1-QB1) and the mimic panel (M1-MP1) join the model as instruments, their parts bound through them. The pool clock, the plant time display and the RESET pedestal are marked decorative: they are the exhibit's, and work no plant.
- Museum Hall 2, Clean water, bound in full to its own model (2026-10-31): the binding test passes. Each pipe's engraved plates and flow arrows bind to its link; P1's local control station to the pump; the pump's suction and discharge gauges, the filter's inlet, outlet and differential gauges (the last with its stand), the injector's vacuum gauge, the tank's level and the pool's temperature displays, the UV unit's intensity display, the sight glass, both dissolved-ozone analysers and the ozone-in-air monitor each to its own instrument (they were bound to their machines or lines, or not at all). The mimic panel joins the model as an instrument (M2-MP1, coded MP as the plant rooms' mimic panels are), its lamps, readouts and buttons bound through it. The pool clock, the plant time display and the RESET pedestal are marked decorative: they are the exhibit's, and work no plant. The sand filter, UV unit and ideal vessel views now expose their gauges and displays for binding.
- Museum Hall 0, the welcome hall, fixed after its audit (2026-10-31): all four walls are tiled (the north and east were plain, the plan having them face other halls); the tiled cove leaves a gap at the fire exit door's foot, as at the lift's; the ceiling sprinkler that stood in the caustics projector's ceiling plate moves to (0, 1.5); the exit sign hung inside the west archway points, on its room side, right (towards the fire exit, to the right and a little behind), each face worked out with `arrowTo`; the route heading counts its halls from the plan ("THE ROUTE: NINE HALLS, AND BACK HERE"), in the owner's words file too, which also now sends visitors west through the archway. The lifebuoy sticker's place moves into the plan, and tests keep it and every wall item out of the archways, the cove broken at the lift and the fire exit, and the welcome panel clear of the medical wall and the archways' approaches.
- Museum, Hall 9 cleared for layout C (112): both archways are in its east end wall, so the pool stands 2 m further west (`EAST_CLEAR`), clear of the way in from Hall 8, every pipe routed again; the entrance panel and RESET, which hung in the way on to Hall 0, move onto the east wall between the two archways. In the walkway test.
- Museum Hall 8, Never again, fixed after the layout C audit (2026-10-31): the opening panel no longer hangs across the east archway; it stays on the east wall north of the arch, its place worked out from the arch (0.45 m clear of the jamb), and chapter 1 with its photograph and lectern moves 0.5 m west so the lectern keeps out of the archway's approach; a test checks that nothing on a wall crosses an archway and that no lectern stands within 2.5 m before one.
- Museum, Hall 4 has no columns (the owner, 2026-10-31: "get rid of the columns in this room"; `NO_COLUMNS`); its roof's beams span wall to wall. Its layout drawn for the owner to rearrange: `doc/temp/museum4-layout.dxf` (and `.svg`), each piece of the exhibit a named block where it stands, the walls cut at the archways, the windows and the wall boards.
- Museum way-finding signs hang in the corridors (the owner, 2026-10-31): no longer on the halls' walls over the archways, but hung on two rods from the deck 0.6 m inside each archway, 2.88 × 0.64 m within the corridor, below the arch's crown, double-sided: each face names the hall the one reading it walks towards, NEXT EXHIBIT or BACK TO; both halls build the corridor with its two signs.
- Museum, Hall 0 (the owner, 2026-10-31): the welcome panel stands before the west wall, south of the archway into Hall 1, between the wall and the painted pool, facing into the hall; the lifebuoy sticker moves to the west wall south of that archway; the panel's words now say to follow the arrows west through the archway, and the route nine halls.
- Museum Hall 6, Grundflow's Amazing World of Pumps, fixed after the layout C audit (2026-10-31): the wall clock comes down below the hall's title (its top under 2.5 m, its placard under it); the split-case pump's GRUNDFLOW badge and rating plate sit on its motor foot on the visitors' side (they floated 1.35 m behind the casing), and the mag-drive pump's badge moves to its fan end, its front, by the facing rule; the west wall, behind the reservoir trough, gets no sockets (three were hidden behind it). The fill lines rise from each pump and run level into the header, never above it (the DN250 line looped up to 4.5 m and down, a loop that would trap air; the DN25 line and the tank fill stepped up 0.1 m), the overflow, now DN50, and the drain fall all the way to the tundish at 1 in 40, and tests check both. The layout comments describe the turned hall.
- Museum: the STAFF ONLY door no longer stands half sunk in the floor (the owner, 2026-10-30): its sticker's lift to its foot is baked into its plane, since the safety layer's merge dropped the mesh's own offset.
- Museum, Hall 3: the STAFF ONLY door moves to the west end wall, between the two archways; on the street wall it stood behind the hypochlorite store (the audit, 2026-10-30).
- Museum Hall 4, The chemistry of pools, fixed after the layout C audit (2026-10-31): the entrance panel hangs on the north wall beside the arrival archway, facing into the hall, outside the exhibit's half turn (it had turned with the exhibit to stand by the exit with its back to both arrivals); RESET and its sign stand beside the dosing desk, lectern 2 moved west so it hides neither. The make-up pipe now carries over the pool's rim to a spout above the water, the 40 mm header runs into the dosing pot, the pump's discharge meets the injection tee, and the sample line runs to the wall and up into the readout board's underside. The mimic, challenge cards board and readout board hang with their backs on their walls (they stood 40 mm off). The pump, teleprinter and lectern footprints are sized from their meshes, and the teleprinter's extra plinth is gone (it stands on its own pedestal). The pump's front faces the arrival archway and the hall's middle, by the facing rule. Tests check every pipe run's ends against its nodes (within 5 mm) and the wall boards flush (within 0.05 m).
- Museum, Hall 1 rearranged for layout C (112): both archways are at the west end, where its plant stood along the street wall, so the whole exhibit stands turned half round, the plant along the east wall and a clear walk down the west side; the gas main and the pool moved off the columns (the audit), and every pipe routed again with the columns kept clear. In the walkway and column tests.
- Museum Hall 2, Clean water, fixed after the layout C audit (2026-10-30): the entrance panel, RESET sign and pedestal, filter settings card and ozone-in-air monitor hang flush on the east wall again, and the mimic, activity card, pool clock, plant-time display and clock placard on the south wall (they floated about 5 m off, carried by the L's shift); their walls are worked out from `hallInterior` less `PLANT_SHIFT`, and they are spread along the longer walls. Columns are no longer put up through an exhibit (`EXHIBIT_FOOTPRINTS` in `museum-plan.ts`): Hall 2 loses the two north-row columns that went through lectern 7, the ozone bay and the contact tank's pipe. The hanging exit signs in a hall whose way out is off in a corner point towards it (`arrowTo`: up-left or up-right, or to the side) instead of straight on at a blank wall. A test keeps every Hall 2 wall item within 0.05 m of a wall and every column clear of its solids and pipes.
- Museum columns kept clear by the pipe router (the owner, 2026-10-30: "mark the columns as exclusion zones for each room before we start plumbing it ... at least 10 centimeters outside of their bounding cylinder"): one shared keep-out per hall (`columnKeepOut`: each column's widest part, its 0.66 m abacus, and `COLUMN_CLEARANCE` 0.1 m all round, floor to the router's ceiling), used by Halls 1, 3 and 9's plumbers, which had moved their columns by the hall's origin twice, so the router never saw the real ones; every pipe routed again; a test keeps every pipe of Halls 1, 3 and 9, and every machine of Halls 3 and 9, 0.1 m clear of every column.
- Museum, Hall 5's photographs rehung for layout C (112; the owner, 2026-10-30: "there are pictures blocking the corridor"): the way on to Hall 6 is now at the east wall's south end, where the last decades hung, so six decades hang along the north wall, three down the east wall clear of that archway, and the 2020s on the south wall's east end; the Pool of the Future moves west of the No running sign; the entrance panel's route follows.
- Museum corridors: no second pendant beyond the hand-over (the owner, 2026-10-30: "a weird extra reflection of a light on the most distant corridor wall"). Each hall split its copy of an L-shaped corridor the other way round (its own leg took the corner) and hung a lamp and a services ceiling over each leg, so the hall beyond, drawn through the hand-over at reduced resolution, showed its lamp where this hall had none. Both halls now cut every corridor into the same pieces (`corridorPieces`): the corner, and each arm without it, a pendant over each arm, the services seeded by the pair of halls.
- Museum, Hall 3 rearranged for layout C (112; the owner, 2026-10-30: "we still have stuff blocking the exit, and we have a signpost blocking the door"): the hypochlorite and the acid, their tanks, pumps and lecterns, stand 5 m further east (`DOOR_CLEAR`), clear of the way on to Hall 4 at the west end of the north wall, the sample cell between them and the balance tank, every pipe routed again; the entrance panel, which hung in the new archway from Hall 2, and RESET move east along the south wall; the lecterns and the DPD test kit reshuffled clear of the columns. In the walkway test.
- Ambient occlusion: the development note "AO BAKING" shows only for BAKED (the slow ray-traced bake), never for SIMPLE (the owner, 2026-10-30).

- Museum, Hall 6 rearranged for layout C (112; the owner, 2026-10-30: "swap things around so they're on the opposite wall"): the whole plant, trough, pumps, header, tank and their pipes, stands turned half round, the trough against the west wall, so the east side is a clear walk between its two archways; the split-case showpiece now greets the visitor by the way in. In the walkway test.
- Museum corridors built as the halls are (the owner, 2026-10-30: "the same style for the windows in the corridors as ... in the hall ... that curved top, and it's slightly inset"): their walls by the halls' own wall builder (`wallRun`), in the halls' paint, the windows cut through them with round heads, glazed in their reveals with the halls' glazing bars.
- Museum windows all far above head height (the owner, 2026-10-30): every window in the halls and the corridors from 4.2 m to 7.3 m (`WINDOW_SILL`, `WINDOW_HEAD`), the corridors' as wide as the halls' (2.4 m).
- Museum corridors as tall as the halls (the owner, 2026-10-30: "the same height as the rooms that they connect ... the same ceiling style, and the same windows"): 9.5 m, under the halls' metal roof deck and cellular beams with a few services, their windows the halls' tall gridded street windows, 2.4 m wide from 3.2 m up, all above head height; the pendants hung lower from the deck, larger.
- Museum, Hall 0: the way-out sign (stairs, toilets, cloakroom, the exhibition) moves to the north wall, between the lift and the fire exit, its arrows turned to suit (the owner, 2026-10-30).
- Museum, Hall 4 rearranged for layout C (112; the owner, 2026-10-30: "put everything that is on the door side on the opposite side of the hall"): its whole exhibit, tanks, desk, mimic, pot, pool, Balance Beam and lecterns with their pipes, stands turned half round along the south wall facing north, its readout board on the west wall, so the north side is a clear walk between its two archways; checked by the new walkway test (`walkways.test.ts`), which every rearranged hall joins.
- Museum corridors have windows (the owner, 2026-10-30): along the leg that runs along the ring, in both its walls, one side looking out of the building and the other into the courtyard, white with the sky, in steel frames with a mullion and a transom; never in the leg leaving a hall, whose walls face along the ring (`corridorWindows`).
- Museum: the hashtag is now #ThePlantRooms, on the title wall, the banners and the closing words (the owner, 2026-10-30; it was #FindThePlantRoom).
- Museum corridors made big and industrial (the owner, 2026-10-30): square in section, 4.4 m wide and 5.6 m high, bare concrete walls and ceiling on the halls' polished floor, with one hard right-angled corner (no more kit corridor), entered from each hall through a 3.6 m round-headed archway; one of the Encabulator's industrial pendant lamps (after Lamp Copper's Astra) hangs on its chain in each leg, each with a warm light. Every corner is still blind: the halls stand 9.5 m staggered and 5 m apart, Halls 2 and 7 grown 5 m. Each doorway's lit EXIT box is straight over its archway, the NEXT EXHIBIT or BACK TO header above it; a test keeps every corridor clear of every window. Hall 0's fire exit and the freight lift's landing move to its north wall, opposite the PLANTROOMS title wall, clear of the doorway into Hall 1, with the defibrillator and first-aid kit beside them; the notices move beside that doorway.
- Museum windows and doorway signs for layout C (the owner, 2026-10-30): a hall's windows are only in walls that look out of the building (obscured) or into the courtyard (clear), never in a wall with a corridor's doorway (`wallOutlook`); each NEXT EXHIBIT and BACK TO header slides along its wall, off its doorway's middle, as far as it must to clear the corner.
- SIMPLE ambient occlusion fixed (the owner, 2026-10-30: open wall shaded, corners not): the plant room's box was scaled twice (plan metres times `METRE`, then by the room's own scale), so its walls, floor and ceiling were nine times too far away and only stray occluders darkened anything; a museum hall's floor was never baked (its tiled canvas texture was taken for a display); and long thin things' boxes (pipe runs, cables) darkened open wall. Now every wall-floor, wall-wall and wall-ceiling junction darkens over about 0.6 m; occluders are only big solid things (smallest side 0.2 m, 0.05 m³, filling their box, never spanning half the room, never cables, conduits, trays, signs, labels or plates by name or `userData.aoOccluder = false`); the room's own surfaces are subdivided near its planes and occluders instead of by probing. `?aodebug=1` shows the raw bake as grey with the room's box (red) and occluders (yellow) outlined. plant_pool_01 bakes in about 1.2 s.
- SIMPLE ambient occlusion, now the default (the owner, 2026-10-30; [report](doc/reports/cheap-ambient-occlusion.md)): instead of ray tracing, each room's per-vertex occlusion is worked out once, analytically, from a few boxes: its own walls, floor and ceiling (the plan's rectangle in plant rooms and museum halls, else the shell's bounds), each adding its share so a three-way corner is darker than a junction; up to 32 occluder boxes from its biggest meshes (machines, plinths, tanks), so floors darken round a machine's foot; and, in plant rooms, more darkening far from the lamps. It runs in the same worker, subdivides faces near junctions as before, and fills the same `aoBake` attribute, material patch, fade-in and cache (under keys of its own): about 0.7–1.2 s of worker time for museum4, museum2 and plant_pool_01, nothing per frame. The AMBIENT OCCLUSION switch now has four positions, LIVE, BAKED (the ray-traced bake, once ONE-TIME), SIMPLE and OFF; a saved ONE-TIME becomes SIMPLE. Looks per kind of room (`SIMPLE_AO_LOOKS`): the museum subtle, plant rooms grimier; `?aomethod=rays|simple` picks one for a room to compare.
- Museum, layout C (the owner, 2026-10-30): the ten halls stand apart round the ring, staggered 5 m along each side, and each pair of neighbours is joined by an L-shaped corridor of the corridor kit (1.8 m out, a turn, 4.8 m along; one shape, mirrored for a left turn) round the ring's outside, so no hall can be seen from another (`museum-layout.ts`). Each hall builds both its corridors and the visitor is handed to the next hall at each corridor's turn; the neighbouring halls' copies, the empty halls and the far-hall pass are gone. Halls 3 and 9 stand long north–south, their exhibits in their own frames; Halls 2 and 7 are 3 m bigger (19 × 17 m) so Hall 2's doorways clear its plant, which keeps its corner. Exhibits are rearranged for their new doorways hall by hall.
- The gas cylinder builder (the owner, 2026-10-30): one shared builder (`src/toolkit/components/plant/views/gas-cylinder.ts`, its data and rules in `logic/gas-cylinders.ts`) draws any industrial gas cylinder the supplier, Arncliffe Gases, fills: oxygen, nitrogen, argon, carbon dioxide, compressed air, helium, hydrogen, acetylene, propane, butane, chlorine and refrigerant R134a, in UK sizes from 2 l to 50 l and 7 kg to 47 kg. Shoulders in BS EN 1089-3's colours, bodies as UK suppliers paint them; BS EN ISO 7225 shoulder labels with the gas, UN number, ADR diamonds (2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 5.1, 8) and short hazard statements; the supplier's collar label, stamped serial, pressures, tare and test date; a valve with handwheel or key spindle, its outlet left-hand for flammable gases, a residual pressure valve where typical, under a tulip guard, a cap or an LPG shroud; FULL, IN USE or EMPTY and TEST DUE tags. Shapes, paints and labels are made once per kind and shared; a tag changes by swapping its material. Dev55 shows one of each gas, other sizes, a small cage and a trolley, and names each cylinder and its tag in the overlay. The CO₂ cage's and the propane cage's cylinders now use it; the CO₂ banks' tags follow the supply (FULL, IN USE, EMPTY).
- Hall 7, the grout hall: each plinth's approach spotlight now visibly switches on as the walker comes near (the owner, 2026-10-30, reported it never did). The approach was still detected, but since the round spots it only raised the plinth's always-on pool from 28 to 48 units, too little to see after tone mapping. It is now its own spot from the plinth's lamp head, dark when off and faded up to a pool of light round the plinth (`approachLampAt`), the plaque at its soft edge; the round pools are unchanged, and the oil wheel and era music still follow the plinth approached.
- Hall 6, World of Pumps: two pumps, the only two found nowhere else in the game (the owner, 2026-10-30): the MX 6 mag-drive pump (now M6-P1) and the HSC 250-500 split case (now M6-P2). The four dosing pumps (DMX 1602, DDS 7.5-16, DMH 21-10, PERI 30) and the two NKC end-suction pumps are gone with their stands, plaques, RUN stands, readouts, fill lines, mimic lines, slogans and the dosing pumps' raised sounds. The two stand spread along the trough with even margins; the mimic spreads them across its board; the entrance panel's ratio is now about 100 times, and the two-families panel points to the plant rooms for the dosing pumps. The reservoir trough, header, test tank and its tundish drain stay, as both pumps use them.
- Hall 6, World of Pumps: eight pumps, not eleven (the owner, 2026-10-30: the exhibit should not show pumps that appear elsewhere in the game). The Unilift 12 submersible sump pump, the SPA 7 spa pump and the MAGNA D twin-head circulator are gone with their plaques, RUN stands, flow readouts, fill lines, mimic entries and slogans; the rest renumbered (NKC 125-250 now M6-P6, NKC 150-315 M6-P7, HSC 250-500 M6-P8) and the row re-spaced across the hall. The sump pump had looked invisible because it stood at the bottom of the reservoir's 1.05 m opaque trough under the water surface, only its riser showing.
- Museum: no fire points in any hall (the owner, 2026-10-30); their free-standing boards with water and CO₂ extinguishers stood in Halls 1, 3, 5 and 9. Call points, sounders and escape signs stay.
- Museum walls featureless matte 95% grey (#f2f2f2; the owner, 2026-10-30, first 80%): no brick texture, the reveals the same; the far shells and neighbour copies follow, as they share the material. Halls 7 and 8 stay black; Hall 0's tiles, the floors and ceilings are unchanged.
- One filter model for every discrete filter (the owner, 2026-10-30): the ideal filter (`ideal-filter.ts`) now takes the flows at a filter's five ports (inlet, outlet, backwash in, backwash out, rinse) from whatever plumbing it sits in, and its bed and its status (Backwashing, Rinsing, Filtering, Standing) follow from them by one rule. The lounge's pair (IV1, IV2, BV1, DV1), the Olympic's battery (V1 to V5), and the jacuzzi's and the footbath's cabinet programmes are thin layers mapping their valves and pumps onto those ports. The footbath's filter now reports its status too. A lounge filter being rinsed no longer shows its rinse on its outlet flowmeter (its outlet is shut); an Olympic filter with V1, V2 and V5 all open reads Filtering, not Rinsing; a lounge filter in service with its drain open reads Filtering, not Rinsing.
- Plant room 2 (the owner, 2026-10-30): PL2-T2, the ozone contact tank, now has its tag, name and capacity stencilled on its shell, the paint following its curve, instead of a flat engraved plate standing off it; every upright vessel drawn by the shared tank builder (`createTank`, its new `marking` option, `'stencil'` by default for a vessel) is marked so, with its tag from the register. The FILLING lamp of each chemical day tank's inlet (seen floating in mid-air beside PL2's inlet valve) now sits in a grey one-hole control station, strapped to the inlet riser.
- Annunciator push buttons are no longer black on black (the owner, 2026-10-30): on every annunciator (the control room's Warden assembly, each location annunciator in its plant room and its twin in the control room, and the two on the chemical handling kit) ACCEPT is yellow, RESET light grey (a new `grey` operator colour), LAMP TEST stays white and MUTE is blue, after the usual alarm-panel convention (yellow acknowledge, blue silence, grey reset, white test), their legends white on black plates above them.
- Lounge filters: a filter with its inlet and drain open and its outlet shut is now rinsed, the pumps' water running down through its bed to the drain at up to its service flow, and its status reads Rinsing, not Standing (the owner, 2026-10-30, PL1-F2).

- The freight lift's B2 landing moves (mission 108; the owner, 2026-10-30) from the plant corridor's north wall to the south arm's inside wall, centred at x 19.4: the north wall is whole again (blockwork and dado), the new wall is cut for the frame (its dado with it) and keeps solid wall either side for the door stack; posters and fire points keep 1.75 m clear of the landing (`LIFT_CLEAR`), so the poster there moves along the wall; the SERVICE LIFT wayfinding now lists it on the south side, between the boiler house and the switch room; the arrival out of the car faces south into the corridor.
- Every lift doorway made real (the owner, 2026-10-30), through the shared landing and door code: a landing's doors stand 15 cm back in a reveal lined in stainless round its jambs and head, a sill plate across it, the car joining at its sill's edge (`LANDING_SET`, 0.29 m behind the face; the hand-over and arrival follow); a 21 mm black shadow gap round the panels, sides and head, at every landing and inside the car behind its front returns (`SHADOW_GAP`); dark pockets behind the wall for the stacked panels where the wall is thinner than the reveal. Two pairs of doors everywhere, in step: the landing's, in the landing's room and lit only by its lights, and the car's, lit by the car's light, seen one behind the other across the sill gap; closed landing doors are no longer black after the car has called (their panels were hidden while the car was seen and never shown again). The corridor's buffer rail is cut across the B2 landing (`bufferRailRuns`, derived from `CORRIDOR_LIFT`, tested). The arrival chime is heard at every landing: each landing places the listener at its room's camera while in charge (the corridor never placed it), and development builds log each one in `window.liftSounds`.
- Baked ambient occlusion, first stages (`doc/reports/baked-lighting-and-reflections.md`, the owner, 2026-10-30): a room's static meshes (those `mergeStatic` merged, or marked `userData.bakeAo`; never doors, moving or clickable parts) have their large faces subdivided without cracks, are hashed, and are baked per vertex in one background Web Worker with three-mesh-bvh (32 cosine-weighted rays to 1.5 m with a distance falloff); the bake darkens only the ambient light, through a material patch, and fades in over a second once ready, with no AO before. Bakes are cached in IndexedDB by the geometry hash (about 200 MB, least recently used evicted first, corridors, lifts and control rooms never; in memory when storage fails), so a room is baked once. The baker pauses while recent frames run over budget (`reportFrameTime`, `pauseBaking`). Tried in the new dev56 (a plain room: 3.5 s to bake, 0.1 s from the cache) and in every hall's plant room (plant_pool_01: about 21 s in the background, then cached); `?bake=off` shows a room as before, `?bake=fresh` bakes it again.
- Baked ambient occlusion in the museum (the owner, 2026-10-30): its walls (boxes of several materials) were only ever occluders and are now baked, each material patched and its groups carried across subdivision; its depth is 0.2 (a fully occluded spot darkens by at most a fifth), after depth 3 looked far too dirty. In development an AO BAKING note shows while the walker's room is still baking.
- Baked ambient occlusion, looks (the owner, 2026-10-30): each kind of room has its own (`AO_LOOKS`): how it is baked, `depth` (how strongly it darkens) and `direct` (the share of the lamps' direct light it darkens as well as the ambient, so it shows in rooms lit mostly by lamps; still the lights-on bake). Plant rooms are grimy and strong (12 rays, a 1 m grid, no smoothing, depth 2, direct 0.5), the blotchy look the owner liked; the museum is smooth and strong (depth 3, direct 0.8), and its bake is on again (`?bake=nomuseum` to leave it out). `bakedAo.report()` in the browser lists bakes queued, under way and done, and each room's state (applied, meshes patched, strength). `?aolook=`, `?aodepth=` and `?aodirect=` compare looks.
- Baked ambient occlusion: only the walker's room really matters (the owner, 2026-10-30). Its bake goes first; any other room's (a neighbour, the lift's destination) starts only when the game is truly idle (the walker's room done, no hand-over settling, the last 60 frames all well within budget) and pauses the moment a frame rises; gathering and swapping results in run in idle callbacks. Occlusion is baked for the room with every light on: lights take no part, lamps' meshes count whether lit or not, and nothing about lighting enters the cache key. The pool halls and the main corridor are baked; the museum's hook is held back (`?bake=museum` to try it) after it slowed the museum, and distant halls and neighbour copies are never baked.
- Baked ambient occlusion, baking ahead (the owner, 2026-10-30): every room the game builds before it is walked into (the halls round the pool loop, a museum hall's neighbours, the freight lift's destination warmed during the ride) queues its bake as it is built, behind the room the walker is in; on every hand-over and room change (`focusRoom` in `main.ts`) the room entered goes first and the baker stands still for a moment. The baker watches every room's frame times itself and waits while they run over budget. Rooms beyond doorways that the game does not already build ahead are not built for baking: that waits on incremental room preparation (TODO).
- Baked ambient occlusion off the main thread (the owner, 2026-10-30): the game now only lists a room's static meshes and copies their arrays into a few packed buffers, a couple of milliseconds a frame, and hands them to the worker, which does the rest (world space, the hash, the IndexedDB cache, the BVH, finding which faces need subdividing, subdividing, the rays, smoothing, bounds). The result is swapped in a few meshes a frame (under 2 ms and 100 thousand vertices a frame), its shaders compiled beforehand without blocking (`compileAsync`). In plant_pool_01 the main thread's share went from about 200–410 ms in one go to at most 2–4 ms in any frame (about 12–17 ms in all, spread), with no long task from baking; dev56's from 70–120 ms to under 1 ms. Bakes are quicker and better: 16 rays in big rooms; only faces whose corners, edge midpoints and centre differ are subdivided (none wholly buried or wholly open); buried vertices (inside a flange or wall) no longer darken whole faces; see-through materials neither cast nor take occlusion; double-sided parts take their open side; small parts under 3 cm are left out; one smoothing pass. dev56 bakes in 1.2 s (53 thousand vertices, was 3.5 s and 158 thousand). The plant rooms' machines and services now take occlusion too (their parts carry it as they move), canvas displays excepted: plant_pool_01 bakes in about 47 s in the background, then loads from the cache in under a second. The cache key is the room and its meshes' shapes; where each stands is compared within 5 cm (small parts not at all), so a level or a needle moving as the plant runs no longer forces a re-bake.
- Settings: an AMBIENT OCCLUSION switch on the settings panel's new LIGHTING module (the owner, 2026-10-30), LIVE, ONE-TIME or OFF, ONE-TIME by default (`aoMode` in `settings.ts`). LIVE is the screen-space GTAO every frame at the preset's quality (the old quality levels are kept only for it, set by the preset), with no bake shown; ONE-TIME is the baked occlusion, faded in once ready; OFF is neither, and no bake is started (queued bakes wait). Switching acts at once in the room: the bake fades in when ONE-TIME is chosen and goes at once otherwise. An old save with live ambient occlusion at any level becomes ONE-TIME; an old Off stays OFF. Choosing a preset sets ONE-TIME, or OFF on Low. In plant_pool_01, LIVE drew 1,323 calls a frame against 668 for ONE-TIME and OFF.
- Settings: live ambient occlusion (GTAO) is off in every preset and by default (the owner, 2026-10-30), baked occlusion taking its place; LIVE can still be chosen (the AMBIENT OCCLUSION switch, above), and old saves are moved to ONE-TIME. In plant_pool_01 at 2800 × 1800 it took the frame from about 30.6 ms (1,575 draw calls) to 17.4 ms (793 draw calls), the same with its bake shown as without.

- Bridge: after the knob plate's RESET, the bridge's theme starts again from the top on the next visit (the owner, 2026-10-30); it had resumed where it was stopped, or not played at all, because its playlist kept its place between plays.
- Bridge: every LCARS screen is drawn in full wherever it is seen from in front, however far off or obliquely (the owner, 2026-10-30): the pages on the bridge's consoles and walls (schematics, mimics, readouts) were made as ordinary LCD and touch screens, so went flat beyond 8 m and faded off-axis; they are now `lcars`, never flattened or faded, only hidden from behind.
- Museum: the empty shells of Halls 7 and 8, seen from afar, keep their black-box finish (black walls, floor and roof), not the white halls'.
- Museum: halls two or more away from the walker are drawn in their own pass at a fixed quarter resolution (`far-hall-pass.ts`), laid behind the scene, so they never take the resolution of the hall they are seen through; a neighbour copied into the scene goes in the same pass, so what lies ahead no longer sharpens and then softens across a hand-over.
- Museum tiers simplified (the owner, 2026-10-30): a hall next door is always drawn in full detail (no 30 m rule there), its resolution eased by the distance to its doorway; halls two or more away are low poly; the walker's own hall keeps the 30 m rule.
- Museum: every hall two or more away shows its exhibit low poly in place, never an empty white hall; a neighbour's copy no longer fades in from an empty hall; a neighbour's resolution is judged from the doorway into the hall walked in; far placards drawn with a polygon offset, 4 mm proud, so they do not z-fight.
- Signs merged with others of their material (`merge-static.ts`) showed no lettering however close the visitor stood, a blank dark board where Hall 0's wayfinding sign should be (the owner, 2026-10-30): their distance was measured to the merged mesh's origin, the room's, tens of metres off, so they were drawn at their least detail. A sign is now sighted from the middle of what it draws (`sighting`, `display-reach.ts`).
- Museum, Hall 0: its caustics projector is off when the hall is seen from Hall 1 or Hall 9 and fades up only once the walker enters (the owner, 2026-10-30); Hall 0's own room seen through a doorway never ran its projector's update, so it stayed on.

- Museum: a neighbouring hall seen through a doorway now sharpens from a quarter of its resolution 10 m from the doorway to full within 5 m (it was 5 m to 2 m; the owner, 2026-10-30).

- A link that puts the walking eye where no stance can reach (`y=0`, the floor's height, or more than 1.1 m below or 0.7 m above standing, the reach of Alt+wheel) now stands the walker at its own eye height, where it put the eye on the floor (the owner, 2026-10-30; `reachableEyeOffset`, `first-person.ts`).
- Museum level of detail (the owner, 2026-10-30): a neighbouring hall seen through its doorway is drawn with no blur, at a quarter of its resolution from 5 m out and full within 2 m of the doorway, eased between by a smoothstep in 24 steps (`museum-lod.ts`); its exhibit no longer swaps to the far version at 5 m. Each exhibit component more than 30 m from the eye is drawn as its low-poly version, back within 28 m; signs, banners, black boxes and architecture are exempt (`LOD_EXEMPT`) and always drawn as they are. `window.museumLod` shows each hall's resolution share and how many components are low poly.
- Desktop builds (mission 111; the owner, 2026-10-30): the game as an Electron app (`desktop/`, its own package and lockfile), serving the release's web build unchanged on an `app://game/` protocol, fullscreen, with the title music free to start at once and the GPU blocklist off; every release now attaches a Linux AppImage and tarball (for the Steam Deck too), a universal macOS disk image and zip, and a Windows installer and zip to its GitHub release, with checksums and build attestations, all unsigned for now. A Desktop check workflow packages them from any branch by hand. Sentry reports desktop runs as a `desktop` environment.
- Licence: an additional permission under the GPL's section 7 lets the game be linked with game platform libraries (Steamworks, Epic Online Services, GOG Galaxy) without bringing them under the GPL (`LICENSE-EXCEPTION.md`).

- A shared welded-mesh cage (the owner, 2026-10-30; `architectural/logic/mesh-cage.ts`, tested, its view and part beside it): given height, width, depth and its door (side, place, width, hinge, swing), it lays out posts on a regular spacing (at most 1.2 m), top and bottom rails, 50 mm welded mesh of 4 mm wire on all four sides, an optional mesh roof, mesh partitions, base plates or feet, and a hinged mesh door in its own frame with a hasp, staple and padlock; every bar and wire one instance of one box, so a cage is two or three draw calls. Its door works: a use swings it open or shut. The goods yard's CO₂ cage (two partitions, its door in the middle section) and propane cage (its sign now on its door) are redrawn with it and installed as working parts; the CO₂ cage no longer looks wonky and has its sides. Mission 109's pH gameplay with CO₂ is deferred (the owner, 2026-10-30).
- Docs: mission 110, a desktop release for Steam (`doc/prompts/110-desktop-release.md`, the owner, 2026-10-30): Electron, Tauri and NW.js compared for a WebGL game (Electron with steamworks.js recommended), what the game would change (saves to files and Steam Cloud, fullscreen, pointer lock, autoplay, a `steam` Sentry environment, the version), the build, signing and SteamPipe pipeline, Steam's requirements and costs (the US$100 app credit, store assets, review, the coming-soon page, Deck compatibility, content and AI disclosure), and a staged plan with the owner's decisions.
- The CO₂ cage (mission 109, stage 1; the owner, 2026-10-30): in the goods yard, against the wall beside the gas (propane) cage, a locked galvanised mesh cylinder cage holding two banks of four tall 50 l CO₂ cylinders (34 kg; black, the grey shoulder of BS EN 1089-3, Arncliffe Gases' collar and UN 1013 labels), chained upright on pigtails to a two-bank automatic changeover manifold in the cage's middle section; the spares stand apart, chained upright on no pigtail, in a full bay at one end (four, tagged FULL) and an empty bay at the other (two, tagged EMPTY), each closed off by a mesh partition under its sign (the owner, 2026-10-30: nobody should mistake which are connected); only the banks' cylinders count in the model. Its pipework is identified to BS 1710:2014: galvanised tube with yellow ochre bands (gases other than air) on every run, at the manifold's outlet, either side of the isolation valve and at the wall, a CARBON DIOXIDE · CO₂ 4 bar legend with its flow arrow, the banks' copper headers stencilled CO₂ HIGH PRESSURE; the isolation valve's handle yellow, not red. It works: each cylinder holds its contents and stands at CO₂'s vapour pressure until its liquid is gone (`co2-supply.ts`, tested); each bank's gauge reads it; the flags show IN USE, STANDBY or EMPTY and change over by themselves when the service bank is spent; the red lever names the service bank; the regulator's outlet gauge reads 4 bar; the low-pressure line runs along the wall to the CO₂ inlet port, its emergency isolation valve and line gauge before the wall. Nothing draws on it yet. Tried in `?room=dev55` (trial buttons draw 20 kg or refill). A new maker, Arncliffe Gases, the industrial gas supplier (`brands.ts`, its cylinder mark).
- A favicon (the owner, 2026-10-30; `resources/images/icons/meta/`): a pool in section, white tiles over the top quarter and water below, with two layers of caustic web drifting across it in a 3 s loop (still where the browser asks for reduced motion). The SVG animates where the browser animates SVG favicons (Firefox) and shows its first frame elsewhere; a 32 px PNG serves browsers without SVG favicons, and a 180 px PNG is the Apple touch icon. Both pages, the game and the bench, use it.
- Museum showcases: the lighting attic's diffuser and LED strips hang 2 mm under the attic's underside, where the diffuser's face met it and z-fought (the owner, 2026-10-30).
- Chemical delivery couplings modelled properly (the owner, 2026-10-30; `chemical-couplings.ts`): the camlock's adaptor (its cam groove, hex and thread) and coupler (cam arms on pins with pull rings, open or shut, its barbed shank) and dust cap, the Guillemin's symmetrical half (locking ring, hooked lugs, face seal) and blank, and a hose end with its crimped ferrule and chemical's band. Hall 8's case shows the old identical brass camlocks with their labels worn almost away (HYDROCHLORIC ACID, SODIUM HYPOCHLORITE, just legible close to), the old hose's brass coupler with its arms open, and the new hose ends; the stores' fill cabinet takes the new couplings, the hypochlorite's now polypropylene (hypochlorite attacks brass; the case's plaque says so), its Chlorella nameplate moved up clear of the lines' tags; and the chemical store's dev room (`dev_chemicals`) has a bench of them, old and new, whose camlock arms Use opens and shuts.
- Signs' lettering fades in and out over 1.2 s as it comes within reading distance or goes past it, where it appeared and vanished all at once (`SIGN_FADE`, `display-reach.ts`), and the museum's wall panels (titles, introductions, cards), its exhibits' placards and Hall 7's plaques draw all their lettering anywhere in their hall, within 25 m, so it is there to read on arrival (`WALL_PANEL_REACH`; the owner, 2026-10-30).
- Museum, Hall 7 (the owner, 2026-10-30): every plinth now stands round the edge of the hall, in date order from the way in (1 on the north wall beside it, 2 in the north-east corner, 3 and 4 on the east wall, 5 in the south-east corner, 6 and 7 on the south wall, 8 in the south-west corner, 9 on the west wall, the coda in the north-west corner past the way out and the bench), the oil-wheel projector placed from the 1970s' plinth so it always aims at it; the introduction hangs a metre higher in the middle of the east wall; the card saying there is nothing to reset is gone; the bronze timeline is gone from the floor, and the rope barriers from round the plinths; the title wall hangs higher, its middle at 7.3 m; each corner has a plinth facing the middle of the hall; and each plinth has a round spotlight straight down from above it and its lectern, casting a circle on the floor with a soft, fading edge over the specimen, the lectern and the floor round them, brighter over the plinth approached (it replaces the four slanting group spots and the single approach light).
- Museum, Hall 7: each grout plaque's index number stands 5 mm higher (the owner, 2026-10-30), the rest of its text where it was.
- Museum: a hall seen through an archway from the next is no longer blurred (the owner, 2026-10-30); it keeps its far version of the exhibit and its lower resolution (a quarter beyond 10 m of the archway, half within, full and its real exhibit within 5 m).
- Docs: a chemical storage report (`doc/reports/chemical-storage.md`, the owner's guidance, 2026-10-30): where every pool chemical belongs (acids in the acid store, oxidisers and alkalis in the hypochlorite store, CO₂ cylinders in neither), and what the game would have to move or add to meet it; and mission 109, the CO₂ supply (`doc/prompts/109-co2-supply.md`): a cylinder cage in the goods yard, a changeover manifold, a low-pressure line through the wall to the Olympic plant room's injection, CO₂ detection.
- Museum, Halls 1 to 4: the pumps sound (the owner, 2026-10-30): each pool pump holds the 30 kW circulation pump's sound while it runs, running up as it starts and coasting down as it stops, and Hall 1's twin-head circulator the smaller heating circulator's (`heldPumpSound` in `museum-exhibit.ts`, silent in a neighbouring hall's copy). The pump control station's title plate lies flat on its face in a strip of its own (it stood 5 cm proud and, from eye height, hid the RUN and TRIPPED legends), in Halls 1 to 3 and the ideal pool plant.
- Error reporting tells runs apart (the owner, 2026-10-30): every run now reports to Sentry under its own environment, `prod` (itch.io), `pre-release` (GitHub Pages) or `dev` (the dev server, or any other build, such as a local preview), tagged with its build (the commit, marked if uncommitted), and the dev server reports the version it really is (`package.json`'s with the commits since, as CI derives it, in `vite.config.ts`), not the last release's. `?dev` and automated browsers still never report.
- Museum, Hall 1: the pool's big temperature display (M1-TI5) moves to the street wall just below the hall's title (the owner, 2026-10-30), and its unit and tag (°C POOL TI5) now sit in a line of their own under the digits, where before they were printed over the digits' window and off the board's edge. Its digits are a new kind of display, `giant-led` (`display-reach.ts`), drawn in full from up to 30 m, so they can be read anywhere in the hall, where every other display goes flat beyond 8 m.
- Museum, Hall 1: the motorised pool cover (the roller and its drive box at the pool's end), its key-switch station (CV1) and the mimic's COVER OFF and COVER ON lamps are gone (the owner, 2026-10-30), with the cover from the hall's model (its evaporation saving, its motor); the heat-balance and gas placards now have the visitor turn the SWIMMERS dial instead.
- The freight lift's museum landing moves to Hall 0's west wall, the fire exit's (the owner, 2026-10-30), at its south end past the defibrillator and the first-aid kit; the lifebuoy sticker moves to the east wall, south of the doorway from Hall 9, and the east wall is whole again. In the main corridor the wall over the lift's frame is the corridor's own painted blockwork, not a grey header plate.
- The main corridor's wayfinding signs show the way to the service lift (the owner, 2026-10-30): the freight lift's B2 landing is listed as SERVICE LIFT in its place along the north side (between Plant Room 2 and the engineer's office) and, on the east and west sides' signs, round the corner; derived from its landing (`CORRIDOR_LIFT`), as the rooms are from their doors.
- The freight lift's ride (the owner, 2026-10-30): the car now speeds up and slows down (a half-cosine ramp of 1.2 s at either end of its run, `runProfile`), and its vibration is the effects lab's lost precision (every vertex snapped to a fine grid and trembling), as strong as the car's acceleration: hard as it sets off and comes in to a floor, still while it runs at speed or stands, in place of the old 4 mm bob of the car's body. The car and its landings draw the lift between the world's steps (`liftBetweenSteps`), so the doors (already eased) and the shaft move smoothly every frame, not ten times a second. The shaft wall seen through the car's door ran the wrong way (going up, it rose with the car); it now falls past as the car rises. `window.liftCar.press` presses a car button in development.

## [0.7.0] - 2026-10-30

- The freight lift's doors replaced (mission 108; the owner, 2026-10-30): the car's door and every landing's are now centre-opening, four panels, two telescopic panels each side parting from the middle, the fast one travelling twice as far as the slow, both stacking behind the wall (`doorPanels` and `doorTravel` in `lift-doorway.ts`, eased as a variable-speed operator, tested). The opening is 1.7 m clear and the car 2.62 m wide (2.8 m deep, 2.4 m clear; still 2000 kg, 26 persons). The mesh car gate is gone: the car door is solid brushed stainless with kick plates, so the shaft is never seen while riding, and a light curtain of small red LEDs runs down each leading edge, lit while the reopen-on-obstruction logic is held. The heavy clank is now a door operator's whirr while the doors move and a soft thud at each end (synthesised). Each landing carries its floor number on both jambs and the Stannard Lifts mark on the frame's head; the bridge's landing moves to the middle of its east wall, facing the corridor's doors across the room (the owner: the lift takes the whole east wall), and the ready-room reserve on that wall (`READY_ROOM_RESERVE`) is retired; a landing's call button may go on its frame's jamb (`callOn: 'jamb'`).
- The freight lift installed (mission 108, stage 3): its car `freight_lift` joins the B2 landing in the plant corridor's north wall, the floor-3 landing on the bridge's west wall beside the corridor's doors (always the bridge, whatever the CONTROL ROOM MODE knob says; arriving gives the bridge's own entrance, its first view on a first visit) and the floor-4 landing on the museum's Welcome hall's east wall, the museum's first way in that is not a link. G (reception) is a fitted, unlit button that does nothing until reception is built. Hall 0's wall, its tiles, cove and bands and Hall 9's copy of the shared wall are cut for the landing; each landing draws a depth-only pane over its doorway while the car is seen, so nothing behind the wall shows through. Shell rooms take `extras` (a landing) from their runner.
- The trial lift (mission 108, stage 2; the owner's design: the ride masks loading): `?room=dev54` is a Stannard Lifts goods car (chequer-plate floor, steel walls and buffer rails, fluorescent light, a collapsible mesh gate, a floor indicator over the doorway, the car operating panel with lit floor buttons, DOOR OPEN, DOOR CLOSE, ALARM and an independent-service key switch, the rated-load plate and its LOLER certificate), and `dev54_1` to `dev54_4` four dev floors, each a room of its own in its own colours with its number painted big, each with a landing: vertical bi-parting doors with striped safety edges, an indicator with direction arrows and a lit call button. While the car runs the floor ahead is built and warmed unseen (`room-readiness.ts`, the contract the real floors will keep); the car waits short of it until it is ready, so its doors open onto a room already drawn; the walker is handed over through the open doors both ways. Motor hum, judder, brake clunk, chime, door clanks, warning tone, nudging buzzer and alarm bell, synthesised. A new maker, Stannard Lifts (`brands.ts`, its sheave mark). `?liftDelay=8` (development) holds every floor back eight seconds more, to see the car linger; `window.liftTimings` records each floor's build and warm times.
- The freight lift's logic (mission 108, stage 1): its floor list (B2 the plant corridor, G reception not yet built, 3 the bridge, 4 the museum), a goods lift's state machine under collective control (landing and car calls lit until answered, a 3 s dwell after a car call and 5 s after a landing call, the light curtain reopening the doors, nudging shut with a buzzer when held 20 s, a warning tone before closing), and a ride never under 7 s that waits short of a floor until its room reports ready, so the walker never sees it load; in the world as `lift` (`WORLD_VERSION` 60), with `lift/call`, `lift/press`, `lift/hold` and `lift/ready`.
- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer's lectern is gone; the orange floor arrow pointing at the skimmer now reads TRY ME (`floorArrowTexture` takes a label). The lifeguard's chair (its rungs read as a pool ladder) and the lost property box are gone too.

## [0.6.0] - 2026-10-29

- Museum, Hall 5: an orange floor arrow (the museum's own sticker) on the deck points the visitor to the skimmer.

- Museum, Hall 5: using the skimmer with its basket empty plays the same film, to the small-item fanfare, its banner reading THE BASKET IS EMPTY (`revealCueOf`, `EMPTY_BANNER` in `skimmer-reveal.ts`); nothing is counted or handed over.

- Museum, Hall 7: each plinth's era music (`resources/sound/music/grout/`, prompts in `doc/prompts/art/grout-music.md`) plays quietly and looped, from above its plinth, while its lectern is the one approached, changing as the approach light does and silent with none; a loop not yet supplied is simply silent.

- Museum, Hall 5 and dev53: the skimmer film no longer goes black. The rising gold shafts' shader took `pow` of a negative number where multisampling read past a shaft's tip, a NaN the hall's bloom spread into black rectangles over the start of the film and the whip up to the find, hiding the find and its banner; it is clamped. The film can be started from the URL (`?room=museum5&skimmer=reveal`, `?room=dev53&skimmer=reveal`), slowed (`&filmSpeed=0.25`) or stepped a fixed 1/fps per frame drawn (`&filmFps=10`) for capturing it (README).

- Museum, Hall 6: the water falling from the fill pipe is a white, aerated stream, its streaks running down at the speed it falls, never drawn thinner than 7 mm, so it shows at every stream size; the tundish (DR1) reads as an angular cut, a bright stainless kerb round it, a chamfer down to the grille in its rebate and the funnel shading dark into its throat through the more open grille; the mimic's blue chaser LEDs are 24 mm across at a 50 mm pitch (a new `pitch` on a line's flow), unshaded, so they read as running lights from 2–3 m.

- Museum, Hall 5: the green timeline line under each decade's photographs now points on in time, an arrowhead at its right end where the next decade hangs; its dot at the left end had read as an arrow pointing back.

- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer and its lid sit back from the water, in the deck's tiles 7 cm beyond the white coping (the owner, 2026-10-29), its throat running on under the coping to the pool's wall.

- Museum, Hall 5: the painted poolside (its deck tiles, coping and painted water, the skimmer's throat painted into its tile face) is a layer of its own 2 mm above the museum's floor (`DECK_LIFT`), pulled toward the eye so the two never fight from afar; everything standing on it is lifted with it: the skimmer's well and lid, the bin and its sign, the reset point, the lectern, the hall's floor arrows and its safety fittings.

- Museum, Hall 8 (Never again): a small lectern before each of the six photographs, each with its own HEAR WHAT HAPPENED button telling its chapter (again to stop); the lectern the visitor stands at lights its photograph and dims the others, chosen with 0.5 m of hysteresis so it does not flicker (`nearestLectern`). The single button by chapter 1 is gone.

- Museum, Halls 7 and 8 are plain black boxes (the owner, 2026-10-29: "just have two black rooms with no windows ... very simple black rooms"): a per-hall flag in the museum plan (`BLACK_BOX_HALLS`, `isBlackBox`) gives them windowless walls painted matt black, a black floor and roof deck, no columns, services ceiling or gallery track, and no fire safety kit, keeping their archways, archway signs, title (now white lettering) and floor arrows (back to the brand's orange); the walls and archways are unchanged, so the ring still joins up. Hall 7's dark lining and Hall 8's black wall sheets are gone.

## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-29

- Museum, Hall 6: the mimic's water lines carry bright blue chaser LEDs, chasing the way the water goes along the running pump's path only (the reservoir's main up to it, its suction and fill line, the header from it on to the tank) and down the drain while it drains, dark otherwise; mimic lines may now give their chasers a lit colour of their own.

- Museum, Hall 7 (Grout): the oil-wheel projector lights only while the visitor is at the 1970s plinth's lectern, as each plinth's spotlight does (the approach light now actually follows the visitor, by the lectern nearest, and is gentler); the dark lining sits behind the room's sign instead of over it, and the title line stands clear above that sign.

- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer film no longer glitches. The hall's render target now has a stencil buffer, so the skimmer's inside is painted only through its opening (it had been painted over the deck and the water); the ambient occlusion pass skips the skimmer's inside and the caustics (which it had drawn as solid, darkening the shafts); and the film now plays through the walker's own eye (`first-person.ts`'s `film`), so the eye others follow (a hall drawn through an archway) is the shot, not the walker's turn at the shot's place.

- Museum: the signs over the archways cast a tiny drop shadow on the wall, their green printed matte and their orange in glossy vinyl, as the floor's arrows.

- Museum, Hall 8 (Never again): HEAR WHAT HAPPENED now tells the chapter whose photograph you stand nearest, each chapter its own clip (`chapter-1.mp3` to `chapter-6.mp3`), lit alone and silent for 20 seconds while its clip is unrecorded; every voice line the museum needs recorded, with its file name, cue and words, is in `doc/scripts/museum-narration.md`.

- Museum, Hall 6: the test tank is open-topped, the fill pipe running down through it to an open end just over the brim, its water the pools' own rippling surface; what leaves the pipe follows the delivery (drops at a dosing pump, as often as the flow fills a 0.05 ml drop, up to 20 drawn a second; a stream from about 9 l/h, widening as the root of the flow to the pipe's full bore at P11), with splash rings, foam and spray where it lands; DR1 is a tundish, a chamfered stainless funnel let into the floor under a grille.

- Museum, Hall 0: leaving it, the caustics projector fades to black over the 4 seconds after the threshold and then stops being drawn (it held 5 seconds and faded over 5 before).

- Museum, Hall 7 (Grout): coming near a plinth turns a spotlight on over its specimen and its plaque, from the lamp head above it, fading out as you leave (one light serves the hall, moved to the plinth approached); the title wall's line is half a metre higher, clear of the room's sign; prompts for an era-appropriate music loop for each plinth (`doc/prompts/art/grout-music.md`).

- Museum, Hall 6 (World of Pumps): the dosing pumps (P1 to P4) are now heard when running, each its own sound raised to about the circulators' level (P1's solenoid tick had been some 18 dB under them, too quiet to hear); RUN runs one pump at a time, starting one stopping the last; each plaque has its own groan-worthy Grundflow slogan; and the RUN stand's card sits clear of its controls, RUN and PUSH smaller and 5 mm either side of the button.

- Settings: Scaling mode, for a maximum resolution of another shape than the window: Fill (the window's shape, fewer pixels, as before), Centre (the capped size shown as it is, shrunk in proportion only if the window is smaller), Letterbox (fitted, its shape kept, black bars; the default) and Stretch (fitted to the whole window, its shape not kept). On the menu panel it shares a DISPLAY module with the maximum resolution: two rotary switches side by side, each with a backlit LCD naming its choice. The rooms now size their cameras by the canvas's laid-out size.

- Settings: a new player starts at a middling render quality (Medium's) and a maximum resolution of 1024 × 768 (XGA, new on the switch), so a first run is light on any machine; saved settings are kept.

- Settings: Maximum resolution (was Resolution cap) caps the image's width and height, not only its height. On the menu panel it is a rotary switch, MAX RESOLUTION, stepping from 640 × 480 (VGA) through the common PC gaming resolutions (720p, the Steam Deck's 1280 × 800, 1366 × 768, 900p, 1080p, 1920 × 1200, 2560 × 1080 and 3440 × 1440 ultrawide, 1440p, 2560 × 1600, 4K) to Viewport, which draws at the window's own size; a backlit character LCD under it names the choice. An old 720p, 1080p or 1440p cap carries over.

- Settings, Graphics: Overlay scale (0.75× to 2.5×) sizes everything drawn over the view (the help line naming what is pointed at, the HUD buttons, the crosshair, readouts and notices), so it reads clearly in gameplay recorded on a high-resolution screen.

- Title screen: a new logo, a vector drawing after the owner's roundel (`doc/marketing/logos/05-circle-notext.png`), made by `node scripts/title-logo.ts` as `resources/images/title/the-pool-logo.svg`: a dome of flat pool-blue water with white waves over a white tiled band, closed in one navy keyline, and below it on a transparent ground the plant (sand filter with sight glass and stand, strainer, pump and motor, gauges, yellow-handled valves, flanged pipework, plate heat exchanger), run for run as the roundel pipes it. The script also writes `the-pool-logo-animated.svg` (the sight glass swirling, the gauges' needles trembling and the waves rolling and bobbing, by SMIL, looping every 16 s) and `the-pool-logo-titled.svg` (with "The Pool" as SVG text in embedded Antonio Bold, for marketing). Four seconds into the attract sequence the logo appears, unblurring as it fades in over 2 s; two seconds later the title "The Pool" appears the same way, drawn live in Antonio, white with a blue outline and a black drop shadow, its feet dipping into the water with a darker rippling reflection beneath; the logo's water ripples, its sight glass swirls grains over a stirring sand bed and its gauges' needles tremble (redrawn at no more than 10 Hz; timings and motion in `src/toolkit/title/logic/title-logo.ts`). The raster logo and its PNG pipeline are gone.

- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer game now plays dev53's film. Using the skimmer lifts its lid with gold caustics rising from the rim, the camera orbits the skimmer (from no further off than 1.6 m) through the suspense, eases to a 50 mm lens, whips up to the find turning above with its banner, then returns to the walker's eye as the lid goes back on, the find in hand; the skimmer is a hole in the deck with its throat toward the water, a grimy basket full of sludge until the find is out, and shut it is only a white circle (so from neighbouring halls too). The finds are thrown into a grey commercial waste bin (Rubbermate, the game's own maker, of the Rubbermaid Brute kind) instead of the skip; the sign and the how-to say bin. The museum's exhibit hook gains `film`, a shot the exhibit gives each frame (`exhibit-carrying.ts`).

- Attract shots recorded as videos (`node scripts/record-attract.ts [shot…]`): each title fly-by played end to end at `?record=<shot>` (full resolution, no title, fade, blur or HUD) and rendered frame by frame on a stepped clock, so it is smooth however slowly it draws; 3840 × 2160, 30 fps, H.264, no sound, into `doc/marketing/tmp/video/` (not versioned), for the itch.io page and the trailer.

- Skimmer reveal: the big-item cue and the fanfare now take turns, reveal by reveal (more hair keeps its deflated fanfare), in Hall 5 and dev53.

- Dev53: six more finds on trial beside Hall 5's six, at random: a dead frog, a used plaster, a pair of hoop earrings and a bottle cap from free models (credited in `CREDITS.md`, files in `resources/models/skimmer/`), and the project's own swimming goggles and crisp packet. More hair comes out only once the hair has.

- The aiming reticle is slightly larger and has a light ring with a dark outer edge, so it stays visible against pale surfaces; the amber aim and green acceptance rings keep that edge, and the red rejection cross has a dark outline.

- Museum, Hall 5 (and dev53): the hair finds are matte, not glossy, and their matted core is a felt of fine crossing strands.

- Dev53, the skimmer reveal: Hall 5's skimmer reveal tried as a film. The skimmer is a hole in the deck, drawn without cutting one: its opening is masked in the stencil buffer and its inside (walls, floor, basket) painted over the deck there in layers, the lid after (`views/skimmer-well.ts`): a backstop under every layer, the rectangular throat running back under the deck to the pool with its weir hinged at the pool end (as 1980s skimmers', Hayward's SP1070), a ledge with the basket sitting on it, and CERTIKIND on the basket's handle. The film eases to a 50 mm lens (about 27°) over its first second and back as the lid goes on. Using the lid lifts it aside and gold caustics rise at once from the rim, tapering upward; the camera orbits the skimmer, sinking, through the suspense cue; then a sudden whip up to a look diagonally up (0.8 s, overshooting a little and correcting, onto the find held where it comes to rest), the big-item cue, and one of Hall 5's modelled finds picked at random turning above with its banner and caption; then back where the walker stood, looking at the skimmer as the lid goes back on, with the find in hand to carry off or drop (a fresh one each time; one still held goes back in the skimmer). A mini skip stands across the deck to throw finds into. The well's inside is grimy, painted as the capacitor soot is: blots brown at the heart, yellow round it and clear at the edge, heavier the deeper, with a tide line at the water's level and drips below it. The basket below, a little narrower than the shaft, is as grimy, and shown dirty (full of dark brown, fibrous, lumpy sludge, as each film starts) or clean (only its walls' grime, once the find is out). A find is a thing to carry only once handed over, and nothing can be picked up while the film runs.

- Museum, Hall 5: each find now appears to the big-item reveal cue (`reveal-big-item.mp3`, 3.1 s) instead of the short fanfare, and stays above the walker for its whole length; more hair, the last, keeps the deflated fanfare.

- Museum, Hall 5: the suspense cue now uses the complete 7.60-second opening phrase of the owner's newly supplied treasure-chest piano reference, resynthesized with ACE-Step; the reveal and fallback wait for the full phrase. The earlier four-second cut ended mid-phrase, and the earlier excerpts were item-reveal phrases rather than the suspense motif.

- Museum, Hall 5: corrected the suspense cue to the rising suspense passage in the piano-cover resynthesis (4.35–7.40 s of its trimmed render); the previous 1.10-second cut was the short item-get flourish. The reveal again waits 3.05 seconds before the fanfare.

- Museum, Hall 5: the suspense cue is now only the 1.10-second rising piano arpeggio; the reveal and fallback sound follow that shorter phrase before the fanfare.

- Museum, Hall 5: replaced the suspense excerpt with a higher-retention take of the preferred piano-cover reference, keeping its rising piano phrase closer to the source while preserving the 3.05-second reveal timing; the fanfare remains unchanged.

- Museum, Hall 5: the suspense and fanfare now use a resynthesis of mrpianoman's piano cover of the item-get theme, cut to the reveal's 3.05-second suspense and 2.50-second fanfare timing; the prior source-conditioned cues remain recoverable in git.

- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer reveal's suspense cue begins 0.55 seconds earlier in the selected source render, extending its rising arpeggio to 3.05 seconds; the reveal and fallback cue now wait for the full phrase before showing the find.

- Museum, Hall 5: the suspense and triumphant fanfare cues are replaced by 2.5-second excerpts from an ACE-Step resynthesis of the owner's supplied item-get reference; the full render and split recipe are retained for review.

- Museum, Hall 5: four original, precisely timed skimmer reveal music cues replace the synthesized stand-ins: suspense, heroic fanfare, deflated fanfare and skimmer-cleared jingle. The score generator and provenance are kept with the MP3s.

- Museum, Hall 5: three variants of each Pool of the Future scene are saved; the matching `b` set now hangs in the gallery, with captions that follow Reg's opening round, water-model inspection and closing skimmer check.

- Static signs draw each size of their lettering only once it would cover 5 pixels of the screen, seen as it is: its distance, the camera's field of view and zoom, the screen's height and how obliquely the sign is seen (the owner, 2026-10-29; `watchSign`, `signStep`, `smallestReadable`): so a big title shows from across a hall and small print only close to, and a sign too far for any of its lettering shows only its regions of colour; each step drawn once, by running the sign's drawing again without the lettering it leaves out. Covers the museum's signs (`signMesh`) and every printed face made with the fitting tools (`printed`: placards, wall panels).

- Far versions keep static signs as they are, their pictures and all, one draw each (the owner, 2026-10-29: static signs should always be visible); Hall 2's lecterns seen from Hall 3 were black backing plates.

- Museum, Hall 5: the skimmer's finds are revealed as treasure (the owner, 2026-10-29), a parody of a 1990s adventure game's treasure chest: reaching in, the walker is held and looks up while a suspense phrase plays; the find appears above, turning, with a fanfare and a banner ("You got a DEAD RAT!"); then it is in the hands to carry to the skip (`logic/skimmer-reveal.ts`; `ExhibitView` in `exhibit-carrying.ts` lets an exhibit hold the walker and set its look). The skimmer now holds six finds, in order, each modelled and disgusting: a clump of hair, rotting leaves, a dead rat, a dildo, a butt plug and more hair, the last with a deflated fanfare. The music is synthesised stand-ins until recordings are supplied in `resources/sound/music/item-reveal/` (prompts in `doc/prompts/music/item_reveal/`). The basket no longer shows what is on top.

- Museum: photographs in the galleries (Hall 5's decades, Hall 8's chapters) show a low-resolution copy, a sixteenth of their pixels, unless the visitor is within 3 m and looking at one square on, when the full photograph is shown, so they never suddenly draw in (the owner, 2026-10-29; `photo-print.ts`, `logic/photo-detail.ts`; the copies are made by `node scripts/photo-lows.ts` into each gallery's `lo/` folder). Hall 5's bays no longer paint their photographs into their canvas, which had made each a live display drawn as a plain rectangle beyond 8 m.

- Museum, Hall 5: the Pool of the Future's three photographs, generated by Codex from the prompts in `doc/prompts/day-in-the-life/midjourney-prompts.md`.
