- Museum, Hall 5: the Pool of the Future's captions tell the scene in the gallery's voice, a day's work (opening, testing, closing), not the image prompts (the owner, 2026-10-29; a first draft).

- Museum, Hall 3: the DPD test kit stands just west of its own placard (station 6), no longer where lectern 2, The sample, had moved to (the owner, 2026-10-29; `TEST_KIT_X` in `museum-3-layout.ts`, tested clear of every lectern and column).

- Museum, Hall 1: the losses board's meter labels are smaller, on two lines (tag, then name) and no wider than their meters, sat close under each, and SWIMMERS is smaller and beside its dial, so each label plainly belongs to its own instrument (the owner, 2026-10-29).

- Museum: the insides of the archways and doorways are the posters' flat green, not brick (the owner, 2026-10-29); window reveals keep their brick.

- Museum: a hall seen through an archway is drawn at a quarter of its resolution, blurred, with its exhibit as its far version, coming clear as the walker nears the archway: half resolution within 10 m, full resolution and its real exhibit within 5 m, the blur, at most 3 px, gone by 4 m (the owner, 2026-10-29, who then asked for it to come clear sooner and blur half as much; `scaleRatio` in `render-style.ts`). Hall 1 on High: 420 to 244 draw calls per render callback.

- Museum: the copies of the neighbouring halls' exhibits are drawn as far versions (the owner, 2026-10-29; `far-version.ts`): every visible opaque part baked and merged into one mesh for each of a few flat colours, no textures, screens as dark glass, no writing, small parts and see-through ones left out, a handful of draw calls an exhibit.

- Displays are drawn only where they could be read (the owner, 2026-10-29): every live canvas display (one redrawn after it was first drawn, made by `createCanvasTexture`) shows a flat rectangle of its own average colour, and its redraws are not uploaded, unless some camera sees it within 8 m, from in front, near enough its face's normal for its kind, and subtending more than 1°; with margins so it does not flicker at an edge (`display-reach.ts`). Each kind of screen has its own profile (`DISPLAY_PROFILES`, given as `createCanvasTexture`'s `kind`): an LCD fades up from flat between 70° and 65° off its normal, CRTs, plasma, VFDs, LEDs and nixies between 85° and 80°, printed canvases (dials, charts) are only hidden from behind; LCARS touch screens (the bridge's) and the game's own menus are always drawn in full; every other touch screen is of the `touch` kind, LCARS being a touch screen of its own kind. Each kind shows a new picture no more often than it could (the owner, 2026-10-29): an LCD and a nixie 5 times a second, a VFD and LEDs 10, a CRT 25, plasma, touch screens and LCARS 30; a redraw sooner waits its turn. A room may tighten the rules for its own screens (`setRoomDisplayLimits`: a shorter reach, a larger angle subtended, narrower viewing angles). Texture uploads with the world running: Hall 1 from about 560k texels a second to none, Hall 9 from 398k to 14k.

- Museum: ambient occlusion runs only in the hall the player is in, not in a neighbouring hall drawn through an archway (the owner, 2026-10-29); in Hall 1 on High this cuts draw calls by about a third (617 to 420 per render callback) and frame time by about a fifth.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-10-29

- Performance pass (the owner, 2026-10-29; [report](doc/research/2026-10-29-performance-pass.md)): the museum halls' exhibits are in the component benchmark; Hall 1 no longer uploads 2 MB of textures a second with nothing changing (its mimic's readouts take a reading a second, its gas-burn digits and thermometers likewise); every mimic board's readouts are drawn at a board's texel density, a quarter of the texels; Halls 3, 4 and 9 build less each frame. Hall 3's lecterns 2 and 7 stand clear of the columns, and Hall 9's fault pedestal is no longer hidden behind one.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-10-29

- Hall 6's test tank is the game's standard 1,000-litre IBC (the owner, 2026-10-29): its water shows through the bottle to the shared level (redrawn only when it moves a visible millimetre), LI1's litres scale, LSH1's lamp and the TEST TANK plate on its visitors' face; the fill drops into its top filler, the overflow leaves its shoulder, and RESET opens its bottom outlet valve (a DN150 butterfly, its lever turning) to drain in under a minute; the lift falls from 1.7 m to the filler's 1.25 m, the signs' fill times derived from it. The game's IBC is sized to a real one (1,009 litres brimful). The pumps' plaques stand clear of the columns and of their own discharge pipes, with keep-clear zones the router respects and a sight-line test (`sight-lines.ts`); `createTestTank` removed.
- Museum: every hall's title card shows its real title (Halls 5 to 8 no longer "Coming soon"); Hall 6's subtitle is "We Keep Water Moving"; Hall 5's stray leaf-net and hook boxes on the south wall removed, and that wall carries a last bay, "The Pool of the Future?" (photographs `future-1` to `future-3`, placeholders until supplied).
- Hall 4's map above the dosing desk shows a dose happening (the owner, 2026-10-29): from the model's delivery, the dosing tank's lamp and the pot's DOSING lamp light, its line's chaser runs from the tank along the header and injection into the pool, and DOSED SO FAR counts up, all going still when the dose ends. Mimic chasers may set a larger LED size (`flow.size`) for boards read from a distance; Hall 4's use it.
- Halls 3 and 9: the day tanks' lids no longer z-fight (a screw cap sunk into the drum, the liquid never level with its top); their labels are printed round the front in two lines with the CLP corrosive pictogram; each has a bulkhead outlet and fill lance reaching its ports, so the suction hose meets the tank. Experimental sources letter their substance and role round their front in two large lines (the owner's bugs, 2026-10-29). Hall 3's STAFF ONLY door is now a glossy vinyl sticker on the wall.
- Hall 5's picture lights hang above each decade's bay, no longer across the top of its print (the owner's bug, 2026-10-29).
- A sand filter says it is rinsing only while its flow goes to waste (a rinse), not when it is put straight back into service after a backwash with its bed still unsettled (the owner's bug, 2026-10-28, at the Wash-O-Tron).
- Hall 5's gallery shows the owner's thirty photographs of Reg the poolman, decade by decade (`resources/images/day-in-the-life/`), painted into each bay as they load; a missing one keeps its placeholder.
- Museum: the empty halls across the courtyard no longer show through a neighbour drawn through its archway (seen from Hall 0 through Hall 9): an empty hall standing aside as a depth-only occluder is now drawn before everything else.
- Hall 0 faster and its pool lanes projected: the caustics are drawn into a small texture at most ten times a second instead of by a heavy shader over the whole pool every frame, and the projector's spill light is gone; the lane lines (with their T ends) are part of the projected picture on a plain white pool; only the title wall has cove light, now a gentle narrow halo; the projector fades out 5 s after the walker leaves Hall 0 (over 5 s, then standby) and fades back up on return.
- Hall 0 relit: the painted pool is now white, lit by moving water caustics from a ceiling-hung Lumenhaus laser projector (10 Hz frames, status LED); the room is darkened, and its title wall and archway signs stand 50 mm off the walls with warm cove light behind them.

- Museum Hall 9, The whole plant (107e, the finale): Halls 1 to 3's models joined into one working plant (`museum-9-plant.ts`, tags M9-…, the plumber adding nothing) with a basket strainer and a P1 starter; ten faults each fixed at its machine (`museum-9-faults.ts`), the random-fault pedestal with the attendant's key, the scoreboard, the station clock; the water panel and a 24-window annunciator read only from instruments; mimic, layout (24 × 14 m, every line routed), placards and the closing wall with the owner's message. `createAnnunciatorBoard` shows the location annunciator on any state; the ideal sources' port labels are printed on the cylinder.

- Museum carrying (107f): the museum halls now use the game's own pick-up, carry and drop. An exhibit declares portable objects (`userData.portable`) and drop targets (`userData.placementTarget`) as any room does, and gets the hands, `divert` and target feedback through `exhibit-carrying.ts`. The control scheme gains `give` (an object handed straight into the hands, from a container) and `forget`, and a bin may refuse things (`TossBin.takes`). Hall 5's skimmer uses them: lift the lid and set it down or back on its seat, take each find from the skimmer, carry it, and drop it or toss it into the skip, where it counts as it lands. The hall's own click-carrying is gone.

- Museum Hall 3, Safe water (107c): a closed dosing loop on the ideal pool: sample cell, Chlorella DC-1 pair with its tuning menus (XP, tn) open to visitors, Grundflow DDS 7.5-16 on hypochlorite and DMX 1602 on acid, 60 l day tanks filled from ideal stores, SWIMMERS dial, DPD test kit, fault pedestal (AIR IN THE PUMP, WEAK DELIVERY) and a 24-hour chart recorder telling the hall's time. Model `museum-3-dosing.ts` (tags M3-…, the plumber adding nothing), layout, mimic and words pure and tested; new views in `museum-dosing-parts.ts`; `createDiaphragmPump` may bind its keys to a model.

- Museum Hall 6, Grundflow's Amazing World of Pumps (107j): a 1,000-litre test tank fed through an overhead header by eleven pumps, from a 2 l/h solenoid dosing pump to a new 45 kW split-case showpiece, each drawing from an ideal reservoir trough, with its RUN stand, plaque (its fill time from the model, "At this rate it would take 20 days to fill the tank") and flow readout; one shared level, every pump locked out by LSH1 at full with the piezo `error` bleep, RESET draining the tank; centrifugal pumps held at duty by commissioning valves and solved together on the shared header; Penfold mimic, Grundflow clock. Model `museum-6-pumps.ts` (tags M6-…, the plumber adding nothing), layout, mimic and words pure and tested; new views in `museum-pump-parts.ts`.

- Museum Hall 5, A Day in the Life (107l, with 107f's What's in the Skimmer?): ten decades of a poolman's day as placeholder photographs (morning, midday, night), earliest on the visitor's left round the north and east walls; a dry painted poolside with a Certikind skimmer whose lid lifts aside, 27 finds (adult ones plain and deadpan) carried one at a time to a skip, caption card, optional voice clips, cut-away, clock and RESET. Game and gallery logic pure and tested.

- Museum Hall 2, Clean water (107b, stage 2: in the room): the exhibit built in `museum2` and registered, its plant laid out in an L round the corner hall (the owner, 2026-10-28): up the west wall from the way in from Hall 1 (mains, balance tank, pump, sand filter, the ozone bay), then along the north wall to the way on to Hall 3 (contact tank, carbon, UV, pool), re-routed with no clashes and the plumber adding nothing; every machine, valve, pipe and instrument bound to the model (M2-… tags), the mimic, clock, placards, filter-settings card and RESET. `createOzoneGenerator` takes a model, a rating and bound MAINS and OUTPUT SET; `createUvUnit` shows a given intensity and lamp hours.

- Museum Hall 2, Clean water (107b, stage 1: model and layout): a new, smaller plant of its own (dev51 unchanged), no heating, the pool a constant 27 °C. Schematic `museum-2-clean-water.ts` (tags M2-…, the plumber adding nothing): mains make-up and balance tank with a low-level cut-out, the pump with the museum's shorter dead-head trip (a casing parameter now in `pump-deadhead.ts`; dev51 keeps its own), the sand filter on its five-valve nest and backwash, ozone (Trioxa generator on ideal dry air, side-stream booster and venturi, 7 m³ contact tank with off-gas destructor, carbon deozoniser, ozone-in-air monitor) and a medium-pressure UV unit, then the flowmeter and the pool; normal duty 183.5 m³/h, 2.6 h turnover, PI2 2.02 bar, PDI1 0.42 bar. Layout and plumbing left to right along the west wall (`museum-2-layout.ts`, every line routed, no clashes; the row needs a hall 20.5 m long), mimic and words (`museum-2-mimic.ts`, `museum-2-placards.ts`, the filter's settings card), all pure and tested; research note `doc/research/107b-ozone-uv-products.md`. Not yet wired into `museum2`.

- Museum Hall 1, Warm water (107a), and the exhibit groundwork (107-0): every hall can now hold a working exhibit (loaded lazily, worked through the control scheme, inspected by the overlay, its solids walked round), reset with its clock at the visitor's local time on every entry, one shared hall clock (`museum-time.ts`, ×1 to ×3600 in half-second slices), room codes M1, M2 …. Hall 1 is the first exhibit: an ideal gas main, a real Hamwarmy HW 160 condensing boiler with its working panel and a CONTROL selector (ON-OFF · MODULATING), its twin-wall flue to the roof, a sealed primary circuit with twin-head pump, pressurisation unit, motorised valve, differential bypass and heat meter, a plate exchanger, the pool pump with its station, butterflies, flowmeter and flow switch, the ideal 470 m³ pool with a motorised Certikind cover, a capillary thermostat (20 to 35 °C), the losses board with a SWIMMERS dial, a Splashonomics display of the gas burnt now (kW and m³/h, no price; the owner's answer), a Penfold mimic, the pool clock, seven placards and RESET. Schematic, model, layout (voxel router), mimic and words pure and tested.

- Museum (107-1, the owner 2026-10-28): from any hall both ring neighbours are drawn in full, even when both archways are in view (each a full copy in the hall's scene, its own room drawn through the archway looked at); every hall further away is its empty model, never missing. A neighbour coming into range fades in from the archway's line to 1 m into the new hall, and out again walking back, over its empty hall. Hall 0 is a tiled pool hall: coloured tile bands, a tiled cove at every wall foot and corner, a glossy lifebuoy sticker on its west wall; its empty model carries the same tiles, bands, cove, sticker and the painted dry pool floor.

### Changed

- Hall 7, Grout, laid out anew (the owner, 2026-10-28: "grout plinths around the edge, with a few in the centre. The oil wheel light should be high in the ceiling, focusing down on the 1970s exhibit"): plinths 1 to 3 on the east wall, 4 to 6 an island in the middle with the 1970s' avocado at its centre, 7 to 9 under the title on the south wall, the coda on the west wall by the way out, each roped round with its plaque before it; the timeline across the way in; the oil-wheel projector hung on a rod over the island, aimed down at the avocado; the hall's floor arrows black vinyl in their white border.

- The museum's ring is no longer a uniform grid (the owner, 2026-10-28: "make one room on each side 50% bigger, we will need the whole plant room to be bigger"): column 1 (Hall 3 and Hall 9, the finale) is 24 m wide and the middle row (Hall 1 and Hall 6) 21 m deep, the building now 72 × 49 m; Hall 0 and the corner halls keep 16 × 14 m (`MUSEUM_SQUARE.colW`, `rowD`). Hall 1's exhibit stays centred along its west wall.
- The museum's courtyard windows show only a plain white sky, opaque, so no other hall is ever seen through them (the owner, 2026-10-28: "you never see the other rooms, just white skybox"); the sky box and its stencil pass are gone.
- The museum rebuilt as the owner's ring (mission 107-1): 10 halls, `museum0` to `museum9`, four rooms east–west by three north–south round a courtyard two rooms wide and one deep (16 × 14 m rooms, provisional; the owner, 2026-10-28: "the 4x3 room layout"), Hall 0 in the south-west corner with arrows on its floor pointing north into Hall 1, the route round to Hall 9 and back into Hall 0; Halls 1 to 4 and 9 signed with their themes (Warm water, Clean water, Safe water, The chemistry of pools, The whole plant), Halls 5 to 8 "coming soon"; the safety layer recomputed for the ring (exits to Hall 0 the shorter way, fire points in the odd-numbered halls, the lift's place moved along Hall 0's east wall beside the new doorway from Hall 9). Every hall is now the same height, Hall 0 included, and the courtyard windows are high clerestories that show only the sky (the owner, 2026-10-28): the courtyard's paving, trees, pond and far walls are no longer drawn.

### Added

- Thirty original period-style photographs for Hall 5's “A Day in the Life” gallery, three scenes in each of ten decades from the 1920s to the 2020s, generated into `doc/gens/day-in-the-life/` for later selection and installation.
- Error reporting with Sentry (the owner, 2026-10-28): the published builds on GitHub Pages and itch.io send uncaught errors, unhandled rejections and the errors the game shows to the project's Sentry, tagged with the version, room and graphics preset; no tracing, profiling, cookies or personal data, and nothing from the dev server or `?dev`.

- Dev52, Museum stuff (the owner, 2026-10-28: "a devroom called museum stuff, 90% black walls, minimal lighting"): a dark room (10% grey walls, dark floor and ceiling, a low ambient, a gallery spot over each case) for the museum's fittings. In it a reusable museum showcase (`src/toolkit/gallery/views/museum-showcase.ts`, sized by `logic/museum-showcase.ts`): a plinth with a recessed black kick plate and a shadow gap under its fabric deck, a low-iron glass hood with faint reflections and just-visible bonded edges in a thin bronze-black channel, a lighting attic with warm LED strips and one spot (no shadows), and a small object label on an acrylic wedge, the deck set by an eye-line rule. Hall 8's case of the two fill points now uses it (`views/fill-points-case.ts`), shown in both places, with its plaques on a bracketed rail clear of the plinth and its hose tags no longer overlapping.
- Hall 8, Never again, has black walls (the owner, 2026-10-28: "Make hall 7, 'Never again' black walls"): matt black from floor to roof deck, round its archways and windows, with a white panel left behind the hall's title vinyl so it stays readable; it replaces the charcoal lining to 2.55 m.

- An oil-wheel projector on Hall 7's 1970s grout (the owner, 2026-10-28): a disco-era effects projector, the Psycholux 250 (`src/toolkit/gallery/views/oil-wheel-projector.ts`, its wheel and oils in `logic/oil-wheel.ts`), its glass wheel of coloured oils turning across the lens and its swimming colours thrown on the avocado specimen; nothing redrawn after it is built, only turned.

- Thirty-three OpenAI-generated reference photographs for the fictional 1997 chlorine-release museum sequence: three variants of each of eleven scenes in `doc/gens/hindenburg/` (the owner, 2026-10-28).

- The empty hall as a component (`src/toolkit/gallery/views/museum-empty-hall.ts`; the owner, 2026-10-28): generic, side and corner, the architecture only, merged by material; each hall's scene draws every hall beyond its neighbours empty at its true place, replacing the plain stand-ins, with the ring's adjacency and full/empty sets pure and tested in `museum-plan.ts`.

### Fixed

- The museum: a neighbouring hall's machines fade in with the rest of the hall as you cross the threshold, instead of appearing all at once. The fade gathered the hall's materials when it was built, before its exhibit had loaded; each neighbour's exhibit now joins its hall's fade when it loads, on its own copies of its materials (a pipe kind's are shared, and the hall you are in must not fade with it).

- Pipe tees reach their ports: a tee's sleeves were sized from the pipe's diameter alone, so where the plumber ends each pipe 0.15 m from the tee's centre a gap showed between fitting and pipe (Museum Hall 1's bypass tees, most visibly; the ideal pool plant's tees too). `createPipeTee` takes how far its ports stand out, and Hall 1 and the ideal plant pass it.

- The Release workflow fetches its tag again after checkout, which had replaced the annotated tag with a lightweight one and failed v0.2.0's "not annotated" check; it can now be run by hand for an existing tag (`gh workflow run release.yml -f tag=vX.Y.Z`) to publish a failed release again with the same checks, keeping a GitHub release that already exists. Release notes longer than GitHub's 125,000-character limit (v0.2.0's were) are cut at a line and link to the full `CHANGELOG.md` at the tag.

## [0.2.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added

- Versions bumped only by a release (mission 104; the owner, 2026-10-28; reviewed by Codex, `doc/reviews/2026-10-28-codex-review-104.md`): a push to `main` is the next patch, derived by the build from the commits since `package.json`'s version last changed (`scripts/versioning.ts`), so a build writes no file and CI makes no `[skip ci]` version commit; `npm run release` (the next minor, a major only when asked) checks, bundles, then writes the version and changelog, commits `Release vX.Y.Z`, tags and pushes atomically, undoing itself on any failure; GitHub Pages follows every push to `main`, and only a release reaches itch.io, from the Release workflow, which now checks the tag is annotated, on `main` and matches `package.json` and the lockfile. CI fails a commit that changes the version outside a release (`scripts/version-guard.ts`). The `release:patch|minor|major` scripts are gone.
- Plans for the museum's exhibit halls in the owner's new order (mission 107; the owner, 2026-10-28: "a prompt with a sub-index letter … for each of the halls. Each needs to build on the understanding of the previous one"): sub-prompts 107-0 (groundwork: the halls re-signed, an exhibit hook, reset on entry, one hall clock), 107a (Hall 1, Warm water: a real gas boiler and exchanger heating an ideal pool), 107b (Hall 2, Clean water: dev51 moved in), 107c (Hall 3, Safe water: automatic dosing), 107d (Hall 4, The chemistry of pools: a hands-on lab), 107e (Hall 13, The whole plant, the finale, its closing wall echoing Hall 0's welcome), 107f (What's in the Skimmer?, a skimmer-emptying game for children), 107g (Gutters You Can Trust, a century of pool edges in cut-away), 107h (Controls from the 1920s to the Present Day, and Beyond, seven booths running one small pool), 107i (Never again: putting safety first, a fictional chlorine release told in our own press photographs, sponsored by Chlorella), 107j (Grundflow's Amazing World of Pumps, a tank-filling demonstration from a 2.1 l/h dosing pump to an Olympic-sized pump, each sign giving its fill time) and 107k (Grout: The Unsung Hero of the Aquatics World, ten identical specimens and one in avocado), the last six not yet placed in halls; the owner's rules for every hall (reset on entry, time from now, halls empty until built) and new layout (a ring of 14 halls round a courtyard, Hall 0 in the south-west corner, Halls 5 to 12 for themes to come) recorded in the prompt, with its stage plan.
- The museum's safety and services layer (mission 107; the owner, 2026-10-28: "look up what kind of safety gear you'd expect a museum to have"), after current UK practice (the Fire Safety Order, Approved Documents B and M, BS 5839-1, BS 5266-1, BS 5499-4 / ISO 7010, BS 5306-8, BS 1363): an escape route signed from every room back to Hall 0 the shorter way round the ring, and Hall 0's push-bar fire exit door to the stair (shut, set decoration) with its final exit, keep clear, keep shut and Ground floor ↓ signs; a call point by every way out, sounder-beacons, emergency bulkheads, maintained exit signs and hanging escape signs; fire points on boards in Museums 1, 3 and 5; white twin sockets at 450 mm and floor boxes; CCTV domes, PIRs and the CCTV notice; the defibrillator cabinet and first aid in Hall 0; the hearing loop, occupancy, wayfinding and please-do-not-touch notices; a staff-only door. Built of the game's own fittings, merged by material. Hall 0's east wall is kept clear for a future goods lift.
- PLANTROOMS, the pool science museum's shell (mission 107, stages 1 and 2; the owner, 2026-10-28: "a series of exhibits in a science museum … about how swimming pools work"): Hall 0, the welcome (`?room=museum0`), a dry, tiled pool hall whose water is paint on the floor and whose chrome ladder stops at it, then five empty exhibit halls, `museum1` to `museum5`, in a converted warehouse round a courtyard, each its own room and scene, joined by archways knocked through old brick walls and walked through without a break, round every corner too (the pool loop's hand-over; every wall built in the museum's coordinates, so both rooms build it alike, and the floor's joints laid from one origin), the route closing its loop from Museum 5 back into Museum 1. White-painted brick, polished concrete with saw cuts, cast-iron columns under steel beams, the services ceiling, clear windows onto the courtyard (paving, trees, a pond and fountain, the sky, drawn only through the windows) and obscured ones onto the street, gallery track lights and benches. The brand the owner chose, PLANTROOMS, "The real backrooms of the Poolrooms" (a new maker in `brands.ts` with its `tiles` mark), on Hall 0's title wall, banners and every archway's header; a "coming soon" panel in each hall. The plan for every hall and the owner's questions in `doc/prompts/107-pool-science-museum.md`.
- The ideal filter's sand loss, an option off by default (the owner, 2026-10-28): with a filter's `sandLoss` on, a backwash rising faster than 40 m/h washes sand away in proportion to the excess, leaving a shallower bed (`bedSizing`) that holds less dirt and lets more past, until new media; off, nothing changes. Nothing in the game turns it on.
- Dev51's second round (mission 106; the owner, 2026-10-28): a dead-head trip (`pump-deadhead.ts`: the casing's water heating at shut-off, a latched thermostat trip, TRIPPED and RESET on the pump's local station and the mimic); a motorised heating interlock valve (IPP-HX1-V6) shutting the hot water when the pump stops or the exchanger's pool-side flow falls; TIME ×3600; a sweep-second pool clock on the plant's time, from the visitor's local time, with a PLANT TIME display (`pool-clock.ts`); the filter's five-valve backwash nest, waste tee, ideal drain and sight glass SG1 on the ideal filter's physics, the bed fouling with bathers' dirt, a surge on PI2 when a valve is worked under flow; an ultrasonic heat meter (IPP-HM1) and a "heat used since the demo started" sign in kWh and kettles. The plant's solver now also leaves out a loop reaching a single reservoir.
- Dev51, the ideal pool plant (mission 106; the owner, 2026-10-28: "a demo … to show that we can simulate three-dimensional schematics with ideal sources"): a working three-dimensional schematic of a pool's circulation, filtration and heating, laid out as a museum's working model at `?room=dev51`. The schematic (`ideal-pool-plant.ts`) gives every valve, meter and instrument, the plumber adds nothing, and every tag is derived (IPP-P1-V1, IPP-FT1, IPP-TI3). An ideal pool and balance tank, one real Grundflow end-suction pump on its curve, a real Ø2.8 m sand filter, a plate heat exchanger on a throttling bypass and an ideal hot-water source; the water network solved by the plant's own solver (with dead ends pruned, so a shut valve leaves no unconverged pocket), the heat by ε–NTU with the pool's thermal mass. New components: a throttling butterfly valve with a notch plate (`throttle-valve.ts`), a paddle-wheel meter, a metal-tube variable-area meter and a glass rotameter (`small-flowmeters.ts`), and an ideal vessel (`ideal-vessel.ts`). A Penfold mimic repeats every instrument, lights each valve green, amber or red by its lever, and runs time at ×1, ×60 or ×600. Museum lecterns at six numbered stations with derived names and facts, an entrance panel, an activity card and a big RESET SIMULATION button; the equipment room's inspection overlay is switched on for it.
- The pools' dosing controllers, designed and working in dev44 (mission 105; the owner, 2026-10-28: "Let's design those two machines"): one pure controller model (`dosing-controller.ts`) generalised from mission 101's hidden controller (with the world's settings its outputs are `stepControl`'s step for step), taking a sample cell's signals and giving each channel's output and release, AUTO or MANUAL, setpoints and limit alarms, the interlocks (sample flow, circulation, the pH lockout, the maximum dosing time, a feed delay), an alarm list, a trend, relays for the control room, and calibration (chlorine zero and DPD slope, pH two buffers or phenol red, each refused outside the D1C's and W&T's limits). Two faces, both Chlorella's (the owner: specialist supplier equipment, an exception to Penfold's panels): the Olympic's CX-7, after ProMinent's DULCOMARIN 3 and W&T's DEPOLOX Pool, a 7" touch screen drawn as a 2010s plant panel (home, chlorine, pH, trends, alarms, guided calibration, status); and the Lounge's DC-1 pair, after the DULCOMETER D1C, each a four-digit LED readout, nine LEDs and six membrane keys. Each on a sample cell (`sample-cell.ts`, after ProMinent's DGMa: isolating valve, flow regulator and rotameter, reed flow switch, probe holders, sample tap, the pH electrode lifted into buffers), driving a DMX 1602 and a DDS 7.5-16 that dose its own demonstration pool in the chemistry model, its pump signals in conduit and its relays in trunking to a marshalling box TO CONTROL ROOM, a photometer for the DPD and phenol red tests, and a trial station of faults (soda ash and acid slugs, drifted probes, the plant's pace; the hypochlorite pump's suction valve shut so it delivers nothing). The seven-segment readouts gain `i`, `n`, `u`, `G` and `Y`. Not installed: `doc/prompts/105-dosing-controllers.md`.
- The pause menu's System Panel SP-1 (the owner, 2026-10-28): fitted to the window, its width chosen so the whole panel is drawn as large as the screen allows with nothing to scroll; its name and rating plates and their bands gone for room, the name now a screwed-on engraved plate, SP-1 · IN GAME CONTROLLER / PENFOLD CONTROL SYSTEMS, on its largest blanking plate with the build stamped below it and the DEVELOPMENT BUILD strip; a click on a plate's bare face eases the view in to it (80 % of filling the screen, so its neighbours show), Esc or B eases back out, and a second closes the menu; the STATUS display a dot-matrix VFD, saying SYSTEM READY and VER. <version> in turn, 3 s each. Its modules' title plates end clear of their corner screws, and the render quality knob's WORST and BEST are smaller, inside its dial.
- PL3-F1, the jacuzzi's sand filter, now says what it is doing on mouse-over (filtering, standing, backwashing or rinsing, and how clean it seems), from its cabinet's filter programme; it had no status. PL1's dosing boards (DP1 hypochlorite, DP2 acid) are 0.2 m further apart each on the south wall, leaving room for a dosing controller between them; the hypochlorite board's FROM DAY TANK tag no longer overlaps its yellow plate.
- Research and a plan for mission 104, bumping the version only when the owner asks for a release (the owner, 2026-10-28): today every local build and every CI run advance the patch (0.1.0 to 0.1.710 in six days, 250 `[skip ci]` commits, bumps in half of all commits and conflicts between sessions); proposed a plain `npm run build` that never writes, one `npm run release` that bumps, moves Unreleased, commits and tags only after the bundle succeeds, and build labels naming the release and commit between releases; compared `npm version`, Changesets, release-please and semantic-release.
- Research and a plan for mission 103, clearing the walker to a reachable safe position before a door swings through them; compared The Dark Mod's blocked-door rule and the Half-Life and Source door implementations.
- A library of piezo beeps (mission 100; the owner, 2026-10-28: "can we give button pushes a credible piezo bleep"): 117 MP3s (1.8 MB) in `resources/sound/effects/piezo/`, every one synthesised from scratch by `generate.py` beside them as one cheap 27 mm piezo sounder behind a panel (a band-limited pulse drive, three resonances, a soft clip, the fascia's comb, rattle and ripple, then a synthesised room by convolution), by pitch (low 1.3 kHz buzzy, mid 2.4 kHz, high 3.9 kHz piercing), meaning (entry, confirm, done, error, deny, ready, fault, priority-high, priority-medium, and the seamless loops countdown, hurry, warning and alarm) and reverb (dry, small, large). Every dry beep is equally loud while sounding (K-weighted, to 0.5 dB), each room's version keeps its gain, the loudest file peaks at −1 dBFS; the generator checks each file's onset, peak and loop join after decoding and writes `index.md`, `index.html` (players) and `manifest.json`, and reruns byte-identically. In code, `piezoBeep(meaning, { pitch, room, level })` and `piezoLoop(...)` (`sound/audio/piezo-recordings.ts`, typed so a loop cannot be played once or the reverse), `playPiezo` and `piezoSounder(voice, from)` for a device's own sounder (`sounder.key()` on a key press), the axes and levels pure in `sound/logic/piezo-beeps.ts`. A piezo test set in dev7, Machine Tones: a PZ-27 sounder on a Penfold bench controller with PITCH, MEANING and ROOM selectors, a readout and a TEST button, so the whole library can be heard in the game (`?room=dev7&view=piezo`). Nothing switched to it yet: the candidates are the owner's to choose.
- The dosing pumps are real in the world (mission 102; the owner, 2026-10-28: "link it to our ideal pump model"): `world.dosingPumps` (WORLD_VERSION 59) holds each pool's pump as a metering pump of the family, the lounge's and the Olympic's hypochlorite DMX 1602s and their acid DDS 7.5-16s stepped stroke by stroke, the spas' heads still ideal; what each delivers is the day tank's draw and the water's dose, so a gas-locked pump stops dosing and its pool's chlorine sags. A `dosing-pump` action acknowledges a fault, opens or shuts a bleed, or lets gas into a head; the boards show their pump's fault (ERROR, `ERR`). The Olympic's DMX now gives about 2.09 l/h flat out rather than exactly 2.1.
- The water-chemistry model is in the world (mission 101, stage 1; the owner, 2026-10-28; `doc/prompts/101-water-chemistry-in-the-world.md`): each pool's balance tank and its pool's three cells carry the model's state (`src/game/plant/water-treatment.ts`, `world.treatment`), stepped every 10 s of world time with the hydraulics' flows, bathers driving the demand and make-up at the mains' composition; each pool's pH, free and combined chlorine and redox now come from it, so every display, annunciator and the bridge read the real water. A controller per pool (PI on chlorine, P on pH, the pH lockout) sets each dosing pump's real rate, capped at the drawn pump's (`pumpRates`, where the pump family of mission 102 will plug in), only while the water circulates and its day tank has stock and an open path; the day tanks are drawn by the litres pumped, replacing the fixed daily draw, and the dosing boards, DoseCell heads and the spa cabinets' peristaltic heads show the real rate. `WORLD_VERSION` 58: a save keeps its pools' readings. The Olympic keeps its undersized 2.1 l/h hypochlorite pump (the owner): pinned at full from midday, its chlorine falls below 1.0 mg/l by mid-afternoon and CHLORINE LOW stays lit through the evening (the before picture, `doc/prompts/101-olympic-before.svg`).
- The Olympic's replacement hypochlorite pump, designed and working in dev44 (mission 102; the owner, 2026-10-28: "Design a new pump"): Grundflow's DMH 21-10 (Grundflow's, at the owner's word, not a new maker), a motor-driven diaphragm metering pump after the ProMinent Sigma/ 1 S1Cb 12017 (21 l/h at 10 bar, 90 strokes of 3.9 ml, its stroke dial at 60 % for 12.6 l/h, the Olympic's sizing), its logic pure and tested (`motor-dosing-pump.ts`): rate from its stroke dial and its stroke rate (MAN, or 4–20 mA in EXT); gas in its head that each stroke must compress before it delivers, so a slug of air or a night standing gas-locks it (it strokes and shows its rate, delivering nothing) until its bleed valve is opened, or a degassing head vents it; an integral relief valve; a flow monitor that stops it after 20 short strokes (`Er F`, reset at the pump); EMPTY (`Er L`), LOW (WARN) and a burst diaphragm (`Er d`, its indicator red). A 1990s fascia: four-digit red LED readout, unit, mode and status lamps, STOP/START, P, i, ▲, ▼; a motor hum, and each stroke heard (a knock delivering, a hollow tick gas-locked, a gurgle bleeding). On a trial station beside the DMX 1602 it is to replace, both asked for the same dose, with the DMH's pulsation damper and gauge, its faults and the litres each delivered. With it, the plant's metering pumps as one family on the ideal pump (`metering-pump.ts`, the owner's request): one interface for the ideal pump, the PERI 30 (tube wear, leak detection, never gas-locking) and the diaphragm pumps on one stroke-by-stroke head model (`diaphragm-head.ts`): the DMX 1602, the DDS 7.5-16 (deaerating itself standing by) and the DMH. Not installed: linking the family to the world waits for mission 101, and the DMH replaces the Olympic's pump one for one (no duty pair, the owner) when the owner asks.
- The water-chemistry bench's pools have a mixing lag (the owner, 2026-10-28: a dose into the balance tank should take about a turnover to show fully in the pool, not ten minutes; `doc/research/water-chemistry-models.md` §5.1): each pool is three well-mixed cells in series round a ring with its balance tank (`src/game/water/circuit.ts`), exchanged exactly as a matrix exponential, so a hand dose reaches the 25 m pool's far side 63 % in 69 minutes and 95 % in 113, against 10 and 24 before. The bench's chlorine loop is retuned to the dead time that brings (band 0.5 mg/l, integral 6 h), the overdose scenario's fault lasts the 12 h it says, its treatment uses soda ash for pH and bicarbonate for alkalinity, and hand doses are judged a turnover after they go in; all ten scenarios pass with no model constant changed.
- A water-chemistry model, tested in the abstract before it goes into the world (the owner, 2026-10-28; `doc/research/water-chemistry-models.md`): `src/game/water/pool-chemistry.ts`, the full set of species a zone (free chlorine, inorganic and organic chloramines, trichloramine, ammonia, urea, organic demand, alkalinity and total carbonate with pH solved from them, hardness, TDS, sulphate, chlorate, cyanuric acid, thiosulphate), each process exact over the step; built on the Jafvert & Valentine scheme (ported from the US EPA's MIT simulator to make fixtures), the carbonate system checked against PHREEQC 3.8.6, and the Bristol, Schmalz, Wojtowicz and Berg pool studies. A headless bench (`chemistry-bench.ts`) runs ten problem-and-treatment scenarios on two-zone circuits (pool and balance tank), one zone against two, and the game's four pools, with an HTML report of charts it generates into `build/reports/water-chemistry-bench.html`. Not installed in the world.
- Researched dosing, its control and the pool water's chemistry for the next mission (the owner, 2026-10-28; `doc/research/pool-dosing-and-water-chemistry.md`): the chemistry in moles and equivalents (chlorine species, breakpoint, carbonate and pH, the Langelier index, TDS and dilution, hypochlorite's second-order decay and chlorate), how UK plants measure and control (amperometric and pH probes, calibration and drift, PI controllers, the flow, circulation, tank, dosing-time and pH interlocks), what over- and under-strength deliveries do and what a hydrometer can and cannot see, the chemicals added by hand and the routine hand tests, what the game has now, a proposed model and twelve questions for the owner.
- Dosing told truthfully from the model (the owner, 2026-10-28): a DoseCell head's pump page says DOSING with its rate in ml/h, OFF, EMPTY, or FAULT (asked but nothing drawn, its way shut), from what its pool's dosing asks and gives, not from its level, which at the footbath's millilitres an hour never seemed to move; a spa cabinet's dosing pumps turn each only while its chemical reaches it, its readouts show its water's chlorine and pH, and its display adds HYPO / ACID FEED FAULT (a day tank empty, its outlet or dosing isolating valve shut, or the chemical service off) and LOW HYPOCHLORITE / LOW ACID (down to the tank's dry-run cut-out). A day tank's outlet isolating valve, and a cabinet's dosing end's, now stop its chemical being drawn (`pool-dosing.ts`).
- An annunciator for each major location (mission 099; the owner, 2026-10-28: "at least 3 annunciators. One for the Olympic pool, one for the lazy pool, and one for the wet hall"): in the old control room beside the lounge's (PL1), the Olympic's (PL2, 24 windows: its pumps by their tags, circulation, each filter's DP, the Wash-O-Tron, UV, chlorine, pH, dosing, day tanks, temperature, hall air and humidity, ozone, room power) and the wet hall's (WH, 18 windows: the break tank low, the booster set tripped, stopped or isolated, the town main, the backwash pit high, inhibited and overflowing, its pumps out of AUTO, the sewer's consent, each chemical transfer tripped on high-high, its store at its low-level cut-out or its line out of AUTO, the transfer's supply, the hall's power and its fire zone), the wet hall's also on the make-up bay's wall beside its mimic (WH-CP2), one state for both. Every window of every board also on the bridge: its viewscreen, alarm sweep, Central Control's alarm rows and alarm bleep, ACCEPT ALARMS accepting them all. The lounge's annunciator now shares their sequence (`location-annunciator.ts`); the control room is 1.5 m deeper to take them. World version 56 keeps a saved lounge annunciator.
- The loading bay's chemical lines on the plumber (prompt 085, gap 2): taken from its schematic, the hypochlorite's delivery line and both mains laid by the voxel router between their wall sleeves, clear of the lorries' bays and the doors, the acid's riser by rule, the fill ports' valves set in their risers; the mains now cross at 3.8 m, the hypochlorite's fill line at 5.3 m. The corridors' chemical runs are still the corridor services' own (to be tied to the schematics).
- The Olympic's chemical lines in a schematic of their own, as every other plant room's (prompt 085, gap 3; `olympic-chemical-schematic.ts`): its two branches into its day tanks, their supply lines to the dosing pumps and the pumps' dose lines to the spool, with every valve given and tagged from the model (PL2-DT1-IV1 to PL2-DP2-NRV1): each day tank's inlet and outlet valves (the 1,000 l hypochlorite tank's actuated inlet the distribution's, its hand inlet valve on its riser beside it) and each dose line's isolating and non-return valves, in front of its dosing board. The room's enhanced network takes them in; the plumber adds nothing.
- Every chemical valve in the model (prompt 085, gap 1): the schematics give each chemical link's valves with what each is for, its normal position and its tag, derived from the model (room, machine, kind and number: LB-CAB1-IV1, HS-T2-IV3, HS-P1-NRV1, WH-XS2-IV7, PL3-DT1-IV1): each fill port's isolating valve (kept shut between deliveries) and non-return valve, each bulk tank's inlet, overfill (motorised) and outlet valves, each transfer pump's suction (once, on the header its tanks' draw-offs share) and discharge valves and its non-return valve, each selector outlet's manual and actuated valves, each day tank's inlet and outlet valves and the Olympic's 1,000 l tank's actuated inlet, and each spa cabinet's dosing isolation. The plumber adds none (it may now take several of the schematic's valves at one place, in order from the machine outward, and one at a port several links share once, on their common pipe). The valves the machines draw (the bulk tanks', the skids', the selectors', the framed day tank's) are bound to their fittings and, worked by hand, to the world's installed valves; the rest are set in their lines by the rooms' plumbing as PVC-U ball valves and check valves. Shutting a store's outlet, suction or discharge, a selector outlet's valve or a day tank's inlet valve stops the transfer there (its pump runs on against it). The fill cabinet's acid line now leaves by its top, as the hypochlorite's does, through its valves. World version 55.
- Graphics presets, Low, Medium and High, for less powerful machines (mission 087; the owner, 2026-10-28), over the individual graphics settings. High is the game as it looked; Medium draws at render quality 3 and at most 1.25 pixels a CSS pixel, without ambient occlusion or antialiasing, bloom at half size, shadow maps cut to 1024 px; Low at render quality 6 and one pixel a CSS pixel, without shadows, bloom, film grain or the pool's refraction too. Choosing a preset sets the individual choices, which can then be changed one by one; what has no choice of its own (antialiasing, the pixel-ratio cap, refraction, shadows) follows the preset in every room at once (antialiasing from the next room), with no change to the rooms (`render-style.ts`). On the settings panel's new GRAPHICS selector (LOW · MED · HIGH) in PICTURE, and the plain menu's Graphics preset. Start-up settings in the address: `?graphics=low|medium|high` and `?resolution=N` (render quality 0–10, over the preset's), applied before the first frame and saved (`settings/logic/url-settings.ts`). Screenshots draw at an exact ratio past every cap (`drawAtRatio`).
- WH-CP1, the wet hall's chemical distribution panel, is retired (prompt 091; the owner, 2026-10-28: kept as a working exhibit): gone from the wet hall, replaced by the OT-216 terminals and the control box; in dev44 it works on as the old design beside them, under a red OBSOLETE plate (retired 2026-10-28, replaced by WH-OT1, WH-OT2 and WH-CP3), its code flagged obsolete.
- The chemical transfer control is installed in the wet hall (prompt 091): Chlorella's OT-216 terminals WH-OT1 (hypochlorite) and WH-OT2 (acid) where WH-CP1 was, the control box WH-CP3 on the east wall by the acid manifold, each terminal's serial cable in its own conduit along the walls over the manifolds to the box's glands (`chem-control-conduits.ts`), the box fed from the hall's distribution into its top gland. The world holds the box (`world.chemControl`: door isolator, MCB, controller starting and hanging, each terminal's link; `chem-control.ts`) and each day tank's own low and high levels in litres, its inhibit (STOP) and FILL NOW, kept through a power cut; AUTO calls and serves only while the box's controller runs, a pump in AUTO runs on its remote-run relay, HAND needs no box, and the high-high trip still trips. Every stored level and command is logged under the terminal's tag. The schematic's `chem-panel` node is replaced by the terminals, the box and their data, control, level-signal and power links, their tags in the register (asset-0152 to asset-0154); the bridge's distribution page is now WH-CP3's. World version 57: saved day tanks get today's 20% and 90% levels.
- The wet hall annunciator's fourth row (prompt 091, for mission 099): CHEM BOX SUPPLY FAIL, CHEM BOX MCB TRIPPED, CHEM CONTROL CONTROLLER FAULT, HYPO TERMINAL NO COMMS, ACID TERMINAL NO COMMS and CHEM TRANSFER AUTO STOPPED, read from `chemControlFaults`; on its twin in the control room and on the bridge too.
- Press-and-hold for a use, in the one control scheme (prompt 091; the owner, 2026-10-28: "Let's test it out and see if it works"): every use is also a held input until the mouse button, E, the pad's A or the touch Use button is let go, and a touch (or a free mouse, where dragging looks) held still for 0.45 s uses at once and holds until it lifts (`logic/use-hold.ts`, `input/use-holding.ts`); a quicker tap uses as it lifts, as before. The OT-216 terminal's cursor keys repeat while held, the first repeat after half a second, five a second, then twenty after 2 s (`heldRepeats`); every other part still sees one use.
- The OT-216 chemical distribution terminals and their control box finished for the owner's look in dev44 (prompt 091; the owner, 2026-10-28): the box's bought-in modules badged by their makers (Voltara MCB and isolator, Neumann Dynamik supply, Quorvane QL-12/24RC controller, Fankel comms, Hartwell & Bligh relays), an I/O schedule card naming every LED, readable captions on the trial station, and script hooks for walking the menus. Dev44 is now 22 m square and laid out with the square layout's new `fit` option, so the transfer trial no longer runs into the panels in the corner.
- The double-contained chemical pipe as a pipe kind (`double-contained` in `PIPE_KINDS`, prompt 086): a PVC-U containment round an ABS carrier, with a cut-away stand in pipes_n_dreams showing the carrier, a spacer and the leak detection port at the low point; the rooms' and corridors' contained chemical lines are now drawn as that kind. The fill cabinet's two ports are now of different types, not only sizes: a 2" brass camlock for hypochlorite, a 1½" polypropylene Guillemin for acid.
- The plant's annunciator has six more windows (the owner, 2026-10-28): FILTER 1 to 5 HIGH DP, each Olympic bed's own pressure difference over the limit, and WASH-O-TRON NOT IN AUTO; on the control room's board, its twin on the bridge, and the bridge's alarm lists. World version 54 gives saved games the new windows dark.
- The Olympic's dose lines go into the dosing spool through injection quills (the owner, 2026-10-28: the tiny tube met the large pipe with no sign of a seal): a PP clamp saddle bolted round the spool, its boss, the PVDF quill and a compression nut on the tube (`createInjectionSaddle`).
- A machine's running sound is heard only in its own room (the owner, 2026-10-28: plant room 2's dosing pumps were heard in the corridor): each room tells the sound engine its scene as it listens, and a held sound from another room's scene is quietened until the player is back there.
- The dosing boards' pipework meets the room's (the owner, 2026-10-28, at PL2-DP1 and PL2-DP2): each board's suction rises to one connection at its top, where its day tank's line comes down from overhead, and its discharge falls to one low at its edge, where the dose line leaves for its injection quill; their machine ports are those points (`dosing-boards.ts`).
- Turning a bridge console's page (◄ or ►) is just a little chirp, two quiet rising blips (the owner, 2026-10-28): the recorded cue it played was too disturbing for something done so often.
- Central Control's extra-wide console pages only through designs as wide as it (the owner, 2026-10-28): a station's smaller panel no longer shrinks into the middle of it; today that is its own page only.
- The bridge warns of every annunciator's alarms (the owner, 2026-10-28): its ACCEPT, alarm bleep, ALARMS IN, NEWEST and GO TO and its alarm sweep take in the lounge annunciator's windows as well as the plant's, ACCEPT ALARMS accepting both; the tank levels page flags a pool's balance tank tripped on low-low ("47 % · TRIPPED") whatever its level now.
- The pause menu half as large again, to be read easily (the owner, 2026-10-28).
- The lounge pool's annunciator (the owner, 2026-10-28): sixteen windows of its plant room's faults (balance tank low; circulation tripped on low-low; either pump tripped; no pump in AUTO; flow low; each filter off-line; filter DP high; backwash pit high; UV lamp fail; ozone in air; chlorine and pH out of range; day tank low; room power fail), ISA sequence M with a first-out window flashing twice as fast, a horn with MUTE, ACCEPT, RESET and LAMP TEST; on the plant room's south wall and, its twin, in the control room, one state in the world for both. Annunciator windows' legends now shrink to fit.
- Every dosing pump about a third as loud (−10 dB) (the owner, 2026-10-28): plainly heard at its board, as a diaphragm pump is loud for its size; masking it under the circulation pumps across a room is voice limiting's (prompt 098).
- The stepper dosing pumps (DP-102, the acid pump) rattle like a motor-driven diaphragm pump (the owner, 2026-10-28, from a recording he gave): 42 knocks a second rich in harmonics from 250 Hz to 1 kHz, swelling slightly, synthesised from the recording's measurements; their sound was a tone swept up and down, a siren's wail. The solenoid pumps' tick is now a hard clack, a bright tick over a low knock.
- The solenoid dosing pumps (DP-101A and the like) tick once a stroke, as meant (the owner, 2026-10-28): their tick was a noise gated by a square wave, open half of every stroke, so they sounded like a pulsing warning beeper.
- Sand filters say what their tank is doing (the owner, 2026-10-28): the lounge's and the Olympic's filters' descriptions and inspection headings read from each bed in the world, "the tank is backwashing", "rinsing", "filtering, and seems clean", "filtering, but seems dirty", "filtering, but seems very dirty and the sand is pitted", or standing (`filter-condition.ts`).
- Graphics settings for the costlier effects (the owner, 2026-10-28): Ambient occlusion and Bloom and glow each Off, Low, Medium or High (fewer AO samples; bloom's blur on a quarter or half of the image), Film grain and vignette on or off, and a Resolution cap of 720p, 1080p, 1440p or Native, which limits the image's height whatever the screen (screenshots ignore it). Live from the pause menu's Graphics tab; every room with post-processing follows them through one shared `followEffectSettings`. Defaults keep today's look.
- The bridge uses six GTAO ambient-occlusion samples instead of twelve, retaining its contact shadows with less GPU work at high render resolutions.

- The Lidoluxe spa cabinet's upper row of controls (POWER, its lamps, JETS) moved 1.5 cm down and 2 cm left, clear of the foot spa's DRAINING and FILLING strip, which overprinted it (the owner, 2026-10-28); the jacuzzi's cabinet, built the same, moves with it.
- The menu panels have accessible copies for screen readers and switch access (mission 096): the settings panel as a hidden dialog of real buttons, ranges, a select and a live status line, built from the same menu model; the title's list kept, hidden, beside its box; focus kept in step with the panels' focus rings both ways. The menu bindings are in the control scheme's help and the bindings table.
- The menu panels' sounds (button presses, knob detents, selector and key clicks) are heard front centre, not placed in the room (the owner, 2026-10-28), and on the title too (`front-centre.ts`).
- The menus are now Penfold control panels by default (mission 096; the owner, 2026-10-28): the title's box in the bottom left and the settings panel SP-1 over the game (Esc, Start or the gear), worked by mouse, touch, keyboard and gamepad alike. Keyboard and gamepad move an amber focus ring between the controls by where they stand (arrows, d-pad, left stick; Tab cycles), press with Enter, Space or A, turn a focused knob or selector with Left and Right or LB and RB, and back out with Esc or B. One menu reader in the control scheme (`menu-navigation.ts`, `menu-input.ts`) now serves every menu, the plain ones' own gamepad polling included; while the settings panel is open the walker takes no input. `?menu=plain` gives the plain DOM menus, which are also used where WebGL 2 is missing or a panel fails to start.
- Haulmark, a lorry maker (prompt 097), the game's thirtieth brand, with a crest of its own; the brand gallery (dev8) widened to seven double-sided boards down its middle, so every brand has a board (Ironside and Lumenhaus had none).
- The title's menu as a Penfold box, behind `?menu=panel` (mission 096, stage 2; the owner, 2026-10-28): one machined plate in the bottom left of the title, four illuminated push buttons in a column with an engraved legend to the right of each (NEW GAME, CONTINUE, SETTINGS, INSTRUCTIONS), over the unchanged attract sequence; NEW GAME asks to be pressed again before overwriting a save, CONTINUE is lit only when there is one, and SETTINGS and INSTRUCTIONS open the SP-1 panel with a BACK button. Laid out on the rack grid, drawn only on change.
- The settings menu as a Penfold instrument panel, a trial behind `?menu=panel` (mission 096, stage 0; the owner, 2026-10-27: "in a game about control panels, I want my control panel to be a control panel"): a full-screen panel drawn over the room on its own transparent canvas with an orthographic desk view, scrolled by wheel or drag; a module bolted in for each setting (four quarter-turn fasteners, an engraved title), set on a rack grid of standard module sizes (aligned edges, even gutters, rows of one height), each plate bolted into a dark rack opening with four domed, slotted fasteners, grouped GAME, PICTURE, SOUND, CONTROLS, INSTRUCTIONS and MAINTENANCE under legends on the frame. Render quality (WORST to BEST, read as the per cent of resolution drawn), field of view, master volume and look sensitivity are knobs on dials printed with their own scales, each driving a moving-coil meter whose needle follows with a real meter's damped lag, and a seven-segment readout; INVERT Y a latching lit push button; ON-SCREEN CONTROLS a lever selector, AUTO · ON · OFF; RESUME, SAVE, VIEW LINK and QUIT TO TITLE (lit, press twice) push buttons with a VFD status line; NOCLIP a key switch and DEV PANEL a button on Dymo tape; the bindings on a printed card; Penfold's mark and an engraved rating plate with the build. All wired to the real settings and actions. It does not pause the game, and draws only when something changes. Lettering can be built finer for close views (`withLetteringScale`). Seven-segment readouts' decimal points now stand clear of their digits. The plates butt edge to edge in one continuous field inside the bezel, every row full width, each plate with rounded corners, a softened edge and four avionics fasteners. Its plates are machined metal, each brushed at its own angle and tone, lit by two lamps carried with the camera that lean after the pointer, so the sheen moves over the plates; the parts' caps and lenses glossier, lettering kept matt. The layout is a reusable rack grid (`rack-grid.ts`: spans, named cells or auto-placement, dense and shelf flows, nesting, stretch, and blanking plates screwed over every rack place left empty), the panel declared on it. The DOM menus stay the default.
- REBOOT DISPLAYS on Central Control (CC-01, its PLANT page; the owner, 2026-10-27: "possible to reboot the bridge controls from one of the bridge controls"): pressed, it asks to be confirmed (the button reads CONFIRM and the question runs across the panel's top, heard as the bridge's confirm sound) and lapses after 5 seconds unconfirmed; confirmed, the bridge's displays are switched off in the world, every one black at once, and on again a second later, each booting on its own drawn durations as in the first view (black, emblem, black, draw-in, the main viewscreen last), heard as their interfaces start up. Its lights and theme are untouched. A confirm-step command for any wired panel (`bridge-bindings.ts`: `confirm`, `pressConfirm`; `parody-panel.ts`).
- The bridge's consoles page and lock (the owner, 2026-10-27; `doc/research/bridge-console-paging.md`, "Implemented as"): every console has a small strip in its glass's bottom-left corner. A workstation's lower panel steps with ◄ ► through the roles (Flow Navigator, Boiler Helmsman, Central Control and the rest), showing the role's control panel, and its upper displays then page only through that role's schematics, overviews and displays, reset to them when the role changes; the displays that turn by themselves (upper screens, back walls, viewscreen) also have LOCK, between the arrows, which holds the design shown. Central Control has a second strip choosing the main viewscreen's page (the alarm list still takes over until ACCEPT). Every choice is kept in the world (`bridgeConsoles`, `WORLD_VERSION` 52). Generic pager widget `ConsolePager` in `ui2d`.
- Sit on any chair (the owner, 2026-10-27: "using a chair should position you in seated position for that console"): using a chair that declares a seat seats the walker, the eye about 1.2 m up over its seat, leaning a little forward, facing the chair's way; a console's chair turns and pitches the view down at its panel, in reach. Looking about and using things stay free; C or V (the crouch button, the pad's B) or any step stands the walker up where they sat down from, at standing height; nothing pushes them out of the chair meanwhile. The help line says how to stand. Generic: a chair declares `userData.seat` (`declareSeat`, `controls/input/seating.ts`: seat height and an optional point to face), the pose is pure logic (`controls/logic/seat.ts`, tested), `createFirstPerson` gains `sit`, `stand` and `seated`, and every shell room's use sits on a declared chair. On the bridge: the operator's chair faces Central Control, the two beside it their command panels, each station's chair its station's panel; the lair's chairs are seats too.
- The bridge's unaccepted-alarm sounder is the owner's `amber_alert`, resynthesised, in place of the synthesised two-tone bleep (the owner, 2026-10-27: "the noise we hear until we press accept alarms is still the wrong noise"): from Central Control at once and every 10 s while any alarm is unaccepted, silenced by ACCEPT, at a level well above the consoles' chatter (`ALARM_SOUND`, `LEVELS.alarm` in `console-sounds.ts`; `alarm-bleep.ts`). It is the bridge's only unaccepted-alarm sound.
- The bridge uses the owner's resynthesised console sounds exclusively for its electronic sounds (the owner, 2026-10-27: "check all the noises we use in the scene"): audited, its touch screens, boot cues, chatter and alarm sounder were already recordings or are now; the last synthesised beep, its doors' card readers going green, now plays `correct_entry` (`CARD_READ_SOUND`; `cardReader` and `equipmentDoors` take an optional beep, every other door keeping the synthesised one). Its room tone is the owner's recording, and its light switch's rocker click is a recorded mechanical click.
- The bridge's theme fades out evenly to the ear (the owner, 2026-10-27: it still seemed to end suddenly): a steady fall in decibels over 12 seconds after its first minute (3 seconds if the walker leaves), where a straight ramp in gain stayed loud and dropped away at the end.
- The bridge's displays are heard booting only as each one's interface starts up, drawing in after its emblem and black (the owner, 2026-10-27): a quiet `now_online`, `processing`, `correct_entry` or `ack_trill` from that screen, `engines_online` from the main viewscreen; the power rising (`system_booting_up`, dropped) and the emblems are silent.
- The bridge sounds like its consoles (the owner, 2026-10-27): its touch screens answer with the owner's own console sounds, resynthesised (`resources/sound/effects/resynthesized_bloops/`), in place of synthesised bleeps: an ordinary press `processing` or `actioning` (never the same twice running), a command carried out `correct_entry`, alarms accepted `ack_trill`, a refusal `lock_out`, a page change `look_here`. Its displays are heard booting: `system_booting_up` from the main viewscreen as their power rises, a quiet `now_online` or `processing` from each display as its emblem comes up and `correct_entry` or `ack_trill` as it comes on (passed through a gate so some 25 displays never pile up), and `engines_online` as the main viewscreen comes on; an instant power-up is silent. Its consoles' background chatter plays a neutral set of them at random instead of made-up chirps. Each file is balanced to one loudness by its measured level (`console-sounds.ts`). `ui2d` gains the press sounds `done` and `ack` and `BootScreen.onPhase`; touch surfaces take their own `sounds` (the parody consoles' recordings; every other touch surface keeps the synthesised bleeps) and a kit's boot phases can be heard (`onBootPhase`).
- The bridge's ramps' oak handrails run only along their glass guards, no longer on down to each ramp's foot (the owner, 2026-10-27: over-extended).
- The bridge's theme no longer ends suddenly: it was released half a second into its fade, cutting it off. It now plays its first minute and fades out slowly over 10 seconds (3 seconds if the walker leaves first), the track let go only once silent (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The bridge's main lights come on one after another, clockwise seen from above from the front, 0.2 s apart (the cove rings now in arcs, the wall coves, the ribs' strips, the aft strip and their lamps), and the dome last, 5 s after the last of the others has started; the same order on every visit (the owner, 2026-10-27; `SWEEP`, `sweepStarts`, tested). The main viewscreen boots last, 2 s after the latest of the other displays has begun (`lastAfter`). The bridge's theme is now the whole track, played from its start and faded out over 3 s from 45 s (`THEME_LENGTH`).
- Displays have power (the owner, 2026-10-27): every display is UNPOWERED, OFF, BOOTING or RUNNING (`ui2d`: `DisplayState`, `BootScreen.setPower`, `BootGroup.setPower`), and the bridge's displays share one power, the control room's supply and a new world flag, `bridgeDisplaysOn` (`WORLD_VERSION` 51: on in older saves that have heard the theme). RESET switches it off, so every display on the bridge (the viewscreen, the desk's panels, the stations' and back walls' screens) is black at once however it was built; the first view switches it on 2 s after the doors shut, each display booting on its own drawn durations; losing the control room's supply blacks them too. The theme is now cued once and stopped only by the walker leaving or its own end (`createThemeCue`, tested).
- The CONTROL ROOM MODE plate's RESET also shuts the control room's doors at once (the owner, 2026-10-27); in development, `window.mainCorridor.useDoor(<room>)` works a corridor door from the console.
- The bridge's first view now begins as the corridor doors shut behind the walker, not on crossing the threshold (the owner, 2026-10-27: "walk into a mysterious space for a bit"): until then the room stays as seen from outside, and leaving before the doors shut starts nothing. The emergency glow now stays black for 5 s (was 4) before fading back; the theme, the displays' boot (from 2 s) and the main lights (2 s after the last display) all follow the doors' closing. `stepReveal` (`logic/bridge-lights-up.ts`, tested), `startBridgeRoom`'s `onDoors`. Fixed: after RESET the bridge's displays were still seen on through the corridor's doors: the bridge is suspended when it is first drawn beyond the doors, and leaving's "displays stay on" ran then too; now only a walker who was inside, past the first view's start, leaves them on (`displaysOnLeaving`, tested in both orders, built after RESET and RESET after building).
- The bridge's reveal rewritten to the owner's specification (2026-10-27, `doc/notes/bridge_reveal.md`): on the first view the emergency glow goes out over 1 s, stays black 4 s and fades back over 1 s; the theme begins at 1 s, in the dark; from 2 s every display boots on its own randomly drawn durations (black about 4 s, the emblem about 7 s, black about 2 s, then the interface), drifting apart; the main lights come up 2 s after the last display is on. On later visits the glow and displays are already on and the main lights come up at 2 s. Leaving, the main lights go out but the displays and the glow stay on, as seen from the corridor. `BootGroup.bootEach`, `sampleBoot`, `sampleSpread` in `ui2d`; `entranceKind`, `firstViewGlow`, `entranceLightsAt` in `logic/bridge-lights-up.ts`; tested. Fixed with it: after RESET the bridge's displays still showed on through the corridor's doors until the walker came in; a bridge built while its theme is unheard now starts with its displays black however the page was opened, and a touch screen repaints at once when booted or skipped (`BootScreen.onChange`), not only at its room's next frame.
- The bridge's reveal in order: the bridge no longer switches its own lights on in the reveal (which brought them on before the displays booted); RESET leaves MAIN LIGHTS on, the reveal keeping the room dark until its time; the emergency glow keeps the entrance's own time; the theme fades out over 3 s if the walker leaves before it ends. The sequence is written down in `doc/notes/bridge_reveal.md` (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The bridge's entrance retimed to the owner's sequence (2026-10-27): first found under its emergency glow alone, its displays black (as seen from the corridor too, and again after the CONTROL ROOM MODE plate's RESET); walking in, the displays boot at scattered times (start delay about 2.7 s on average, the emblem about 5 s); the main lights come on two seconds after the last display is up (11.85 s). The displays' emblem is shown about 5 s everywhere.
- The bridge's displays boot as the walker comes in from the corridor (the owner, 2026-10-27): black at first, their emblems appearing in a scattered wave from 0.2 s to 1.2 s (each 2 s), then their interfaces drawing in from about 2.9 s as the under-console glow comes up (now at 3.0 s), the main lights following from 4.0 s (`ENTRANCE_BOOT`, `entranceInterfaces`, `LIGHTS_UP.dark` now 4.0 s, `ENTRANCE_GLOW.at` 3.0 s; tested); arriving any other way (a reload, a link) they are simply on.
- Displays boot up (the owner, 2026-10-27): every touch screen can boot: black, then 2.5 s of an emblem of our own (a laurel-wreathed grey ring round a dark-blue disc of pool water: a red-and-white lane rope, white ripples, a caustic net, twinkling glints; FEDERATED MUNICIPAL BATHS under it), a 0.5 s fade to black, then its interface drawn in over 1 s (elbows and bars sweeping out, panels appearing in turn), touches taken only once operational. Generic in the `ui2d` kit (`BootScreen`, `BootGroup`, pure `bootState`, `drawEmblem`; tested), every touch surface's screens run through it (on until booted; `bootGroupOf(kit)` boots a room's at scattered offsets), at most 15 repaints a second while booting. dev48 and dev49 boot their screens as the room is entered and gain a DISPLAY BOOT panel (BOOT ALL, staggered over 1.5 s; SKIP).
- The bridge's theme, "Boldly Into the Deep End" (the owner's, Treblo), 30 s, plays once a save on the walker's first visit to the bridge (`bridgeThemeHeard`, WORLD_VERSION 50); the CONTROL ROOM MODE plate gains a RESET button, an amber illuminated push button that lights briefly when pressed (pressed anywhere on it, not only its small cap), that forgets the first visit and turns the bridge's lights off, as first found, so the next one plays it all again. The theme is placed nowhere: once begun it plays to its end wherever the walker goes, and starts again from the top on coming in while the bridge is as first seen (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Entering the bridge with its MAIN LIGHTS off, they switch on by themselves after 3 s, as with an occupancy sensor (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- `?room=main_corridor&from=<room>` puts the walker outside that room's doors, facing into the corridor, for every door off the ring; `from=bridge` at the control room's; the wet hall's two pairs told apart by `&door=east` or `&door=west` (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The bridge's entrance in three steps (the owner, 2026-10-27): walking in from the corridor, the displays only, then at 1 s the glow under the consoles coming up quickly, then from 3 s the main lights as before (`ENTRANCE_GLOW`, `entranceGlow`, `LIGHTS_UP.dark` now 3.0 s; tested); once the main lights are full the glow fades out again unless EMERGENCY LIGHTS is on.
- The bridge's EMERGENCY LIGHTS are now only a very dim glow from under the consoles (the owner's decision, 2026-10-27): hidden strips at the feet of the operator's desk, the six stations and the four rear stations, their faint cool light spilling half a metre across the floor, and a little fill, enough to just about get around (`underConsoleGlow`, tested); the luminaire over the doors, the aft walls' fittings and the step and ramp strips are gone. MAIN LIGHTS stays the overhead and cove lighting; the glow still comes on from the central battery if the lighting way fails.
- Light switches with locator LEDs, a standard part (the owner, 2026-10-27: so a switch can be found in the dark): the parts library's plate light switch (`lightSwitch`, one, two or three gangs) now has a small amber neon-style locator lens under each rocker by default, glowing softly while its gang is OFF and out while it is ON, as long as the switch's `supply` is live (live where none is given; `locators: false` leaves them off; `switch-locator.ts`, tested). Every light switch in the game has them (the bridge's, dev0's, the architectural rooms', the courtyard's); the bridge's is supplied while the control room's lighting way is live or the central battery can supply. Tried on a new LIGHT SWITCHES board in dev0 (south wall, beside the character LCD), with a SUPPLY switch to show them out.
- OLYMPIC FILTER WASHING, a working filter-washing console on the bridge (the owner, 2026-10-27: "a filter operations console that really works ... see the dirt percentage of each filter, and then trigger a cleaning operation immediately"; then the Olympic alone for now): a page (FILTER WASH) on the Flow Navigator's PL2-CP1 after PUMPS, MIMIC and SAFETY, and a panel of its own in dev48. For each of the Olympic's five filters (PL2-F1 to F5) its state under the Lidoluxe Wash-O-Tron (IN SERVICE, BACKWASHING / SETTLING / RINSE with the step's progress, TO SERVICE while its valves move, OUT OF SERVICE, LOCKED OUT, VALVE FAULT, AUTO or HAND, QUEUED), its DIRT % as a bar with the backwash-due mark, its bed's pressure difference and flow; the programme's state, the wash's progress and time left, the backwash pit. CLEAN NOW sends the Wash-O-Tron's WASH NOW (`wash-now`), starting the programme if it is off; RUN and HOLD work the programme; LOCKOUT, and CLEAN NOW of a filter locked out or out of service, are refused as local control only. DIRT % is the bed's dirt against its backwash-due loading, twice its dirt reference (`BACKWASH_DUE_LOADING`): 0 clean, 100 due; a new game's beds read 80 %. Pure logic in `logic/olympic-filter-washing.ts`, a page registry for the coming per-console paging (`logic/bridge-pages.ts`, `views/bridge-pages.ts`), tested: a wash asked from the page runs in the world and the filter's dirt falls to a tenth.
- The bridge's room tone wherever the walker is on the bridge, and through its open doors (the owner, 2026-10-27: "the room tone should be heard anytime you're in the room, and also if you're standing outside the room with the doors open"): one shared room tone for the game (`sharedBridgeAmbience`), driven by the bridge (the walker in it, however they came: walking in, a reload or link straight into it, noclip; rising with the lights only when coming in from the corridor) and by the main corridor, which reports each frame, while the walker is in it, how far its control room doors stand open when the CONTROL ROOM MODE knob has them leading to the bridge (`doorShare`, `reportedPresence`, tested): full on the bridge, 15 % through fully open doors, fading as they shut, nothing at OLD.
- ACCEPT shown only when there is something to accept (the owner, 2026-10-27): Central Control's ACCEPT ALARMS button, and the ACCEPT in the sidebar of any touch panel with an annunciator (the bridge's CR-CP2, dev48's), are blank while no window flashes and come back when an alarm comes in (`awaitingAccept`, tested).
- The bridge's alarm bleep (the owner, 2026-10-27: "a more persistent, more regular bleeping, like every 10 seconds ... from the center console where I have to clear the alarm"): while any alarm on the world's annunciator is unaccepted and the walker is on the bridge, a two-tone bleep (a high-low pair, twice) sounds from Central Control on the operator's desk at once and then every 10 s, stopping on ACCEPT and starting again at once when a new alarm comes in (`logic/alarm-bleep.ts`, tested).
- Alarm acceptance shared and kept in the world (the owner, 2026-10-27: "if I've cleared the alarm, when I return to the room, the alarm should remain cleared ... and that should also clear it on the annunciator consoles in the rest of the world"): the control room's annunciator is now the world's (`game/annunciator.ts`, `world.annunciator`; `WORLD_VERSION` 49, older saves coming up with every window dark): the world senses the plant's alarms every step, whichever room the walker is in; an alarm coming in flashes until ACCEPT, then burns steady, an accepted window whose alarm has cleared stays lit until RESET (ISA sequence M, as before), and an alarm that clears unaccepted keeps flashing until accepted. ACCEPT at Central Control, the bridge's CR-CP2, or the old control room's annunciator board (new world actions `alarms/accept` and `alarms/reset`, logged) is done for all of them and the viewscreen, and kept across rooms and reloads (`logic/world-annunciator.ts`). LAMP TEST stays each board's own.
- The operator's desk softened (the owner, 2026-10-27: "soften the corners of this desk ... round the convex edges"; then the inside as well): the EPCOT desk is now one sweep of a rounded cross-section round its five segments (`logic/rounded-desk.ts`), its outside's vertical corners where the segments meet rounded at 90 mm on plan, the operator's side's inside corners (the top's edge, the knee face) filleted at 80 mm, the top's operator edge rounded 25 mm above and 12 mm below, the upstand's crest given a 50 mm flat with both edges rounded 25 mm, the ledge under the screens 20 mm; the cheeks at its open ends follow the profile, every edge bevelled 12 mm; the forward stations' and the rear stations' consoles likewise rounded (30 mm bevels on every edge and corner, 45 mm along their tops' edges). Panels and screens stay flush in their segments (the top's plane tested), the plaques in place, the collision unchanged; the desk now two meshes (cream, charcoal) where it was seventeen.
- The bridge's stage steps' handrails end at their foot in line with the ramps' guards beside them, no longer projecting forward to the bottom step (the owner, 2026-10-27: "the same width as the railings"; `stepRailFoot`).
- The bridge's consoles chatter: now and then (gaps of mean 25 s, deviation 10 s) a random station round the room bleeps or bloops a chirp made up afresh (a blip, a pair, a trill, a run, a warble), heard from where it stands, only with the walker on the bridge (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The bridge's room tone is now the owner's own synthesised recording, a seamless one-minute loop (OGG), in place of the procedural hum, which stays for dev47's trial (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Displays with rounded corners (the owner, 2026-10-27: "slightly rounded edges so they don't look like old-fashioned CRTs"): every touch surface's frame is now rounded by default, by 4 % of its shorter side and at least 15 mm (`cornerRadius`; the bridge's main viewscreen's about 0.26 m), its screens rounded to match inside the bezel (`screenCornerRadius`) and their pictures clipped to the rounded corners; `radius: 0` (and `screenRadius: 0`) keep sharp corners. So the bridge's schematic screens, touch panels and viewscreen, and dev47, dev48 and dev49's panels, are all rounded. The bridge's synthesised ambience is hooked in: heard on entering, rising with the lights, silent when the bridge is only built or seen through the corridor's doors.
- The bridge's light switch (the owner, 2026-10-27): a white two-gang plate switch inside the corridor doors, to the right as one comes in, 1.2 m up, its rockers MAIN LIGHTS and EMERGENCY LIGHTS with small engraved labels (the library's `lightSwitch`).
