Its state is the world's (`lighting.switches.bridge`, a `lighting/board` action `switch`; `WORLD_VERSION` 48, `fillDefaults` adding it to older saves). MAIN LIGHTS on brings the bridge's lights up in the entrance's sequence, off fades them down, the displays glowing on; the main lights also need the control room's lighting way live. EMERGENCY LIGHTS lights new emergency fittings, dim and cool (a luminaire over the doors, one high on each aft wall, LED strips on the stage's step nosings and the ramps' open edges, a faint cool fill), from the way or the central battery; they also come on, on the battery, when the way's supply fails. The entrance plays only with MAIN LIGHTS on.
- The bridge's ambience (mission 095; the owner, 2026-10-27: "find the Star Trek bridge noise and see if we can resynthesize something that sounds similar"): an original synthesised room tone in that spirit, nothing sampled: a low drone of slowly beating oscillators about 55 Hz, the rumble and body of a big ship's air and a ventilation hiss from two long noise loops, and sparse quiet computer chirps (blips, pairs, trills, glides, console warbles) from a seeded scheduler at irregular times, each layer swelling at its own slow rate so it never repeats; QUIET, STANDARD and BUSY variants, a gain, fade in and out (`components/sound/logic/bridge-ambience.ts`, `components/sound/audio/bridge-ambience.ts`). Heard only while the walker is in the bridge (faintly through open doors at most, silent as they shut; tested). Tried on a small BRIDGE AMBIENCE plate in dev47; not yet hooked into the bridge (the lines are in the mission). Credited in `CREDITS.md` as original synthesis.
- The CONTROL ROOM MODE knob is locked out while the control room's doors stand open: it rattles against its stop and will not turn (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The CONTROL ROOM MODE knob (the owner, 2026-10-27): an Easter egg on the main corridor's wall beside the control room doors, just past their card reader: an old-fashioned black pointer knob (the parts library's `createKnob` pointer, 40 mm) of two positions on a small screwed grey plate, labelled in embossed Dymo tape CONTROL ROOM MODE, OLD and NEW. Left alone at OLD the doors lead to the old control room, un-retired (its name CONTROL ROOM again); turned to NEW they lead to the bridge. Where they lead changes only while they are shut; the view through them is the room they lead to; both rooms' doors lead back to the corridor at the same doors (`logic/control-room-mode.ts`, `views/control-room-mode-knob.ts`). The world keeps the knob (`controlRoomMode`, `WORLD_VERSION` 47; older saves come up at OLD).
- The bridge's entrance (the owner, 2026-10-27): walking in from the corridor, the bridge is dark, only its controls and displays glowing; after 2.5 s its lights come up over about 3.5 s, the coves round the dome first, then the dome, the walls' and ribs' coves and the aft strip (`logic/bridge-lights-up.ts`, `views/bridge-lights-up.ts`). Presentation only, not in the world: it scales what the room's lighting way gives, plays on every visit, not on opening or reloading the page on the bridge, and the lights go out behind a walker leaving so the room waits dark. The bridge's coves and dome are now light fittings, following the control room's lighting way.
- The bridge's back walls (mission 095; the owner, 2026-10-27: "get rid of the conference table and chairs and just ... have displays on the back wall"): the conference table and its chairs are gone from the bridge's stage (dev46 and the game; dev45's lair keeps its own), the stage now open floor; on the two aft walls six schematic displays from the stations' kit, each turning through its designs and repainting only on change: high over the light strip's hood a 4.8 × 2.6 m SITE STATUS (Central Control's designs) on the west and THE WHOLE PLANT (the main viewscreen's) on the east, and at eye height either side of the rear stations MAIN POOLS (Olympic and lounge overviews), SMALL POOLS (jacuzzi and footbath), HEAT AND GAS and AIR AND POWER (`BACK_WALL_SCREENS`, `backWallScreenPlaces`). The bridge's east side wall, which has nothing on it, is reserved for a future doorway to a captain's ready room (`READY_ROOM_RESERVE`, `inReadyRoomReserve`), tested to stay empty with its floor open. The back walls' displays and the rear stations' wall screens now stand 10 mm clear of the walls' fins (`BRIDGE_FIN`, `WALL_SCREEN_OFF`; the owner saw the large displays cutting into them), tested with the wall panels.
- The bridge's schematic screens (mission 095): an upper screen on each forward station, a wall screen over each rear station, the desk's upstands' screens (where the idle placeholders were), each its station's schematic browser; the main viewscreen shows the plant's overviews, chosen by a touch, until an alarm is up.
- Schematic screens for the bridge's stations (mission 095; the owner, 2026-10-27): new `ui2d` schematic components (a flow network with moving flows, a cut-away vessel, ring gauges, a sensor sweep, a status matrix, a sequence strip, a targeting reticle, a rotator turning through designs with a wipe, and a schematic browser to bring any of them up); a hydrodynamic overview in the style of dev48's OLYMPIC HYDRODYNAMIC OVERVIEW for every pool and for the heating, gas, chemical distribution, air, power, backwash and make-up circuits, each traced from its room's model and live from the world; three to six designs for each station, Central Control and the main viewscreen. All of them on screens in dev48, each labelled; each station's browser in a new dev49.
- The main corridor's sign over the bridge's doors reads just CONTROL ROOM (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The bridge without its stair (the owner, 2026-10-27: going down it, the walker's head clipped through the room above): the game's bridge has one whole floor, its 8 m stair, stair hall, opening and glass guarding gone (dev45 and dev46 keep theirs as prototypes). For now the corridor's control room doors are set flush into the bridge's west wall, a storey up (a room's doorway may now stand in an angled wall and at its own floor's height, `CustomDoorway` and `doorY` in the shell room runner), where the owner's view from (−9.2, −2.7) looking west meets it, the rest of that wall kept clear for the elevator to come; the walker arrives just inside, facing the room. No sign over them on the bridge's side; the corridor's sign over them and its wayfinding say CONTROL ROOM, the room itself THE BRIDGE.
- Moving about the bridge (the owner: hard to get about): its walk collision now follows what is drawn, each desk segment, station and rear console as its own angled outline (`polygonCells`), the chairs by their seats, the corners' fillets as drawn (now no more than 0.9 m out of their corners: one had stood across the east ramp's foot), the walls carrying nothing on the floor; a test floods the room from the doors with a 0.9 m walkway and finds every station, the operator's chair, the stage by its steps and both ramps, and every rear station; the rear stations moved along the aft walls to stand clear of the table. Walking down the stage's steps and both ramps is tested too.
- The bridge's desk panels lie flush in their own segments' sloping tops, centred, parallel to their front edges and inside their narrower edges (the assistant duty manager's and the counsellor's were skewed across into their neighbours: tested); the officers' plaques come from one list (`plaquePlaces`, tested: each in its own desk's edge facing its officer, the assistant duty manager's on the duty manager's right). The conference table's chairs face the table, with one at each end. The desk's end cheeks stand clear of the segments' ends (they striped where coplanar). Panels on the bridge drop the redundant LIVE and REIMAGINES footers; the viewscreen reads OLYMPIC CIRCULATION or ALERT.
- The bridge's officers (mission 095; the owner's scheme, 2026-10-27): the six forward stations are now the Flow Navigator, Disinfection Tactical, Boiler Helmsman, Life Support Officer, Chief Electrical Engineer and Security Chief; four standing rear stations along the aft walls on the stage (Water Science Officer with the chart recorders' trends, Drainage Officer with the backwash pit's pumps, Loading Bay Chief with the fill cabinet, Operations Officer with WH-CP1), wired to the world as the rest; the command chairs' panels on the desk either side of Central Control: the Assistant Duty Manager's (bathers against each pool's limit, turned away, opening hours) and the Bather Welfare Counsellor's (water and hall air temperatures, humidity, and the Lounge and Olympic thermostats raised or lowered half a degree). Each station's title is a small engraved plaque (200 × 36 mm) set into its desk's front edge, not a standing sign; the viewscreen's placeholder label is gone in the game.
- The bridge's main viewscreen, live (mission 095, `views/bridge-viewscreen.ts`): the Olympic's circulation as an animated schematic, each machine's state as the control room's main pool board has it and the water moving while its pumps run, with the pool's flow, free chlorine, pH, temperature and bathers beside it; while any alarm on the bridge's annunciator is unaccepted, a flashing ALERT and the alarm list with the room to go to instead, until it is accepted at Central Control or CR-CP2. Coarse (64 pixels a metre), repainted on change.
- The bridge's consoles, wired to the world (mission 095, first batch): dev48's reimagined panels on its six stations (PL2-CP1 circulation, PL2-CP2 dosing controller, CR-MP2 heating, CR-MP3 air handling, SR-IS3 changeover, CR-CP6 fire repeater), Central Control on the operator's desk, and CR-MP1, CR-CP2 and WH-CP1 on its front walls, each reading the world as its original does and asking it what its original's controls ask (`logic/bridge-bindings.ts`, tested: every item of each panel wired, each bound to its original's node in its room's model; START and STOP reach the starters, as the mimics'). Local-only controls (HAND · OFF · AUTO, trip resets, the changeover, releasing an emergency stop) are shown but refused with a deny bleep and LOCAL CONTROL ONLY and where to go; what the world does not model yet (the pool emergency alarm, a dosing stop, the PA, the fire panel's silence) is refused as NOT YET MODELLED. Central Control (CC-01): the time and day, whether the centre is open, water quality (free chlorine, pH, temperature) per pool against its healthy band, bathers per pool against its limit, the alarm summary with ACCEPT (the bridge's annunciator, shared with its CR-CP2), the fire alarm's zones, STOP CIRCULATION per pool (struck here, released only at its panel), pumps, flow, mains, generator, emergency lighting, the battery and the chemical stores. The desk's upstand screens show an idle display in the bridge's style instead of flat teal. `createParodyPanel` takes an optional wiring; dev48 still runs on its trials.

- Touch panels repaint and upload their canvas only when their `ui2d` tree has changed, and their own animations (blinks, moving flows, wandering trial values) at most 10 times a second by the clock (`PAINT_HZ` in `touch-surface.ts`; mission 095, after mission 094's benchmark): measured with the component benchmark, a still panel uploads nothing (as before), dev47's sidebar frame went from 59.8 uploads and 68.9 M texels a second to 9.2 and 10.6 M, dev48's alert matrix from 59.9 and 35.5 M to 9.2 and 5.4 M, PL2-CP1's reimagining from 47.4 and 23.8 M to 9.2 and 4.6 M (these animate on their trials; a panel bound to a still world uploads only when it changes). On the bridge, the stair hall's walls now stop under the room's floor slab, so their tops no longer flicker through the carpet round the stair's opening, and the carpet's ring is drawn clear of the floor.
- The bridge's look (mission 095; the owner, 2026-10-27: "light the room like the Star Trek bridge ... cove lighting, big sweeping curves ... soft chairs ... beige or peach coloured carpet"; "two layers of cove lighting ... very little direct lighting ... a very rounded space"), in dev46 and the game's bridge (`views/bridge-dressing.ts`): soft indirect light from a lit oval dome over the command area and two concentric coves round it (inward onto the dome, outward over the ceiling), six cove-lit ribs fanning to the big wall, wall-top coves, a strip over the aft consoles, glossy black ceiling panels and chaser lights along the viewscreen's foot; the stage's front now a horseshoe curving round the command area (`edgeAt`, tested), its parapet a beige plinth with a grained light oak cap, black and silver lines and burgundy carpet below, the ramps curving with it, the conference table moved back to clear it; a rounded fillet in every corner; peach carpet with a darker ring round the command area; upholstered chairs, the operator's larger with padded arms and black insets for its armrest panels.
- A component benchmark, development only (mission 094, the owner, 2026-10-27): `bench.html?bench=components&slot=bench094` builds every component kind the rooms use (each room machine as its room builds it, the development rooms' exhibits, dev0's boards and plinths, the touch panels of dev47 and dev48, the Wash-O-Tron and the control room's recorders on their own), one at a time, and measures its update (per frame, and back to back), its render, its texture uploads and texels a second, its canvas drawing, draw calls, triangles, materials, textures and build time, with the world frozen and running; ranks them by cost where used (copies in the equipped rooms, the control room, the Olympic plant room and the wet hall counted twice); checks each against a budget (`logic/component-bench.ts`); prints the table to the console and shows it as Markdown. `secs`, `only` and `limit` narrow a run; a reload part way carries on.
- The bridge is the control room (mission 095; the owner, 2026-10-27: "why don't we wire this in as the real replacement control room? ... we'll just call this room the bridge"): dev46's starship bridge is now a game room, `bridge` (`?room=bridge`, THE BRIDGE · CONTROL ROOM), reached from the main corridor through the doors that led to the control room: the stair hall's doorway is a pair of the standard equipment doors centred in its south wall, joined to the corridor's (walked through both ways, tested), then up the 8 m stair. It runs on the control room's supply (CR-SF1) and lighting way (14). The old control room is retired, not deleted: no longer reached from the corridor, its layout, machines and model kept and reachable by its URL (`?room=control_room`, CONTROL ROOM (RETIRED)), so it can be restored by pointing the corridor's door back at it. A shell room's runner can now take a room whose doorway is not on its own walls (its fly bounds, and doorway stops only below a height), tells a touch surface where it was touched, and has a console handle (`window.shellRoom.place`, `.use`) for pictures.
- The plant reimagined, dev48 (`?room=dev48`, mission 093; the owner, 2026-10-27: "ALL the game's existing control panels reinterpreted in this style"): each of the game's panels, boards and mimics as a 24th-century touch screen with the same controls, lamps and read-outs grandly renamed and the original it parodies in its footer, built from data (`logic/parody-specs.ts`) by one builder (`views/parody-panel.ts`) and run on a small trial of its own (`logic/parody-trial.ts`, tested: buttons read by their verbs, lamps by their words, a fault now and then to be reset, an annunciator that flashes until accepted); hung in two tiers round all four walls of a room sized to fit them (`logic/gallery-walls.ts`, tested). Now 69 of them, the whole inventory (recorded in mission 093): pumps and filtration (PL1-CP1 and PL2-CP1, the lounge and main pool mimics, the Wash-O-Tron, PL2-CP3, the UV controllers), chemicals and dosing (the dosing controller and pumps, WH-CP1, the transfer selectors and skids, the DoseCell heads, the day-tank station, the fill cabinet, the stores' and ozone gas detection, the OT-216 terminals), heating and gas (the boiler panels, gas safety, combustion panel, gas and heat output mimics, pressurisation), air (the AHU controllers and mimic), electrical (MSB-1, the main switch, changeover, central battery, lighting board, switch-fuses, isolators, generator point, metering), the control room (POOLMON, annunciator, printer, chart recorders, readout board, BMS outstation, fire repeater, CCTV), the spas (cabinets, dosing, valve panels, mimic, fibre illuminator, music, poolside plate), the wet hall (mimic, booster set, pit pumps, outfall and make-up recorders, flowmeter), the scenario simulator and the dev rooms' panels; each with 560 pixels a metre, a room holding seventy. Dev47's title card is now a small sign over its door (a room module may place its card, `FINISH.titleCard`), its east wall given two of the reinterpreted panels. A rounded button keeps pill proportions in segmented rows too.
- Touch panels in 3D (mission 093 stage 1, the owner, 2026-10-27): a generic touch surface (`industrial-control/{logic,views}/touch-surface.ts`) for any `ui2d` tree in any theme: a flat black backing frame of any outline (rectangle, rounded rectangle, trapezium, polygon, concave L, or a curved strip bent round the viewer) with one or more screens set into it inside a bezel (4 mm a side by default, 0 for none), the screens coplanar with the frame and drawn over it by render order and polygon offset, each repainted only when its tree changes; a use on a screen (the one control scheme's, which now tells a touch surface where it was touched: `controls/input/touch-point.ts`) goes to its tree as a tap at the touched pixel, from the hit's texture coordinates or its point in the frame's plane, and the tree's sounds play as the game's own touch bleeps (`sound/audio/touch-bleeps.ts`: confirm, deny, page, alert); lettering in Antonio (SIL OFL, credited). Its layout of screens in a frame, outline tests and hit mapping are tested. Dev47 (`?room=dev47`, DEV47 · TOUCH PANELS (24TH CENTURY)) tries it: nine touch surfaces round its walls and on its floor (a sidebar frame with pages, a rounded frame of three screens, a trapezium, a concave L, a bezel-less strip, a curved strip, a hexagon, a raked freestanding console and a lectern), each carrying elbows, bars, buttons, toggles, selectors, read-outs, gauges, trends, banners, lists and a line diagram under pool-flavoured nonsense, answering every touch. In a room with its own layout, a wall exhibit's caption now goes under it on its own wall.
- A 2D touch-panel widget kit, `src/ui2d/` (mission 093 stage 1, the owner, 2026-10-27): a small, framework-free component kit for the Canvas 2D API, kept apart from the game so it could be lifted out (it imports nothing from the game, Three.js or the toolkit): a component tree laid out in a pixel rectangle (`Stack`, `Place`, the sidebar `Frame`, `PageStack`), painted only when a setter has changed what it shows, taking presses in its own pixels and answering with an action and a sound for the host to play ('confirm', 'deny', 'page', 'alert'); elbows, bars, pill buttons (lit, pressed, disabled, flashing), toggles, segmented selectors whose choice may be refused (an interlock), labels, numeric read-outs, bar gauges, trends, alarm banners, lists, page buttons and a line diagram with moving flows and pressable symbols; one theme (`BLOCK_THEME`, colour blocks on black) among possible others. Its design language is the MIT libraries joernweissenborn/lcars (palette, proportions) and agent-P/LCARS-Javascript-Framework (the elbow's outline, button proportions, blink periods), credited in `CREDITS.md` and `src/ui2d/README.md` and recorded in `doc/reuse-inventory.md`; its layout, hit-testing, page and selector state and dirty flags tested.
- The control room's prototype in two versions on one geometry, from one builder (mission 092 stage 0, the owner, 2026-10-27): dev45 (`?room=dev45`) the Bond villain's lair, a ring of photo-flood lamps hung low over the desk, black lacquer, brushed steel and triangular coffers in the gloom; dev46 (`?room=dev46`) the starship bridge, soft cove light, carpet and beige panels, a full-height viewscreen, a flat sloping touch-panel station for each pool system (circulation and filtration, chemical dosing, heating, air handling, power, building) round the front of the desk, and a ramp either side of the stage's steps (about 1:7, steeper than Approved Document M's 1:12: a question for the owner). The geometry they share (first built as dev45): in at a door standing in for the corridor's, a switchback stair climbs a whole 8 m storey in its own stair hall (46 risers of 174 mm, goings of 250 mm, three flights of 16, 15 and 15 to Approved Document K, landings, handrails both sides, a balustrade at the open landing edge) and comes up, off centre, through a glass-guarded opening into a deliberately grandiose flattened heptagon, 28 × 22 m and 9 m high, facing its 24 m big wall. A segmented EPCOT-style heptagonal operator's desk (cream sloping tops, raked upstands with dark screen insets, charcoal fronts, a red chair) under an 8 m ring light after Dr. Strangelove's war room; at the back a raised stage 1.05 m up, six risers with handrails both sides and a wood-capped parapet in the bridge style, holding a 5 m conference table and ten chairs; a labelled placeholder for each era's console on every wall. Ken Adam's dark polished floor, faceted walls and coffered ceiling. Its geometry is pure and tested (`logic/control-room-prototype.ts`): symmetry, the stair's risers and flights, headroom, the desk's walkways, the climb from door to stage. Everything in it is set decoration or a placeholder, marked decorative.
- Floor markings round every bunded tank (prompt 089, item 3, the owner, 2026-10-27): a 50 mm yellow-and-black hazard stripe a short way out round each bund (the eight day tanks in the pool plant rooms and the four bulk tanks in the stores), the chemical's name stencilled on the floor before it (SODIUM HYPOCHLORITE in yellow, HYDROCHLORIC ACID in red, as their bunds' rims). Drawn from each tank's placement (`logic/bund-marking.ts`, `views/bund-marking.ts`), so it moves with it: a side comes in closer where something stands by it, is cut round a machine in its way, and is left out against a wall; it keeps off every other machine's floor and every kept-clear zone, painted a few millimetres proud of the floor. Tried first in dev44 ("Bund floor markings": a bottle in a corner and one by a skid).
- The changeover switch is labelled on embossed Dymo tape, as a box made up on site (the owner, 2026-10-27): its title, rating, positions and lamps on black and blue tape, ONE SUPPLY ONLY on red, only its DANGER 400 V a printed sticker; nothing too large for its door. Its handle is a proper changeover handle (`createChangeoverHandle`, after the Socomec SIRCOVER and ABB OT…C: a 90 mm square escutcheon, a round hub and a one-ended pistol-grip lever with a padlock hasp), not a small selector knob scaled up, tried first on dev0's new CHANGEOVER HANDLE board.
- WH-CP1's replacement on trial in the dev rooms (prompt 091, stage 3; the owner, 2026-10-27; not yet in the wet hall): two Penfold OT-216 operator terminals, hypochlorite (yellow band) and acid (red band), tagged PROTO-OT1 and PROTO-OT2 until the wet hall's model gives them their tags, each a light grey 200 × 140 × 50 mm case with a 16 × 2 STN LCD, RUN, CALL and ALARM LEDs, membrane keys (F1 MON, F2 SET, F3 CMD, ESC, ENTER, cursor keys in an inverted T), a LOCAL ACCESS key switch and its cable in conduit to the control box (PROTO-CB1): a 600 × 400 × 200 mm steel enclosure whose door opens on its latch, its door isolator on the front, inside an MCB that trips and resets, a 24 V supply, the controller (RUN, STOP, FAULT and an LED for every input and output), a comms module, ten interposing relays, field terminals and the wiring diagram in the door's pocket, all live. In dev44 beside today's WH-CP1, both driven by one trial of the two lines ten times fast, with a trial station for the selectors, pumps, RESET and the faults (wet hall power, MCB trip, controller hung, a terminal's link cut, PL4 high-high); the same in dev39 without WH-CP1; the character LCD and cursor keys on their own board in dev0. Without the wet hall's power the terminals go blank and nothing is transferred, as WH-CP1 goes dark.
- The switch room's fire safety (prompt 089, item 5, the owner, 2026-10-27): a fire point by its door, two 5 kg CO2 extinguishers (for live LV gear, BS 5306-8), the fire action notice and a FIRE POINT sign, its floor kept clear in a red outline; insulating rubber matting (BS EN 61111) along the main switchboard's front; and the metre's working space before the switchboard, the main switch and the changeover switch marked on the floor in yellow (`switch-room-floor.ts`). The fire point is now one reusable layout item (`fire-point:<kinds>`, `logic/fire-point.ts`, `views/fire-point.ts`), its kinds of extinguisher a parameter, placed by `layoutRoom` with its footprint the kept-clear floor; the wet hall's three fire points are the same item, no longer drawn by hand.
- Doors close by distance, not by timer (the owner, 2026-10-27): walk away from an opened door, beyond its 1.75 m release, and it closes almost at once (a 0.3 s hold) and briskly (its closer sweeping about 1.6 rad/s, eased, from wherever it is); stay near and it stays open. Doors whose rooms do not watch for walkers, and those of a room drawn under another, keep their ordinary 2.5 s hold.
- The chemical distribution's control box as a trial model (prompt 091, stage 2; `chem-control-trial.ts`, dev rooms only, not the world: the owner, 2026-10-27): one box for both lines (door isolator, MCB, a controller that starts a moment after it comes live and can hang until restarted); each day tank's own low and high levels in litres (today's 20% and 90% to begin with), STOP (inhibit: never served, a fill in progress ended), RESUME and FILL NOW, held by the box through a power cut; each selector in AUTO following the box and in HAND its own pick, box or no box; the transfer pump's AUTO on the box's remote-run relay; the hard-wired high-high trip and its RESET at the store; each terminal's link; every setting and command logged with the terminal's tag.
- The OT-216 operator terminal's menu (prompt 091, stage 1; the owner, 2026-10-27): Penfold Controls' 16 × 2 character terminal for the chemical distribution, as pure logic (`ot216-terminal.ts`) with a test of every key in every state: F1 MON, F2 SET, F3 CMD, ESC, ENTER and four cursor keys; MON at home follows the fill under way (`FILL 142L/300L`) or the line's summary, ◀ ▶ step through the line, its store and its day tanks, ENTER shows a tank's detail and acknowledges alarms, which break into the bottom line in turn; SET edits a tank's low and high levels in litres within their limits (`LIMIT`); CMD fills now, stops (inhibits) or resumes a tank after `CONFIRM? ENT/ESC`; the LOCAL ACCESS key locks SET and CMD; a minute without a key goes home and dims; power-up self test (`PENFOLD OT-216` / `V2.04 SELF TEST`) and `NO COMMS`; RUN, CALL and ALARM LEDs.
- A mains/generator changeover switch in the switch room (prompt 089, item 6, the owner, 2026-10-27): a Voltara 400 A changeover after the Socomec SIRCOVER and ABB OT…C, in a Penfold enclosure on the east wall between the main incoming switch and the switchboard, its big rotary handle I (mains) · 0 (off) · II (generator), one position a turn so it always passes 0 (break before make; mains and generator never paralleled), with MAINS AVAILABLE and GENERATOR AVAILABLE lamps. In 0 the switchboard, and every room it feeds, is dead; in II too, until a hired generator is modelled (TODO). It is in the switch room's model (the main switch to it, it to the switchboard, the loading bay's generator cable on its II side), in the world (`plant.changeover`, world version 46), local to the switch room, and shown in the electrical gallery. The switch room's own generator connection point, a duplicate of the loading bay's cabinet, is removed (asset-0070 SR-FX1 retired).
- Doors stay open while you are near them (prompt 090, the owner, 2026-10-27): an opened plant room door, pair of equipment doors, ladder gate or development-room spring door now waits while the player stands within 1.5 m of its doorway (released beyond 1.75 m, so standing on the edge does not set it chattering), measured from the body's edge to the opening (`door-proximity.ts`), whatever the player looks at or presses. Presence is told to each door before it moves, so it can no longer take a last closing step and latch beside the player; a door already closing opens again smoothly from where it is, a shut door stays shut, and the usual hold and closing start only once the player has left. Only the room the player is in counts them: a room drawn under another, its walker left behind, holds nothing open. A door handed over through its doorway now waits a full hold rather than starting to close at once. The doors' narrow collision and threshold areas are unchanged.
- The chemical distribution needs its power: each transfer pump its store's supply, the selectors' motorised valves and the distribution panel the wet hall's (DB-WET HALL, and the wet hall's own switch-fuse WH-SF1). With either off, nothing is transferred and the day tanks run down; with the wet hall's off, the panel goes dark. The day tanks' heads follow their plant room's supply.
- The loading bay has one delivery fill cabinet, by the tanker's (left) bay on the acid store's wall: its door now opens and shuts, and its two couplings (2" hypochlorite, 1½" acid) pipe away, the acid straight back through the wall into its store and the hypochlorite up out of the top and across the bay over the shutters into its store. The generator connection cabinet takes the other cabinet's place by the right bay, cabled to the switch room in the model. Both stores' mains now cross the bay to the corridor, double-contained and marked.
- The ring corridor carries its services as decoration (prompt 088): the heating flow and return ring (DN80, lagged), the boosted make-up ring, the backwash main from the Lounge's and the Olympic's plant rooms, a cold main and the hot water lines at the boiler house, and the chemical mains and the eight branches, double-contained, each room teed off beside its doors, fire-stopped at every wall and marked to BS 1710 with its flow, all on the wet hall's side and below the cable ladder.
- The Olympic's day tank branches and supply lines are drawn as their chemicals (they were drawn as pool water, so unmarked and not double-contained); a test now checks every room draws every chemical link of its schematic, each as its own chemical.
- Chemical pipework marked and contained (the owner, 2026-10-27): every routed chemical line carries BS 1710 markers (violet, hazard band, legend with pictograms, a flow arrow along its run's direction of flow) at the middle of each straight of 0.8 m or more and every 3 m along longer ones; the transfer mains, branches and fills between rooms are double-contained, an outer PVC-U pipe a size up over the carrier, and marked DOUBLE CONTAINED, as are the dosing lines' carriers.
- The plant rooms plumbed for their day tanks (`day-tank-plumbing.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-27): in the footbath, the jacuzzi and the lounge each chemical's branch from the wet hall's selector drops straight from the services ceiling into its dose bottle's fill, and each bottle's dosing tube leaves its DoseCell head's discharge gland, clears the bottle, rises to its own height above every head and drops to what doses with it (the spa cabinets' new dosing inlets on their tops, the lounge's dosing pumps), the tube in a PVC-U carrier; the lounge's hand-drawn ceiling drops (and their acid wrongly marked hypochlorite) replaced. In the Olympic, each day tank's branch drops from the ceiling into its fill and its draw-off feeds its dosing pump: the hypochlorite along the south wall, the acid, standing out in the room, overhead; the day tanks are no longer counted as open floor. The bottles, the framed 1,000 l station and the cabinets have ports for it.
- The chemical stores plumbed (`store-plumbing.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-27): each bulk tank's fill in from its own sleeve high in the south wall and down to its fill valve; each tank's draw-off in a covered trench in the floor (each in its own lane and depth) up into its own tee on the transfer pump's suction header; the pump's discharge straight up off its skid and out high through the south wall, on to the wet hall. The tanks and the skid have ports for the router. The walking and reach checks no longer ask to stand in front of a sign.
- The chemical distribution plumbed in the wet hall (the owner, 2026-10-27): its plumber now routes the schematic's chemical links as well as its water, in PVC-U DN25 banded by chemical, each main in from its store through the east wall at high level and dropping into its selector's header, each selector outlet's riser up and across to the north wall, out to its plant room beside the make-up main; the selector has ports for the router (`machine-ports.ts`); the walkway strip south of the pit stops short of the selector, whose risers rise there. Some branches take long ways round, to be tidied.
- After the in-game test (the owner, 2026-10-27): the distribution panel reads its store and day tanks on three-digit LED readouts (%) in place of moving-coil meters; its lamps are modern LED pilot lights, plainly lit or dark, their legends short enough to read (SEL AUTO, SEL HAND, PUMP RUN, PUMP AUTO, HH TRIP); ACCEPT, RESET and LAMP TEST are labelled beneath. A day tank's fill stops at its high switch however long the step, so a small bottle is never overfilled into its high-high trip (a test catches it); chlorine undosed is used up in about four hours, not 80 minutes; the lounge's acid bottle is 60 l (about two weeks), not 200. In the chemical stores the sign board and title card hang high, above the tanks, and the acid's sign reads 10% and WARNING (it said 32–33% and DANGER); the transfer pump station's maker's mark moved clear of its title.
- Tools for trying the world in the browser (development only): `?slot=<name>` gives the game a save slot of its own (`?slot=itest`), apart from the player's and from any other tab holding theirs; a distribution action `{ act: 'set', chemical, outlet, share }` sets a day tank's contents (from the console's `act`), logged as such rather than as a trip reset. Used to run the chemical distribution's run-dry and recovery in the game itself (2026-10-27).
- The chemical distribution has an end-to-end test on the whole world (`chemical-distribution.integration.test.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-27): the Olympic's hypochlorite and the lounge's acid day tanks nearly empty with both transfer pumps OFF run dry; those two pools, and only those, stop dosing, and the Olympic's chlorine falls; both pumps back to AUTO, the empty tanks call, the selectors fill them and the Olympic's chlorine recovers.
- The wet hall's transfer selectors finished (the owner, 2026-10-27): a pipe marker on each header (flow from its feed) and on each outlet's riser (flow up to its plant room), each riser saying on mouse-over where it goes and whether it is filling; the floating outlet names, header title and SPARE tags gone (a spare's blind flange says what it is). Their valves were already the working components. The foul sewer drop near them was checked: clear of both.
- The plant rooms' day tanks turned for their plumbing (the owner, 2026-10-27): each with its back (fill socket and power lead) to its nearest wall, where the mains and cables run, and its front (the DoseCell's display) into the room, keeping the walk to the front of every machine clear; none under a stair or landing. The lounge's two and the jacuzzi's and footbath's hypochlorite and the footbath's acid turned; the Olympic's two already faced so.
- Dev44 replumbed (the owner, 2026-10-27): every port carries on to where it goes. Each dose bottle's fill socket rises in rigid ABS (banded by chemical) and runs back at high level to the selector; its dosing tube runs in soft PE along the floor to the injection point; its power lead to the floor trunking. The peristaltic pump's suction and discharge tube, the DoseCell heads' tubes and leads, the trial stations' pump discharges, and the bulk tanks' fill lines (marked) all run on.
- The chemical models built from their components (the owner, 2026-10-27): the transfer skid's suction and discharge valves are working ball valves, its non-return valve a ball check valve that flows while the pump runs, its relief a relief valve, its mains marked with pipe markers (floating NRV, PRV and product labels gone); the selector's outlets each a working isolating ball valve and a motorised valve the selector opens; the day tank's inlet a motorised valve opened while it fills. Every component says what it is and how it stands on mouse-over (`describe.ts`): valves, pumps, the DoseCell head, transmitters and indicators, leak detection, pipe markers.
- The dosing valves, each working (`dosing-valves.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-27): a motorised DN15 PVC-U ball valve (after GF type 107: position dial, OPEN and SHUT lamps, hand override, a quarter-turn stroke to its limit switches); a DN10 PVDF solenoid valve (its DIN plug's LED lit while energised, a relay click); a multifunction valve (relief back to the tank, anti-siphon, a priming knob that bleeds the head); an injection valve that scales up over time and opens only at dosing pressure (click to descale); a back-pressure valve set at 1.5, 3 or 5 bar; a foot valve and strainer in a clear section of bottle; and a three-way valve sending a pump's suction from its tank or its calibration column. On one bench in dev44; not yet placed in the plant rooms.
- The peristaltic pump and the DoseCell head are clamshells of cast polycarbonate (the owner, 2026-10-27): front and back halves with rounded edges and a seam between them (on the DoseCell a gasket in its chemical's colour, replacing the band across its face), each fascia sized to sit within the flat of its front. The peristaltic pump's sound is a low geared-servo hum, not a whine. Plinth columns no longer overlap their caps.
- Level instruments as components (`level-instruments.ts`): the ultrasonic and 80 GHz radar transmitters and the Kessler-Moody LI-5 field indicator, taken out of the framed tank (which now uses them) and shown on a plinth in dev44 reading a rising and falling level; the indicator's face tidied and its floating tag gone (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Chemical isolation valves (`chemical-ball-valve.ts`): PVC-U true-union ball valves after GF type 546, DN15 and DN40, grey bodies with union nuts and red quarter-turn handles that turn when clicked (along the pipe open, across it shut). They replace the drawn boxes as the framed tanks' fill and outlet valves, and stand on a plinth in dev44. Their state is their own until the chemical lines' valves join the plant model (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Dosing pump sounds (`audio/dosing-pump-sounds.ts`, synthesised, held while each runs): the DoseCell's submerged pump, a 50 Hz hum and its harmonics dulled through liquid and plastic with a soft diaphragm thump twice a second; the peristaltic pump's geared-motor whine under the rollers' rubbery squeeze. The leak monitor's sounder buzzes while a leak is unsilenced (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Leak detection modelled (researched 2026-10-27): a double-contained line's low point (`leak-detection.ts`: a containment tee, a 1/2" drain ball valve whose red lever turns, a clear sight tube showing what has collected, a conductivity probe after the Raychem TT-MINI-PROBE with its gland and lead) and a four-zone monitor after GF's 918Q (POWER, ZONE 1–4 LEAK, FAULT, sounder grille; TEST, SILENCE and RESET that work). In dev44 a seep wets the probe every few minutes and zone 1 latches; drain it and RESET. Nothing in the plant leaks yet, so its state is its own; the pipe samples' blue box is now a (dry) low point.
- The DoseCell head in Chlorella's livery (the owner, 2026-10-27): a green surround on a dark panel; a 96 × 40 dot graphic LCD (pale green dots on dark green) of three pages, contents (litres, percentage, bar), switches (HIGH, LOW, calling) and pump; HI, LO, CALL and PUMP lamps; ◄ ► and TEST keys that work, on every bottle and on the plinths. Leak detection's logic (`game/plant/leak-monitor.ts`, tested): a low point that a seep wets and a drain empties, and a four-zone monitor that latches, silences, resets once dry, shows a broken lead as a fault and lamp-tests.
- The peristaltic pump's screen is a 120 × 72 dot graphic LCD (`dot-matrix.ts`, tested): light blue dots on dark blue, every dot visible, the controller's 5 × 7 font, its rate at triple size, an inverted title bar, a flow bar and a turning rotor glyph (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Chemical pipe markers (`pipe-marker.ts`, after BS 1710 and the Safety Signs and Signals Regulations as researched 2026-10-27): round the pipe a violet band (acids and alkalis), a yellow and black hazard band, a white legend (the substance and strength, CLP pictograms, DO NOT MIX – CHLORINE GAS, DOUBLE CONTAINED where so, a black arrow with the flow) and another violet band, sized to the pipe. Tried on dev44's pipe samples in place of their old bands; next, on the real lines.
- A peristaltic dosing pump, Grundflow's PERI 30 after the Watson-Marlow Qdos (the owner, 2026-10-27): a roller head under a clear guard turning while it runs, its tube clamps in Grundflow orange; a colour TFT (light blue on dark blue) of its rate, mode, a flow bar and a turning rotor glyph; START, STOP, ▲ and ▼ membrane keys that work; RUN and ALARM lamps; the wordmark. It replaces the bland box on the day tank stations and stands on a plinth in dev44. The dev rooms' console handle `exhibitRoom.exhibits()` lists where each exhibit stands.
- Dosing pumps are fed by soft hose, not rigid pipe (`hose.ts`: a sagging tube through its points), on the day tank stations and the dose bottles' discharge; the DoseCell head's display, lamps and legends shrunk to sit within its front face (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Dev44 holds only the chemical distribution's worked-out components (the owner, 2026-10-27): both framed bulk tanks, both transfer skids, the DoseCell heads, every day tank size, the transfer trial, the distribution panel (run by its own trials) and the pipe samples. The old delivery rigs and their oversized boards, the old day tank stations and the store dressing are gone (`chemical-delivery-rig.ts` deleted; its delivery logic kept for prompt 076).
- Dev44 is a square room, its exhibits round all four walls, with the DoseCell head on plinths (hypochlorite and acid) to be tried alone. The DoseCell's display is a 16 × 2 character LCD: its tag and litres, and a bar of ten blocks and its percentage, as a 1980s module would draw it. The 1,000 l framed tank and its station lose their floating labels (fill, vent filter, LT, LSHH, outlet, LSL/LSH, TO DOSING); hover says what each is (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- Controls made sensible (the owner, 2026-10-27): the transfer pump's station loses its duplicate selector HAND · AUTO switch (the selector's own station works it), keeping HAND · OFF · AUTO, RUN, TRIP and TRIP RESET; the selector's outlet buttons are named for their plant rooms (PL1 to PL4, SPARE); the distribution panel's lamps say whose they are (SELECTOR AUTO/HAND, PUMP RUN/IN AUTO, HIGH-HIGH TRIP) and its day tank alarm is LOW LOW (10%), below the call point, not every routine call. The dose bottle's labels are wrapped on its body, its plastic glossy enough to show its shape, its lamps smaller, its lid no longer z-fighting; DOSECELL on its front, Chlorella's plate on its side.
- Every plant room's day tank below 1,000 l is now a dose bottle, bound to the world's distribution (its litres, its call; its pump lamp lit while it is drawn down); the Olympic's 1,000 l hypochlorite keeps its framed tank. Each bottle stands on a square spill pallet whose sump holds 110% of it (BUND n L on its front), its rim and its head banded in its chemical's colour (hypochlorite yellow, acid red). Chemical labels follow CLP for their strength: hypochlorite 14% DANGER (burns, aquatic toxicity, EUH031); hydrochloric acid 10% WARNING (skin, eye and respiratory irritant, corrosive to metals), no longer "severe burns". Chlorella supplies every specialist chemical handling component: its plate now on the transfer skids and selectors too (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The dose bottle (the owner, 2026-10-27): an idealised, self-contained day tank, Chlorella's DoseCell, for every size below 1,000 l. A round HDPE bottle in its spill tray with one powered head on its lid: ultrasonic level, high and low switches, its own dosing pump, and a small LCD of its litres with HI, LO, CALL and PUMP lamps; a fill socket for the transfer line, a vented cap (through a carbon cartridge on acid) and its power cable. Modelled (`game/plant/dose-bottle.ts`, tested) and shown in dev44's day tank row, each running on an ideal line; not yet placed in the plant rooms.
- Dev44 shows every day tank size in a row, smallest to largest (15, 30, 60, 200, 300, 500 and 1,000 l), each labelled with the pools it serves (the owner, 2026-10-27).
- The chemical distribution (plan 085, prompts 085 and 086; the owner, 2026-10-26/27), in the model and placed in every room: each store's framed 4,000 l bulk tanks (black HDPE in a galvanised lattice frame, after DENIOS's, with ultrasonic or radar level, display, high-high switch, fill and outlet; the acid's IBC retired, 10% hydrochloric acid now) and its single magnetic-drive transfer pump; the wet hall's two six-way transfer selectors on its dirty side (four live outlets, two blanked for the pools to come; AUTO serves the calling day tanks in turn, HAND the pick); and each plant room's two day tanks in their bunds, sized to its pool (the Olympic's 1,000 l down to the footbath's 15 l), each pool now dosing from its own. High-high trips a line until reset; a low store cuts its pump out. Every tank shows its contents and says how full it is and whether it fills or drains. The stores enlarged to hold them (6 × 9 m, 6 × 6 m). `WORLD_VERSION` 45; a new game starts with the Olympic's hypochlorite and the lounge's acid calling.
- The wet hall's chemical distribution panel (plan 085), Penfold's, on its south wall between the pit and the selectors: each store's contents and its four day tanks' levels on moving-coil meters, a FILLING lamp for each outlet served, each line's AUTO, HAND, RUN, IN AUTO and TRIP, and an annunciator (day tank low, line tripped, store low, pump not in AUTO) with ACCEPT, RESET and LAMP TEST. It shows; the pumps' selectors and trip resets stay at the stores.
- Dev44, the chemical handling room (prompt 086): the delivery rigs, the framed bulk and day tanks, the transfer pump (and a duty and standby set kept for later), the six-way selector, the chemical pipes (double-contained ABS, solvent-welded ABS, PE dosing tube), and a trial from an ideal source through pump and selector to four day tanks, on tested logic.

- A screenshot shortcut (prompt 084): Ctrl+Shift+K, or Cmd+Shift+K on a Mac, copies a best-quality PNG of the 3D view with no interface over it, on the title screen too. It is drawn again at full resolution whatever the graphics quality, at twice the screen's pixel ratio and at most 3840 px wide, within the GPU's limits and a 16-megapixel budget, without the game moving on, then the normal rendering is put back. Where the browser refuses the clipboard it downloads the same PNG as `pool-plant-<room>-<date-time>.png`, with a Save button in case the download was held back. A notice says what happened; Ctrl+Alt+S / Cmd+Option+S and the ▣ button still work.
- Researched the best-quality clean screenshot extension (prompt 084), documenting the existing PNG capture, title-scene integration, gesture-safe clipboard delivery, bounded sizing and download fallback.

- The site's utility meters remodelled after real UK commercial designs (`utility-meters.ts`), with dev43 exhibits whose registers count up: a CT-operated three-phase meter (after the Landis+Gyr E650 and Elster A1700) scrolling kWh, kW and time, its pulse LEDs flashing with load, in a GRP metering cabinet with test terminal block, 4G modem and sealed CT chamber; a DN80 gas meter installation in yellow pipe (ECV, filter, slam-shut regulator with relief vent, a G100 rotary meter after the Elster RVG, a volume converter after the EK205, outlet valve); and a DN100 water meter set (gate valves, Y-strainer, an ultrasonic meter after the Kamstrup flowIQ 3100, dismantling joint).
- The site's intake in the goods yard (prompt 083): the water main with its stop tap and revenue meter, the gas main with its emergency control valve and meter in a ventilated housing, and the electricity supply's cut-out, meter and customer-side isolator, each on the building's outside wall left of the dock, rising from the ground into the wall through a sealed sleeve, behind yellow hatching and bollards. Each lever shuts its service off to the whole site, independent of the building's own switches; the meters count water, gas (from the boilers' heat) and electricity (from a site load model). `WORLD_VERSION` 44; older saves start a new world.
- Copy a clean PNG of the current game view with Ctrl+Alt+S on PC, Cmd+Option+S on Mac, or the view-tools button. Captures render temporarily at higher resolution without HUD overlays.
- Dev43, the loading dock's gallery (the owner, 2026-10-26): an insulated roller shutter worked from its controller (OPEN · STOP · CLOSE, key switch), its dock light green only when fully open; the dock traffic light, leveller, shelter and buffer; wheel chocks and guide; edge posts and chain; a steel bollard, a convex mirror, a pedestrian barrier; a real UK pallet, a stack of empties, a pallet of goods; a hand pallet truck, a small counterbalance forklift, a roll cage, a sack truck; a drum on a spill pallet; the chemical fill cabinet (incompatible acid and hypochlorite inlets) and the generator connection cabinet, their doors opening. All rough, to refine.
- The loading dock rebuilt (prompt 073, in progress): 14.3 m wide, two drive-in lorry channels sunk to the yard's level and running 6 m in, the lorries' flatbeds level with the floor around them; rolled-up roller shutters, a pier door with its lintel, an overcast sky and tarmac outside (the tarmac carried into the channels); after HSE and loading-bay guidance, non-slip edges and yellow lines, posts and chains along the drops, wheel guides and chocks, red and green dock lights, vehicle, edge and PPE signs; two fire points with working call points and a sounder-beacon; the law poster and the chemical notices; pallets of deliveries. The tanker stand is gone from the model.
- Ctrl+Shift+N turns noclip on and off in a room, as the pause menu's switch does; listed in the controls help (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- Heavy pipes (DN150 and up) sit on hardwood bearer blocks on their trapezes and floor stands, in place of a steel saddle post, painted with a weathered oak grain (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The Lidoluxe Wash-O-Tron console on the Olympic plant room's east wall, beside the local control panel, its conduits running into that panel's side (prompt 072): a mimic band of the five filters with a lamp on each valve; under each filter its illuminated FILTER, BACKWASH, RINSE, ISOLATE, DRAIN, AUTO and LOCKOUT buttons, a blanked AIR SCOUR (NOT FITTED) and a FAULT lamp; a 20 × 2 LCD with MODE, −, +, ENTER and PROGRAMME; AUTO, HOLD, STEP, SKIP and WASH NOW; an annunciator with ACCEPT and RESET; Penfold's mark, Lidoluxe's badge, and a whimsical aqua fascia of bubble decals. Its buttons drive the world's valves, which the room's actuators stroke.
- The Lidoluxe Wash-O-Tron's logic in the world (prompt 072): each Olympic filter's state set by button (FILTER, BACKWASH, RINSE, ISOLATE, DRAIN), its old path shut, its new opened and proved by flow; LOCKOUT; in AUTO a programme washing due filters one at a time (BACKWASH → SETTLE → RINSE → FILTER on timers that start once proved), never with fewer than four left filtering, waiting for room in the pit (PIT CAPACITY) and while it is inhibited; a valve worked by hand mid-wash holds it with VALVE FAULT; HOLD, STEP, SKIP, WASH NOW; setpoints editable after PROGRAMME, stored by ENTER; air scour provided for, not fitted. `WORLD_VERSION` 43. The console comes next.
- The backwash pit keeps its sewer consent (Codex's review, 2026-10-26): its two pumps are interlocked, one at a time, even both in HAND; and its discharge valve (valve-0034, WH-DR1-DV1) is the pumps' isolating valve, open in service, no longer an impossible gravity drain adding 40 m³/h from 3.75 m down. `WORLD_VERSION` 42 opens it in saved games.
- The plant rooms' valves travel and are heard again (the owner, 2026-10-26): the room bound every valve's position to the world's open or shut, so an actuated valve snapped and never ran its motor; only the lounge's valves, whose travel the world models, take their position from it now, and every other valve strokes itself at its actuator's speed with its motor's sound and its stop.
- A sight glass's reflection of its room is soft, a small capture stretched over the glass (cheaper too), and its internal reflection fainter (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- A sight glass's reflection of its room is plainer, and its thick glass shows a faint internal reflection: a soft, dim arc just inside the bottom of its rim, opposite its overhead highlight (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- A sight glass reflects its real room (the owner, 2026-10-26): each glass captures the room once from just in front of itself (`room-reflection.ts`, a cube camera, taken between frames once the room is built), so its domed pane shows the room's real lights, walls and plant at their real distances, added over the bed behind it, with no point light's spot on it; its rounded, smooth edge catches the room's lights in a small crescent on the side toward them.
- A sight glass is real glass (the owner, 2026-10-26): no reflection painted into its picture; a clear, very smooth domed pane in front of it, 12 mm proud at its middle, so a light's highlight lies within the glass on the side toward it, and a rounded bevelled edge of the same glass, lit by the room, so its highlights move as one moves; what is behind it matte.
- Every sand filter's sight glass shows its dirt plainly (the owner, 2026-10-26: clean and dirty beds looked the same): a full load is a thick dark crust, more a near-black one, a clean bed none (the Olympic's, the lounge's, the spas' small filters and the ideal filter, all through the one glass). The Olympic's glasses also show a bed left unsettled after a backwash: rinsing while water runs down through it, the water over it clouded.
- Human access ladders now use one helper for their geometry, collision and climb zones. Pool, tank, pit, caged and utility ladders can be climbed at both ends; gates and hatches control access, and the tank and pit have walkable lower floors. Cable ladders remain cable containment.
- The backwash pit's water moves (the owner, 2026-10-26: as the main balance tank's): the backwash pours from its inlet's open end down through the gratings into the pit, heard as it pours, and the pit's surface is stirred white where it lands, calm further off (`backwash-pit-water.ts`); the world keeps the pit's inflow for it (`pitInflow`, `WORLD_VERSION` 41).
- The backwash pit's pump panel shows its water (the owner, 2026-10-26): a backlit display of its depth over its floor and how full it is, and a lamp for each float switch (STOP, START, HIGH, HI-HI), lit while tilted up. The pit's east rail has no post standing in its gate's opening (left there when the gate moved), and the foul sewer's gully is centred on the pipe that drops into it.
- Prompt 071's researched plan for human access ladders: one visual/collision/climb helper, real tank and pit exits, and an explicit distinction from cable ladders and handrails.
- The backwash pit's DANGER sign on its east rail hangs just south of its gate, to its left from the walkway, not where the gate now is (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The backwash pit's hatch moves to its north-east corner, toward the aisle, beyond its float switches and clear of its pumps (the owner, 2026-10-26), hinged on its north edge; its ladder, its gate and its no-pipes zone move with it, its pump panel south along the rail clear of the gate. Its pumped discharge to the sewer is 110 mm (76 mm would block), its risers and header 100 mm; the discharge's line keeps out of the hatch's sweep and runs above head height over the walkways round the pit. The davit's knee brace, left floating in the air when the davit moved, is back under its arm.
- The backwash pit's hatchway is clear (the owner, 2026-10-26): its sump and pump station move west, toward the ramp, out from under the hatch; the pumps' guide rails hang from a steel cross-beam spanning the pit under the gratings, never the gratings; the pumps lift out through a removable lifting panel over them, marked in yellow, by the davit, now on the pit's south side reaching north over it; their risers rise through a fixed service panel beside it, holes cut and collared for them; their power cables still run down the south wall, nowhere near the hatch.
- The wet hall's booster set has dev42's controller and changeover (the owner, 2026-10-26): DUTY 1 and DUTY 2, RUN, STOP and BOOST work the world's set (`wetHall.booster`, `WORLD_VERSION` 40); the pools' make-up demand calls it through its 60 s run-on, its duty pump resting between calls with its valves left open, only STOP shutting them; no make-up is given unless its pumps deliver. Each pump is heard as it runs up and coasts. Steel grating now shows from below too, from under a gantry or inside the pit.
- The pit pumps' power cables go to the pit's wall as soon as they can (the owner, 2026-10-26): from the panel's post through a floor duct round to the pit's south wall, in under the slab and down the wall's face in cleats, clear of the ladder and the hatch, across to each motor.
- The pit pumps' panel stands on its post properly (the owner, 2026-10-26): the post behind the panel, from the floor to its middle, on a base plate with four anchor bolts, a bolted bracket between its top and the panel's back; the pumps' cables drop beside it.
- The backwash pit's sump pumps have a control panel and power (the owner, 2026-10-26): a Penfold panel on a post outside the rail north of the gate, each pump's HAND · OFF · AUTO selector and RUN lamp, DUTY 1 and DUTY 2 (the duty's lit) and a HIGH LEVEL lamp; in the world (`pitPumps`, `WORLD_VERSION` 39) AUTO runs the duty pump on the floats, the standby taking the call when the duty pump is not in AUTO, HAND runs a pump whatever the floats, and the duty passes to the other pump after each pump-down; each running pump puts its rate down the sewer. Each pump's power cable runs from the panel down its post through a floor duct, out under the gratings and down beside its guide rails. The pit's discharge isolator is turned west, clear of the hatch's lid.
- The backwash pit is emptied by a pump station at its bottom (the owner, 2026-10-26: an open pipe from above could never lift the water): a duty and standby pair of submersible pumps in its sump, each docked on a duckfoot bend and held on twin guide rails up to the hatch frame for the davit to lift it out; each riser climbs out of the sump, runs under the gratings clear of the hatch and rises through them with a swing non-return valve whose arm lifts while its pump pushes; the two join in a header over the floor whose outlet is the pit's `discharge` port, where the plumber's line to the sewer and its isolating valve begin. The duty pump runs, and is heard, while the pit discharges.
- The wet hall's booster set no longer flaps on and off with the pools' float valves (the owner, 2026-10-26): any draw starts it, and it runs on for 60 s after the last (`BOOSTER_RUN_ON`), as its pressure vessel and controller would; a trip or loss of power stops it at once. `WORLD_VERSION` 38.
- The wet hall's booster set plumbed in afresh (the owner, 2026-10-26): its discharge leaves level far enough for its motorised valve to sit on the straight, carried by a floor stand, before it turns up (it had sat on the riser, held by a bracket hung in the air off the set's envelope; the support planner now sees a skid's base frame alone as something to fix to); and it is wired to the room's control bus from its control port, the gland on top of its control unit, straight up and across to the bus, keeping its own power feed.
- The booster set holds a constant discharge pressure (the owner, 2026-10-26; its CRE pumps are variable speed): 4 bar on one pump or two, whatever the suction, BOOST adding flow and not pressure; the pressure still builds and falls as pumps run up and coast. Tested in dev42 through stop, start and boost: suction 3.0 bar at rest, 2.5 on one pump, 2.2 boosting; flow 6.2 and 10.5 m³/h.
- Dev42's suction gauge reads its ideal source, the only thing feeding it (the owner, 2026-10-26): the source's 3 bar less what its resistance takes at the set's flow; the source is rated for the set at 20 m³/h, since at its standard 5 m³/h it could not feed even one pump.
- Dev42's booster set's discharge gauge follows its pumps (the owner, 2026-10-26): each pump's head as the square of its speed through its valves, a little more with both in parallel while boosting, and none once stopped, nothing holding it into the rig's open sink (`changeoverPressure`).
- Dev42's booster set has a Kessler-Moody electromagnetic flowmeter (FT-1) in its discharge's run overhead to the sink, reading the set's delivery: one pump's, none while stopped, about 1.7 times one while boosting (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The booster set's network gland is a gland only, no cable drawn, and marks its control port (`status`, `BOOSTER_CONTROL`) in the model, where the room's control wiring is to be drawn in (the owner, 2026-10-26); machine ports may now be `control`, which the port-connection check leaves to the control wiring.
- The booster set's motorised valves are each cabled to its control unit (the owner, 2026-10-26): a thinner cable from each actuator's tail, laid in square runs, into its own small gland under the unit (an actuated valve now marks where its cable's tail ends, `cableTail`); the unit's network gland moves to its top, its cable up to the room's control bus, and the CONTROL NETWORK sign is gone.
- The booster set's pumps' rating plates sit on their terminal boxes' flat lids, sized to them, not on the round stage sleeves; each terminal box's control cable runs from its gland down and along into a gland under the control unit, and the unit has a larger gland of its own, marked CONTROL NETWORK, its cable down to the floor (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- Every character LCD's glass is drawn in front of its own bezel by polygon offset (the owner, 2026-10-26), so it never flickers against it at any distance: fixed once, in `createCharacterLcd`, for every display that uses it.
- Every ideal source's and sink's display stands 2 mm proud of its bezel, not 1, so it no longer flickers against it (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The booster set's STOP shuts its valves (the owner, 2026-10-26): the duty pump coasts to rest, then its valves (and a boosting assist pump's) drive shut; RUN opens them first, then runs it up. No valve shuts on a pump still turning, nor a pump turns against shut valves.
- The booster set's BOOST (the owner, 2026-10-26): an illuminated button on its controller, lit while on, pressed again to cancel; it runs the standby beside the duty pump, its valves open first, the two in parallel giving about 1.7 times one pump's flow, not twice, against the pipework's resistance; cancelled, STOP or a change of duty, the assist pump coasts to rest and then its valves shut. Both pumps sound as if working harder while boosting: a pump's sound may now follow a load, its motor slipping a little lower and its water and casing louder. Its terminal boxes have a screwed lid, louvres and a cable gland; its pressure vessel is lettered round with its rating (24 L, PN10, pre-charge 2.5 bar).
- The booster set in Grundflow's blue, orange and grey (the owner, 2026-10-26): blue motors with orange terminal boxes, grey pump ends, bases and fan cowls; the diaphragm vessel a rounded pressure vessel, domed both ends, blue with an orange band; Grundflow's mark wrapped round the motors and the vessel, not stuck on as flat signs; each fan cowl's top a bolted plate, worn a little, the impeller mark on it. Its pressure gauges sit on what they measure, the suction and discharge manifolds, not on its control panel; dev42's discharge pipe's bends as round as its suction's (a pipe may now set its bend radius).
- Dev42 uses the standard ideal source and sink (`experimental-source.ts`), not drums of its own (the owner, 2026-10-26): mains water, endless, drawn the one way with their lettering and readouts, piped from their top studs to the set's manifolds; the set's delivery drawn from the one into the other.
- Dev42, the booster set modelled (the owner, 2026-10-26): the Grundflow twin-pump set between an ideal clean water source and an ideal sink, its duty changeover a pure model (`booster-changeover.ts`). DUTY 1 and DUTY 2 on its controller choose the duty pump: the running pump coasts to rest, its valves held open until then, then drive shut; the other pump's valves drive open and it runs up. So only the pump running ever has its valves open, and nothing can run back through the standby (tested at every step). RUN and STOP start and stop the duty pump. Its motorised valves are heard driving, its motors running up and coasting down, and its display shows the step and the flow.
- The booster set's own valves, each pump's suction and discharge isolators, are motorised butterfly valves driven by its controller (the owner, 2026-10-26), no longer lever valves for show: open while the set is powered and in service, shut on a low-water trip or with its power off, opened again by RESET.
- The booster set's suction and discharge valves (valve-0032 WH-T1-IV1, valve-0033 WH-MU1-IV2) are motorised and driven from the wet hall's control panel (the owner, 2026-10-26): each drawn as a motorised valve, and on the mimic each side of the booster set with its OPEN and SHUT buttons and lamps; while either is shut the booster set gives no make-up (`booster-valves.test.ts`). The mimic now draws the meter on the main before the break tank, as built.
- The utility's water meter works (the owner, 2026-10-26): its register counts the water let in from the town main past the break tank's float valve (it had counted the booster set's draw), on six black drums of cubic metres and three red of litres, each digit in its own window, its dial's corners its body's blue; hovering over it gives its reading in the information overlay.
- The town main comes in at the meter line's height, just west of its meter, and turns once straight into it (the owner, 2026-10-26: no dogleg): the wet hall's plumber joins two level ports facing at right angles by one elbow, by rule; its stop valve on the leg out of the wall, facing along it.
- The wet hall's pipework tidied (the owner, 2026-10-26): ports facing each other on one line are joined by a straight spool, not routed (the meter to the flowmeter had looped up and down; the tank's outlet to the booster set likewise straight), their valves worked crouching; the break tank's drain leaves its east end, the booster set's side, low, falling straight to its gully moved into the gap before the booster; the plumber's butterfly valves face along their level pipes, away from their machines, as the valve drawing expects.
- The wet hall is plumbed by its plumber from its schematic (prompt 036; the owner, 2026-10-26), after the jacuzzi's: `wet-hall-plumbing.ts` routes each of its seven water links with the clash-free voxel router round its machines, the make-up bay's partition, the ramp and the stair. That is the town main from its north-wall stub through the utility's meter and our flowmeter, down through the break tank's roof; the tank to the booster set; the boosted make-up main out to its north-wall stub; the backwash main in by a new north-wall stub west of the bay, high over the ramp's head, down into the pit; the pit pump's discharge to a new foul sewer connection in the floor east of the pit; and the break tank's warning pipe and drain falling all the way to the bay's own gullies. It sets the register's valves (valve-0031 to valve-0035) in their lines, every one within reach. The wet hall is no longer `BEING_LAID_OUT`, so the valve, port and model-binding checks hold it; the old hand-laid pipework is gone.
- The backwash pit is emptied only by its pump (the owner, 2026-10-26): its old gravity drain, drain valve valve-0036 (WH-T2-DV1) and overflow, from when it stood on the floor, are gone from the schematic, the register and the world, since 3.75 m down they could not drain; its high-high float's inhibit stops it overfilling. The break tank's inlet comes square down through the middle of its roof, never its side. The schematic's ports match the machines' (`mainsIn`, `discharge`).
- The backwash pit's rail signs are 260 × 390 mm rail signs, their wires tied just inside their corners (the owner, 2026-10-26: the wires missed the smaller card).
- The backwash pit's rail signs are wired at their corners to the top and knee rails, no longer floating (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The backwash pit's ladder is centred along its hatch, and nothing of it stands above the deck (the owner, 2026-10-26): a tank ladder no longer puts a wall bracket or its maker's badge above its top, which in the pit had stuck out across the hatch's swing.
- The backwash pit's hatch (the owner, 2026-10-26): painted chequer-plate tread on its lid, its confined-space sign kept and raised clear of it; the pump's discharge riser comes up through a sleeve in the gratings beside the hatch, no longer through its lid (its stub showed as a black disc on it); the davit's arm bare, its lifting eye gone.
- The backwash pit's hatch lid is plain yellow with its handle: its small confined-space sign, whose manhole symbol read as something lying on it, is gone (the gate carries entry by permit); and the davit's lifting eye is gone, nothing hanging from its arm.
- The pump davit stands bare (the owner, 2026-10-26): its chain block, chain and hook are gone, out of the hatchway; only its lifting eye under the arm's tip, where lifting gear is hung when it is brought for a lift.
- A minimal working fire alarm (the owner, 2026-10-26), the start of prompt 053's: world state `fireAlarm` (`WORLD_VERSION` 37) with resettable manual call points; one at each of the wet hall's three fire points, 1.4 m up beside the extinguishers, latching until reset with its key, and two sounder-beacons (the hall's east wall, the make-up bay) sounding and flashing while any is latched. Red keep-clear boxes on the floor before each fire point; the ramp's fire point moved clear of the ramp's rail.
- The backwash pit's gate and hatch open and shut with a click (the owner, 2026-10-26); the hatch is hinged on its outer edge. Its gratings and closed hatch can be walked on; its rail, davit foot, shut gate and open hatch bar the way. The davit's chain block hangs on a trolley with its chain and hook.
- The backwash pit's gate moves to its east side beside the hatch, a self-closing swing gate on a spring hinge with its latch, keep-clear hatching before it (the owner, 2026-10-26). The pump davit is painted yellow, stands on a 400 mm bolted base plate with four anchor bolts, has a gusset and knee brace, and carries PUMP DAVIT SWL 250 kg along its arm.
- The backwash pit's rail carries its warnings, facing out on every side (the owner, 2026-10-26): DANGER, deep pit, risk of falling; do not stand on the covers, not load-bearing; and at its gate, authorised persons only, entry by permit. A lifebuoy with its throw line hangs on the rail beside the gate, and a fixed stainless ladder (Certikind) goes down the pit's east wall under its hatch, so anyone who falls in has a way out.
- The wet hall's safety (the owner, 2026-10-26): yellow-and-black keep-clear hatching on the floor at the ramp's and the stair's feet, either side of the make-up bay's doors and before the pit's gate, and yellow walkway lines along the aisle; three fire points, each a CO2 and a foam extinguisher with a fire action notice (in the bay by its doors, by the stair's foot, by the ramp's foot); at each way in, safety footwear, wet floor and no smoking signs, and no smoking on the bay's doors.
- Floor gullies in the wet hall (the owner, 2026-10-26: spills must drain), each side's apart: two Certikind gullies in the make-up bay, by the break tank's warning overflow and in front of the booster set, on their own branch to the foul drain, never the pit; two on the dirty side, by the pit's pump and in its south-west corner; none in the aisle.
- The backwash pit's four float switches (the owner, 2026-10-26): cable-suspended tilt floats by its sump under the gratings, STOP (5 %), START (40 %), HIGH (80 %) and HIGH-HIGH (93 %), each hanging dry or tilted up on the water at the world's level, each a component saying which. They are its controls (`pitFloats`): its pump started by START and run until STOP drops, its high alarm on HIGH, its inhibit set by HIGH-HIGH and released once HIGH drops. Tested: all five Olympic filters backwashed at once fill it to HIGH-HIGH in about six and a half minutes, the beds already clean, and its inlet shuts with no spill.
- The sewer outfall monitor (WH-FQR1) on the wet hall's south wall (the owner, 2026-10-26): a Penfold panel with the outfall flowmeter's Kessler-Moody converter (its flow and totaliser), an OVER CONSENT lamp, and a single-pen strip-chart recorder writing the world's record of the outfall's flow, a day in its window, the 10 m³/h consent ruled in red. The recorder (`createTrendRecorder`) draws a kept record, so its paper is the same after a reload; tried first on dev0's period-instruments board. The flowmeter's spool goes on the outfall pipe when the wet hall's pipework is routed (prompt 036).
- The sewer outfall's monitor in the world (the owner, 2026-10-26; world version 35): what the plant puts down the foul sewer (the backwash pit's pump, its gravity drains, its overflow, and the big pools' drain-downs), its flow now, its total, and a record of its mean flow every five minutes for a week, kept in the world for a chart recorder to draw. The spas' drain-downs now go into the backwash pit with their filters' washes, at their own drain's rate, so the pit's pump is the site's one controlled discharge.
- Prompt 036's wet-hall redesign plan and scaled 2D layout: a protected GRP make-up tank and a separate below-floor concrete backwash pit, with UK water and wastewater safeguards and a staged dev-room implementation.
- The voxel router understands gravity lines (prompt 065): a line tagged `gravity` is refused if its end is not below its start, and is routed only level or downwards, never rising; the jacuzzi plant room's overflows and drains are now routed by it, not by a rule of their own.
- Unlisted title shortcuts: `[` restarts the current shot from the beginning and `]` advances to the next shot. They rebuild the staged scene, apply only on the title screen, and leave keyboard instructions unchanged. The lights-out switch-room flyby has its flashlight on.

- An underwater Olympic pool title shot follows the owner’s five-key dolly zoom with a roll, rising from 0.93 m to 0.16 m below the surface while the lighting remains on.

- A switch-room title flyby follows the owner’s eight camera views, including the final stationary turn and zoom, with the main lighting supply switched off in its staged world and the camera torch on.

- The Olympic tank hall’s ladder-access gate opens with Use and closes itself. Its floor sump drains by gravity through PL2-T1-DV1, worked from an extended-spindle handwheel on the gantry; the valve position persists with the other installed valves.
- Tank-hall inspection now identifies PL2-T1-LT1, the live ultrasonic level transmitter, and the two pump suction intakes. The transmitter’s signal cable rises to the soffit and runs south then west through the partition towards the pool controls.

- DB-L1, the lighting board, is a 400 V TP&N board (the owner, 2026-10-25): a 4-pole main switch-disconnector, and on each way a 10 A Type B RCBO, 30 mA, as BS 7671 Section 702 wants in a pool building, drawn as DIN-rail devices (a new `dolly` handle style) with their TEST buttons and trip flags, each way's phase on its chart. An RCBO trips on its TEST, or on earth leakage at 30 mA from moisture in its fittings, which builds in a hall whose air passes 75 % RH and dries slowly after; reset with the leakage still there, it trips again (world version 33).
- The emergency lighting's central battery is in the world: charging on the switch room's supply; with its mains gone, or a zone's lighting out, it lights that zone's emergency lighting, discharging in proportion to what it carries (three hours at full load); its FUNCTION and DURATION TEST keys work it; the rooms show a dim emergency level wherever their lights are out and it can supply them.
- The spa cabinets' controllers start and stop like the little boards they are (`cabinet-boot.ts`, world version 32): power lost or switched off, they stop at once and their four servo valves spring shut; powered, they boot (BOOTING, 3 s), check their inputs (SELF TEST, 3 s), open their servos one after another (suction, return, filter, heat; STARTING n/4), and only then let the pump run. A fault (NO FLOW, TANK LOW) stops them, and they check and restart once it clears. A piezo beeper gives one short beep coming online and three for a fault; the servos are heard as they turn. A development-only console hook, `poolWorld.store()`, reaches the world.
- General equipment-status guidance in `doc/equipment-status.md`: identity and observability, power/control/operating phases, position versus command, instrument readings, passive and architectural conditions, safety latches, and the implemented boiler vocabulary.
- The spa cabinets have sand filters inside in place of cartridges (the owner, 2026-10-25): a small Lidoluxe pressure vessel sized as its filter (the jacuzzi's Ø0.44 m on a 0.8 m bed, the foot spa's Ø0.38 m on 0.6 m), its servo-turned three-way valve head and gauge on top, and a small sight glass on its front showing its bed, from the filter's own state: its dirt, its channelling, a backwash's murk and the rinse clearing. Its unions meet the cabinet's pipework where the cartridge's did; nothing outside changed.
- What a spa cabinet's wash changes, besides its water's way (the owner, 2026-10-25; `washSignals`): its servo-driven three-way valves whirr as they move at the start and end, then clunk home; its pump stops for the wash's pause, then runs on, lighter and faster against the drain; its peristaltic dosing pumps stop; its UV goes out and warms up for 30 s after; its heater stays off; its jets and air are refused or stopped; its filter's gauge reads the backwash's and the rinse's pressures; an amber WASH lamp lights by the FILTER on its panel. A wash draws from whichever tank the cabinet is joined to: its own surge vessel, or the external balance tank (the jacuzzi's).
