- The spa cabinets' panels made readable (the owner, 2026-10-25): the dot-matrix display on its own strip under the printed circuit, about twice its old size, with DISPLAY and MODE beside it; DISPLAY steps through the status, the filter's wash and the FILTER PRESSURE page; both cabinets show the wash's phase and time left the same way. The filter gauge is off the door, which nothing connects to; its reading is the display's FILTER PRESSURE page and the gauge on the filter inside, which now reads the pump's pressure while it washes too.
- The jacuzzi and foot spa cabinets backwash their filters automatically, the same way (the owner, 2026-10-25): both trials (dev38, dev32) carry their water's dirt through their filter on the shared programme; washing, the pump sends the water through the filter to drain, the spa's circulation and heating stopped. Both cabinets now have one display, the maker's 16 × 2 dot-matrix (no seven-segment readout), and the same two buttons under it: DISPLAY (the status, then the filter's wash) and MODE (the wash's schedule); the jacuzzi keeps its jets and air as its extra panel.
- A packaged spa cabinet's filter and its automatic backwash (`spa-filter.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-25), one programme for the jacuzzi's and the foot spa's: each the ideal filter (Ø0.75 m and Ø0.5 m) behind a multiport valve turned by an actuator; on its schedule, set by MODE (every 24, 12 or 6 hours, or off), or at once by WASH NOW, it stops the pump, backwashes to drain for three minutes, rinses to drain for 45 s and returns to filtering; its display line is the same on both. The ideal filter's bed physics is now its own step (`stepFilterBed`), which any filter uses. Fitting it into the two cabinets is next.
- Dev41, the water bench (the owner, 2026-10-25; prompt 070): each of the water's machines on a line of its own, an ideal source through it to an ideal outlet, each drum displaying its water. Dirty water through an ideal filter comes out with 0.6 g/m³ of solids from 20; clean water through an ideal boiler at 100 kW comes out 4.3 K hotter; through an ideal dosing station set to 2 mg/l, 2 mg/l more chlorinated; pool water through a UV unit at a full dose, with 0.18 mg/l of chloramines from 0.6. Each line has RUN and − / + for its machine.
- Water chemistry only where it matters (the owner, 2026-10-25): the heating loop, the hot water and the chilled water carry their temperature alone (`ThermalWater`); their ideal sources give no chemistry.
- The water's condition (prompt 070, stages 1 and 2; `src/game/water/water-condition.ts`): temperature, suspended solids, free and combined chlorine, pH and an organic load, with the plant's waters (clean, mains, pool, dirty, heating, hot, chilled), mixing by flow or volume, machines as black boxes on a flow (a filter takes out solids only, a heater raises temperature to its primary's, a vessel loses heat to its room, UV and ozone break chloramines and pollution down, hypochlorite and acid are dosed) and the chlorine's reactions as a mass balance; turbidity and redox derived for instruments. Every ideal water source now gives its water's condition, settable; the ideal filter rig (dev40) carries it, each vessel showing its water's temperature, solids and free chlorine.
- Dev40, the ideal filter rig (the owner, 2026-10-25): the ideal filter as a real sand filter's body in blue and white, with its nozzles, gauges and sight glass, run by the model alone; an ideal clean source, an ideal dirty one, a clean outlet and a waste sink, each displaying what passes it; and its Penfold panel working all seven servo valves, the processes (service, backwash, rinse, all shut), CLEAN MEDIA, the dirty source's dirt and the rig's time (×1, ×600), with readouts of every flow and of the water's and bed's dirt. A sand filter's badge and rating plate now follow its livery and bed depth.
- The ideal filter (`src/game/plant/ideal-filter.ts`, the owner, 2026-10-25): one sand filter model for every filter, its diameter (and optionally its bed's depth) its only parameters, all else following from its size and the ideal filter's physics (`FILTER_PHYSICS`: medium-rate 25 m/h filtration, 30 m/h backwash, 1 kg of dirt a cubic metre of sand doubling its resistance). It captures dirt, less well as it fills and fissures; fissures grow while it is loaded and unwashed and heal only in a full backwash; a backwash washes it out, murky then clear; a rinse settles it before service; a clean leaves it as new. Its rig has ideal clean and dirty sources, a clean outlet and a waste sink, and seven servo valves; its control panel's spec is laid out (`ideal-filter-panel.ts`). The dev room is next; the Olympic and the lounge move onto it after.
- The lounge's filters gather dirt and are cleaned by a backwash (the owner, 2026-10-25; `lounge-filters.ts`, `WORLD_VERSION` 28): each bed takes dirt from the water it filters, a backwash up through it washes it out, and its pressure difference rises with its load. The whole cycle can be worked from the lounge filter mimic alone: a filter backwashed for five minutes goes from about 1.9 kg of dirt to 0.01 kg and from 0.88 to 0.34 bar. A new game's beds start at 0.54 bar, under the 0.7 bar alarm, which they reach after about twelve hours: a backwash falls due in the first day.
- The Olympic's balance tank can be drained (the owner, 2026-10-25): a pump drain valve, PL2-P1-DV1, off the pumps' discharge to a tundish at the floor and the backwash channel, shut in service. With the make-up and the filters' inlets shut, the duty pump empties the tank at 150 m³/h to the wet hall's holding tank, until the tank's low-low level holds the pumps off; the pool then drains back into the tank through its main drains, as pool and tank are joined. The wet hall's backwash discharge valve (WH-DR1-DV1) and its holding tank's drain (WH-T2-DV1), dead until now, drain the holding tank to the sewer by gravity, 40 and 25 m³/h.
- A healthy New Game, checked (mission 067): `healthFindings(world)` (`src/game/health.ts`) judges pools, motors, services, valves, gas, the wet hall, chemicals, air handling, the jacuzzi, lighting and make-up against the healthy-start contract, with type-checked coverage of every world field and equipment kind. A new game is healthy on its first frame, at every step of its first hour and through a day; negative controls prove the checker finds closed circulation, a missing utility, a low tank and a gas alarm.
- Stair camera smoothing (mission 055): the eye glides over each riser while collision follows the exact step height; placement, ladders, noclip and large floor jumps reset it.
- An ozone generator says its state as its display and lamps show it: operational (producing ozone), on standby, blocked by no flow, tripped with its fault code, or unpowered (the owner, 2026-10-25). Exhibits may give a live status from their own machine's model where the world has none.
- A flowmeter's overlay says what its arrow indicates in the plant's words: "The direction arrow indicates water is flowing from the filter outlet header to the lounge pool sand filter 2" (the owner, 2026-10-25).
- A room's one item of a kind is "the only" one: "The only UV unit in PL1" rather than "1st UV unit in PL1" (the owner, 2026-10-25).
- The lounge filter mimic's valves are symbols on their pipes, each flanked by illuminated OPEN and SHUT buttons whose lenses light by the valve's position, in place of separate lamps (mimic buttons may now carry a lamp, `MimicSpec.buttons[].lamp`).
- Every working part describes itself when aimed at (prompt 068, done): each button, lamp, selector, knob, isolator handle and stop from the part library names itself and what can be seen of it (a lamp lit or out, a selector at AUTO, a handle at ON, a stop pushed in), and says which machine it is on ("AHU-802 HAND OFF AUTO — at AUTO. On AP-AHU1: …"); a builder's richer description (the lounge filter mimic, the pump panels' doors) wins. The audit's count of working parts with no description is 0 in every room, and pinned there.
- The overlay says only what the thing itself shows, or what a person beside it would see, hear or feel (the owner, 2026-10-25: an aid for screens too small to read every display, never a peek into the model). A button says what it commands and what its target is for, no longer the target's state now; a flowmeter reads as its display does (the size, and its arrow's way) and says where it is installed, not what water it carries; a gas detector reads as its display does, O-R over its range; the lounge mimic's readouts say what they show.
- The inspection audit (prompt 068, `inspection-audit.ts`): each room's working parts that still name only their panel are counted, pinned (`inspection-coverage.json`, by `scripts/pin-inspection.test.ts`) and may only fall: 398 now. Every mimic's mode buttons and LAMP TEST describe themselves.
- A UV unit (PL1-UV1, PL2-UV1) says whether it is On or Off, from the world: on while its pool circulates and its isolator is closed (prompt 068). The world models no UV fault, so it never says operational, tripped or faulty, words kept for things with failure modes (the owner, 2026-10-25). The rule is one function (`game/plant/uv.ts`), used by the room's reading too.
- PL1-CP1's and PL2-CP1's doors say what each part is (prompt 068, stage 2): each pump's RUN, STOP and FAULT lamps by the condition that lights them, its ammeter by its motor's current, its HAND · OFF · AUTO selector by where it stands, its RESET as a request with the trip as it is, and the emergency stop. The SUPPLY ON lamp, which nothing drove, now lights with its plant room's supply.
- Parts inside components say what they are (prompt 068, stage 1, `part-inspection.ts`): a part's description is typed, never parsed from its legend. A button carries the very world action its press sends ("OPEN button — Requests PL1-P1-IV1 to open.", then what that valve isolates and its state); a lamp the condition that lights it ("RUN lamp — Lights for P-201A running: lit now."); a readout the selector its display draws from, in the mode shown. A marked part wins over its panel; every mesh of a part is one target; two copies of a mimic keep apart. The lounge filter mimic is wired throughout. Installed flowmeters give their live, signed reading and the path the water is taking through them, from the room's model (none invented at no flow; the Olympic's, not yet in a schematic, keep their register's purpose).
- Hovering over a plant room's ozone-in-air monitor (PL1-GD1, PL2-GD1) gives its live reading, as its detector reads it: "1st Ozone-in-air monitor in PL1, currently reading 0.00 ppm" (the owner, 2026-10-25).
- Planned repeatable mission 067 for a healthy New Game: a maintained operating contract, a dated day-long baseline, and an opt-in read-only audit that records the current model’s trajectory and annunciator indications. Runtime defaults are unchanged by this research.

- The lounge pool's filter mimic (prompt 066, `lounge-filter-mimic.ts`): PL1-CP1's fascia is now a schematic of the lounge battery, grouped by filter, every pipe's LEDs chasing its flow, each filter's four valves round it with OPEN and SHUT lamps and buttons, each filter's signed flow readout, and P-201A and P-201B with RUN, TRIP and NOT AUTO lamps and START and STOP. One component (`views/lounge-filter-mimic.ts`) for PL1-CP1 and the control room, whose wall is still to be agreed; HAND · OFF · AUTO and RESET stay on PL1-CP1's doors. Laid out against a new footprint check (`mimic-layout.ts`): nothing overlaps, 30 mm round every button. Shown working in dev39 against a world of its own.
- The lounge's filters backwash for real (prompt 066, the owner 2026-10-25): a filter with its inlet shut and its outlet and backwash valve open is washed backwards at 34 m³/h by the other filter's filtered water, down its outlet, up through its bed and out to waste; the pool's return drops by that much, and with both backwashing neither can be. Two new Kessler-Moody flowmeters, PL1-F1-FT1 and PL1-F2-FT1, in the lounge's model on each filter's outlet riser, read each filter's signed flow from the world: +45 m³/h in service, −34 while backwashed.
- Dev39, control assemblies (the owner, 2026-10-25): a development room for mimics, control boards and other built-up panels, as dev0 is for single components. Its first exhibit is the mimic LED chaser trial: lines chasing forward, back, still, and one that changes every four seconds.
- Mimic lines can carry an LED chaser (prompt 066, `mimic-chaser.ts`): small LEDs along the line, one in four lit, stepping every 0.12 s of real time in the flow's direction, reversed for back flow, dark under 1 m³/h. Lines without a flow draw as before. Tried in dev39.
- Ctrl/Cmd+C copies the highlighted component’s tag or name; Ctrl/Cmd+P copies the current pin or noclip camera URL. Copy results and values are logged in the game and browser consoles, including missing targets and clipboard failures. The shared controls preserve typing, selected text copying and browser modifier combinations.

- Hanging pipe identification plates have two fine metal suspension loops and wires connecting their upper fasteners to the pipe.
- The remaining back rooms are bound to the model (prompt 064, `back-room-schematics.ts`): the hypochlorite and acid stores, the loading bay, the air plant (every unit's outdoor, exhaust, supply and extract duct its own link), the control room's instruments and the engineers' office with its ring main, sockets and PIR switch. Safety showers, spill kits, PPE cabinets and the water-test kit are decorative, by the owner's rulings, as are furniture and gantries. Every room's drawing is now wholly in the model.
- The inspection overlay says whether a valve is open or shut now (the owner, 2026-10-25: a thing with several states says which), as it says a pump's on or off.
- The switch room is bound to the model (prompt 064): its schematic (`switch-room-schematic.ts`), the building's incoming supply through the main switch to the LV switchboard and on to the rooms, the lighting board and the central battery, the generator connection and the main earthing terminal; its cable ladder carries the switchboard's feeds. Signs in every room are marked decorative. With it, every plant room, the wet hall, the boiler house and the switch room are wholly in the model.
- The boiler house is bound to the model (prompt 064): its schematic (`boiler-house-schematic.ts`) has its three cascades and six boilers, their flues and gas, the headers, low-loss header, circulator, calorifier, pressurisation, hot water, panels, mimics and electrics; its seven valves kept under their asset ids. Its tees bind by the run they are of or as they declare; valves' tag plates bind with their valves. Its scene is checked whole.
- The Olympic plant room is bound to the model (prompt 064): its model is its plumber's expansion with an annex (`olympic-annex.ts`) of what the room lays besides it, under the ids the room already gives them (its wall machines, the pumps' two discharges, the battery's headers and side-stream, the regulating valve, the pool's overflow and main drain, the dosing, sample and ozone lines, the busbar and its tap-offs, the room's supply). Its drawing carries its model ids (runs their lines, valves and flowmeters their fittings), and its scene is checked whole.
- The wet hall is bound to the model (prompt 064): its schematic (`wet-hall-schematic.ts`), with every room's standard electrics (`roomElectrics`: supply, switch-fuse, distribution conduit, control bus, a feed to each powered machine); a service conduit binds to the link that reaches its machine in its utility. Static merges keep each marked component's mark (`mergeStatic`). The scene check covers every room with a schematic.
- The lounge plant room is bound to the model (prompt 064): its schematic (`lounge-schematic.ts`) has its machines, headers and boundaries, a link for each of its drawn runs, its eleven valves (under their asset ids and plates, kept by the register) and its motors' isolators, plus the ozone monitor; runs that are part of a header or a link say so where they are drawn (`PipeRun.model`, `PipeTee.model`). Its scene is checked whole: every pipe, marking, plate, tee, valve, isolator, channel and machine bound, the isolators' stands decorative. Tag-only marks resolve through the registers (`bindingForTag`). 235 unbound things remain across the plant rooms.
- HUD declutter (062): no opening wall of controls; one brief menu hint, contextual component/ladder/flight information, and a full pause-menu reference. Automatic controls remember keyboard use across rooms, touch sticks follow active touch, and hybrid laptops start without sticks. On-screen controls now offer Automatic, Always and Never, with saved boolean choices migrated.
- The footbath plant room is bound to the model (prompt 064; the owner, 2026-10-25: keep every room's design, attach every component): its schematic (`footbath-schematic.ts`), in a registry of the rooms' schematics (`schematics.ts`) that the jacuzzi's joins; its valves keep the ids the world already uses (the identified model's return, drain and make-up; the installed heating valves), so saves carry on. Its overflow from the footbath is unvalved, as the owner ruled: a drawn valve its model lacks is no longer drawn (valve-0094 retired).
- The jacuzzi plant room as it was before its re-plumbing is kept as `retired1` (`?room=retired1`): its layout and its cabinet's runs frozen (`retired/retired1.json`, written once by `scripts/retire-jacuzzi.test.ts`), drawn in its shell with its power, valves and model bindings its original room's.
- Codex's review of the implementation, taken on board (prompt 064; `doc/reviews/2026-10-25-codex-review-064-implementation.md`): the jacuzzi's make-up and drain are their valves' states alone (the cabinet's filling and the tub's discharge read the valves, and the pool step derives its flags from them; a test shuts the make-up by the old pool action and reopens it at the valve); the given fittings carry how each is worked, and the drawing takes it from the schematic; a test sets each drawn valve on its own run. The whole jacuzzi plant room, shell and all, is checked on its scene (`buildPlantRoomContents`): its mimic, fibre illuminator, music rack and lights are in the model, and only its doors await the owner's ruling; the hall's lights and deck gullies are in the model too, and the blanket decorative mark no longer covers working things.
- The jacuzzi's own room, the pool hall, is bound to the model too (prompt 064, step 6): its contents are built apart from its renderer (`buildPoolHallContents`), so a test builds them and finds every mesh bound or decorative. The jacuzzi's schematic gains the hall's devices (its control plate, music rack, speakers, water quality display and fibre-optic wall, with the signal links between them and the cabinet), and the tub is its spa; the hall's architecture and the spa rules notice are decorative.
- Everything the scene draws is in the model, checked on the scene itself (prompt 064): each builder marks what it draws with its binding to the room's model (`markComponent`'s `model`: a machine's node, a valve's or isolator's fitting, a pipe's link) or as decorative, and `world-in-model-scene.test.ts` builds the jacuzzi plant room's equipment, pipework and services and finds every mesh bound or decorative; for a room not yet built from its model it names what is not. The jacuzzi's pipes, their stencils, valves, valve panel, isolator, busbar and tap-off, gullies and falling water are bound; pipe supports are decorative.
- The jacuzzi plant room is built from its model (prompt 064, steps 3 to 5): its schematic (`jacuzzi-schematic.ts`) holds its machines (the packaged cabinet, the balance tank, the spa, and the heating main, water service, gully, busbar, switch-fuse and valve panel it meets), every link between them as its runs are drawn, and every valve and the cabinet's isolator, each with what it is for and its state in service; the plumber expands it and adds nothing. Its valves are derived from it (`model-valves.ts`): their ids are the model's, which the world keeps their states by and the jacuzzi's simulation reads (`JACUZZI_VALVES`); their tags follow the hierarchy (PL3-CAB1-IV1 … the cabinet's, PL3-BT1-IV1 and PL3-BT1-DV1 the tank's; the cabinet's were PL3-JZ1-…); their names their links'; their descriptions their links' ends ("Isolates water flowing from the water service to the jacuzzi balance tank."). The tub's drain valve is its drain-down. Everything the room draws is bound to the model: its pin of unbound things is empty (331 left across the plant rooms, from 363). Saves from before (version 26) load as a new world, as the owner allows when the model changes.
- The plumber takes the schematic's own valves (prompt 064, step 2): `expandForPlumbing`'s `given` fittings, each on its link at its end, take the place of the ones its rules would set (matched by the node and port served, so a shared port's is one), a given valve the rules would not set is set in its line all the same, and every fitting the rules add that the schematic lacked is reported in `added` (none, for a correct schematic).
- Everything in the world is in the model (the owner, 2026-10-25): `world-in-model.test.ts` lists what each plant room draws (machines, valves, flowmeters, isolators, electrical components, pipes) and pins, by identity, each room's things neither in its enhanced network nor decorative (`world-in-model.unbound.json`, written only by `PIN_UNBOUND=1 npx vp test run scripts/pin-unbound.test.ts`), so the list can only shrink and nothing new can be drawn outside the model. Each drawn thing has one typed binding: a machine to a node, a valve, meter or isolator to a fitting, a pipe to a link, or decorative. At the start, 361 of 479 are unbound: the Olympic plant room binds 107 of its 143; the rest bind nothing yet. The room-layout guide now sets the planning process in the owner's order: the schematic with every machine and valve, the machines placed and optimised, the plumber's pipes and the schematic's valves, then scoring.
- The old plant network is deleted (`src/game/plant/network/`, `PLANT_NETWORK`, its serial register and the two scripts that served it, `scripts/model-gaps.test.ts` and `scripts/assign-serials.test.ts`): nothing in the game used it, and each room's enhanced network is the one source of truth (the owner, 2026-10-25). The 4 m storey height it held moved to `plant-rooms.ts`.
- The Olympic plant room's UV unit is labelled "Olympic pool UV unit" (it read "uV unit"); the information overlay computes the room's mesh bounds in idle time, so the first look does not stall.
- The engineer’s office has two adjacent working stores cupboards on its west wall, while its scenario simulator remains in its original place. A 240 V conduit ring supplies switched sockets by the desk and on the east and south walls. A wall-mounted PIR turns the office lights on shortly after movement and off after 90 seconds vacant; the passive cupboards no longer darken as electrical loads when office power is cut.
- The information overlay, stage 1 ([061](doc/prompts/done/061-component-information-overlay.md)): the help line at the foot of the screen names what is aimed at, by the free pointer or, with the pointer locked, the view's centre, within 3 m: `PL3-BT1-IV1: Balance tank make-up valve` and, below it, its description from prompt 060, live ("1st Pump in PL2, currently Off" changes as the pump starts, the pointer still). A target is named once it has stayed under the aim 150 ms, so the line does not flicker between neighbours; the line changes only when its wording does. It gives way to climbing, carrying, flying, the menu and the console, and inspecting never uses anything. The first surface the aim meets blocks what is behind it, and the nearest marked owner up its parents is named (a pump within its pump set); scene builders mark each component as they build it (`component-identity.ts`): placed equipment, valves, flowmeters, switch-fuses, the jacuzzi's balance tank and each circulation pump. dev0 names its controls by their parts' names and its tagged flowmeter DEV-FT1. Picking tests only meshes whose bounds the aim enters within reach: 3 ms in the Olympic plant room, where testing every mesh took 11 ms (the services ceiling's merged meshes). The lounge's pump set now prints its own pumps' tags, P-201A and P-201B, where it had the Olympic's.
- Inferred component descriptions ([060](doc/prompts/done/060-inferred-component-descriptions.md)): one description for each component, shared by the overlay, the inventory, the printed manual and the console's `status`, derived from the game's model, never written item by item (the owner, 2026-10-25: the enhanced network, the plumber's expanded port graph, is the source of truth; "Everything has to derive from the actual game model … derived algorithmically"). A valve says what it isolates from its link in its room's enhanced network (`olympicValveRoutes`): "Isolates water flowing from the circulation pumps to sand filter 1."; a node is named by its plan label or, failing one, its kind and port ("the main plant's drain"), and a shared port by its links' kinds ("the filters and the heat exchanger"). Only the Olympic plant room is built through the plumber, so only its valves are described; the rest have no description until their rooms are (prompt 063). A switch-fuse "Isolates power for the jacuzzi plant room."; other equipment its place among its room's items of its kind, its type, its room and, where the world keeps it, its live status: "1st Pump in PL2, currently Off" (`component-status.ts`): motors On, Off or Tripped; balance, break and backwash tanks how full; pump sets their pumps; the booster set; the jacuzzi cabinet; the footbath cabinet's switch, each found from the item's kind and room. Missing state is no status, never Off; an item with nothing known shows no description rather than a generic sentence. The equipment kinds, with their type words and status wording, are one finite list (`equipment-kinds.ts`). The circulation pumps are listed one by one from the world's motor starters (P-101A, P-101B, P-201A, P-201B), apart from their pump sets.
- Reviewed prompt 060 for inferred component descriptions against the executable catalogue and network; specified endpoint validation, shared snapshot-based descriptions, pump child/assembly identities and manual compatibility, and recorded two legacy valve connection conflicts to resolve during implementation.
- Everything registered (the owner, 2026-10-25): installed equipment drawn outside the rooms' layouts has a register of its own (`installed-fixtures.ts`): the jacuzzi's balance tank PL3-BT1, the Olympic balance tank PL2-T1 and the Olympic plant room's six Kessler-Moody flowmeters (PL2-P1-FT1, PL2-F1-FT1 to PL2-F5-FT1), each with a permanent asset id, its maker and purpose, in the inventory and the console's `status`. `registration.test.ts` fails on any plant tag written in the code (comments aside) that no register holds, on any drawn flowmeter unregistered, and on any asset id or tag given twice. The distribution board no longer defaults to the retired SR-DB3.
- The console's `status` takes a name (the owner, 2026-10-25): `status PL3-BT1` prints everything known of an object, from the plant inventory (what it is, its maker, purpose, normal and state), an installed valve's command and travel, a motor starter, live readings the world keeps elsewhere (a balance tank's contents in m³, how full, its make-up and drain), a pool by its id or any part of the world by its name, every value by a dotted name; it suggests near names for one it does not know and completes them (`object-status.ts`). Time acceleration is researched (`doc/research/time-acceleration.md`): 10,000× is safe with 10 s steps, measured against 0.1 s steps.
- Researched component identification in the instructions overlay: existing catalogue tags and purposes, shared input targeting, occlusion and merged-mesh ownership, with a dev0-first implementation proposal in `doc/research/component-information-overlay.md`. Extended the proposal with narrative confirmations for player valve, button and machine operations, reusing action and component events while distinguishing commands, completed operations and ineffective presses. The feature is not implemented yet.
- Published devlog 001, “A title sequence that flies through the building”, through itch.io's authenticated editor with build 0.1.265 attached, and recorded its URL, source coverage and publication time. The project remains in Draft access mode, so the post is not yet publicly accessible.
- The jacuzzi's balance tank can be emptied (the owner, 2026-10-25): a manual drain to waste, PL3-BT1-DV1 (`valve-0101`, shut in service, saved in the world's installed valves), a DN50 lever valve on a nozzle at the tank's foot on its north side, its lever opening away from the tank, piped into the tank overflow's own tundish on its floor gully, beside the overflow's down-pipe (one gully for both, moved 0.15 m further north for the valve; the owner). Opened by hand, it empties the tank by gravity as an orifice under the tank's head (`JACUZZI_TANK_DRAIN`: 45 mm bore, Cd 0.5, about 8.6 m³/h at the float, six minutes from the float, ten from full), pumps or no pumps, power or no power, against the make-up, which holds it a few centimetres deep under its low-low if left open; the low-low trips the pump as before and the tank's sight gauge follows it down. With the pump stopped the tub's main drains run on into the tank, so the tub empties through it too, in about an hour. Its water is seen and heard falling into the tundish. Lever valves on runs along z now take their `facing` too (turned round, a lever swings the other way).
- The title's attract sequence flies the owner's lounge fly-by: eleven noclip shots joined into one smooth closed loop (`src/toolkit/title/logic/camera-path.ts`, after `doc/research/camera-splines.md`): a centripetal Catmull-Rom spline through the shots' positions, flown at a steady 0.7 m/s by arc length, 64 s round; yaw, pitch, roll and zoom (by its logarithm) smooth through every shot and across the seam, turning the short way between shots and never swinging past one, the yaw carrying on round as the loop does. One waypoint is added between the fourth and fifth shots so the camera passes a column instead of grazing it. Attract shots take a `path` beside `orbit`, and their camera now rolls and zooms (`shotPose`).
- The attract sequence's second shot is the owner's Olympic plant room fly-by (2026-10-25): seventeen noclip shots round the room at pipe height, rolled hard and flying close to the plant on purpose, 141 s round; a loop may be given walls to keep inside (`createCameraLoop`'s `within`), and this one keeps 0.1 m in from the room's, where its curve along the south wall would bulge 2 cm through. The Olympic hall's high orbit is gone, so the lounge fly-by opens the sequence. Each shot now plays from a point chosen at random round its move for about eight beats of the theme (60 BPM, measured from its onsets: 8 s, fades included, `SHOT_SECONDS`), then fades to black, and the next plays from a random point of its own (`stepAttract`'s `random`). The attract sequence's rooms are now seen, not heard: `partAudio` has a game bus before its master, which the title hushes while its backdrop runs (`setGameMuted`), and the title's theme alone plays on (`hold`'s `{ title: true }`).
- Two Olympic hall dollies join the attract sequence (the owner, 2026-10-25): a high one along the hall's north side looking down across the pool, and a level one, as Wes Anderson would shoot it, low at the hall's west end, the camera square to the hall and looking straight at the pace clock's wall on a long lens, sliding dead straight across and back. Each is the owner's two shots squared to one line, height and heading. A camera path of two keys is now a dolly (`createDolly`), out and back, easing to rest at each end, instead of a loop that would turn about at full speed. The main corridor has a fly-by too: ten of the owner's shots round the ring at 3 m, a whole barrel roll to the circuit, kept on the corridor's floor clear of its walls (a test checks it). The jacuzzi's placeholder orbit is gone (the owner: "It's boring"), so every shot is now an authored path, and they play in a random order, a pool hall's and a back room's by turns (`chooseNext`: never one room twice running, nor a shot straight back again; the next drawn as each starts, so its room is fetched while it shows). The jacuzzi plant room has a path of the owner's too, not a loop: eleven close shots among its plant on long lenses, start to end (`createCameraPath`): each stretch takes as long as the slowest of its flight, its turn and its zoom (or a key's own `seconds`; the owner's third and fourth shots share a place, and the camera lingers 3 s there), its progress a monotone curve through each key's time, easing from rest and to rest; a shot on an open path starts early enough to be seen before it ends. The shots: three in the pool halls (the high and level Olympic dollies, the lounge fly-by) and three in the back rooms (the Olympic plant room's fly-by, the jacuzzi plant room's path, the corridor's fly-by).
- Every attract shot is now a path, start to end (the owner, 2026-10-25: "don't reverse any of the sequences; they aren't loops, just paths"): the lounge, plant room and corridor fly-bys no longer close back to their first shot, and the two Olympic dollies run once from one end to the other without coming back. Each is flown by `createCameraPath` (which now takes walls to keep inside, as the plant room's does), and its random eight beats are chosen to finish before it ends. Loops and dollies (`createCameraLoop`) remain for any shot that wants one.
- The boiler house joins the attract sequence's back rooms (the owner, 2026-10-25): a track down the room past the boilers, then a slow dolly zoom, the camera drawing back over 6 s as its lens widens from 1.66× to 0.77× and its roll deepens, and pushing in again over 6 s to the same framing.
- So does the air plant room (the owner, 2026-10-25): a long straight track high down the room on a wide lens, then a swoop down and round into a close look at its far end.
- A daily-devlog workflow and draft template in `doc/devlogs/`, using dated, numbered headline filenames, verified source/build coverage and explicit publication records. Compilation remains on request; itch.io editor publication is separate from butler build uploads.
- In noclip the location pin becomes a camera (📷): its link is a camera shot's, adding `noclip=true` and always `roll` (degrees clockwise from level, as before), and a link with `noclip=true` opens in noclip at the shot's height, roll and all. Leaving noclip takes `noclip` out of the address bar, so a reload walks again. Copying a link (pin or camera) flashes the button instead of saying "Link copied" in the overlay, which now speaks only when a copy fails; the pause menu still says it. Entering noclip now starts from the walker's eye where it stands, not the camera's height before its first frame (which had dropped a linked shot to the floor).
- A Quake-style console (stage 1 of [058](doc/prompts/done/058-developer-console.md)). The key left of 1 drops it down in a room. It logs every action done to the world, saying who did it (you or the console) and "no change" where nothing changed, and what the plant does by itself: pumps starting, stopping and tripping, low-low level trips, the jacuzzi cabinet and booster set tripping, the boiler house's gas and SSOV, gas alarms, and lights. Its REPL takes Quake commands (`help`, `status`, `rooms`, `room`, `goto`, `noclip`, `torch`, `pause`, `cam`, `valves`, `open`, `close`, `switches`, `switch`, `motors`, `starter`, `get`, `act`, `log`, `condump`, `history`, `clear`), or JavaScript with the commands as functions and `world` and `act` in scope. It has Tab completion, a kept history and `;` between commands. The log lives in the player's world store (`src/game/events/`) and is never saved; the walker takes no input while the console is down, but the world runs on unless `pause` is typed. A tab following another tab's save now logs when it takes that save up.
- Noclip can roll the camera about its line of sight, for unusual (Dutch) angles: Q and E roll it left and right while held, R levels it; LB and RB on a gamepad, Y to level; held ⟲ and ⟳ buttons and LEVEL on touch. The readout shows the roll, Copy Camera Link carries it as `roll` (degrees clockwise, left out when level), and a link with `roll` opens rolled. The roll carries on into the next room and through doorways; leaving noclip returns to the walking view as it was.
- The attract sequence has worlds of its own, never the player's: prepared presets (`normal`, `lights-out`, `pools-drained`), each built from a new world and frozen (`attract-scenarios.ts`); each shot runs a copy afresh in memory, never saved, its rooms built on canvases of their own. Rooms now take their world from `roomWorld()`, the player's unless something else (the attract sequence) has set another. A shot may light the camera's torch.
- The title's attract sequence: behind the title menu, shots of the game's rooms one after another (for now three slow orbits, the Olympic hall, the lounge and the jacuzzi, each 20 s), fading to black between them and holding black until the next room is built, round and round while the theme plays on; the game paused under it, the rooms it builds let go as it moves on and when the title is left (`attract-sequence.ts`, [054](doc/prompts/done/054-title-attract-sequence.md)).
- The jacuzzi's balance tank is installed (stage 2 of [056](doc/prompts/done/056-jacuzzi-balance-tank.md)): a 1.3 m³ cream GRP tank by Lidoluxe in the plant room's north-west corner, north of the cabinet (`spa-balance-tank.ts`), its sight gauge reading the world's level, its float and low-low marked. The jacuzzi is replumbed: the spa's overflow comes through the north wall and drops into the tank's lid through IV2; the pump draws from the tank through a new motorised suction valve, IV8 (PL3-JZ1-IV8), low along the west wall as a flooded suction must be; the tank's make-up comes in from the south over the room's other lanes to its float valve; its overflow drops into a tundish on a floor gully of its own. In the model the pump's path is its suction and return; shutting IV2 now backs the gutter up over the deck while the pump runs the tank down to TANK LOW, rather than tripping it.
- Dev1's engineer storage cupboards now have fixed, configurable cabinet nameplates and an Ironside maker badge above their paired doors. Each drawer, bin, case and box has an ID and front label; drawers hold removable parts and retract before both doors close.
- Dev38 · Jacuzzi cabinet trial (`?room=dev38`): the jacuzzi's real cabinet, its controller and display, plumbed to an ideal 2.8 m³ spa (eight bathers to seat), an ideal 1.3 m³ balance tank (its float and low-low marked) and ideal sources of power, water and heat, nothing of the world's (`jacuzzi-trial.ts`). A Penfold trial console works its eight valves, cuts its sources and seats the bathers; LCDs read where the water is. The cabinet can now be driven by the world or by a trial (`JacuzziCabinetBinding`). The trial's water goes round (pump to spa, over the lip, gutter, overflow valve, tank), and is tested for steady running, the bathers' surge, the worst case of every seat taken with the pump stopped, each valve, and each source cut. The jacuzzi cabinet's display is larger, and its NO FLOW lamp and RESET stand clear of its gauge.
- The jacuzzi's balance tank, in the model (stage 1 of [056](doc/prompts/done/056-jacuzzi-balance-tank.md)): sized for a spa by what it must absorb (eight bathers at 80 L, the gutter's water in transit, the air's lift, 30 s of the pump's flow and a 10 % margin), 1.3 m³, about 45 % of the spa's water, where the model had 0.84 m³ and the cabinet's surge vessel holds 35 L. Its make-up float sits low enough for every seat's surge, the air's lift and the gutter's water draining back to fit above it. A packaged spa cabinet is plumbed either to its own surge vessel or to an external tank, never both (`CabinetPlumbing`); the jacuzzi's draws from its external tank, its own vessel disconnected and empty. The cabinet's display shows the tank (TANK 34%, FILLING), and TRIP: TANK LOW at low-low. Its controller is tested against an ideal tank, then with the world's.
- The jacuzzi's treatment cabinet has a controller of its own in the world (`jacuzzi-cabinet.ts`), its size unchanged. Its POWER switch is saved. With its return or overflow valve shut, its pump runs dead-headed for 5 s and then trips (TRIP: NO FLOW); the trip latches through power cuts until RESET (a new button beside the red NO FLOW lamp) is pressed with the valves open again. With its water supply cut off and its balance tank at low-low it trips (TRIP: TANK LOW) and restarts by itself as the tank refills. The UV and dosing run only while water moves. Its heating needs its heating valves (IV3, IV4) as well as the heat service: without them it warns NO HEAT, and TEMP LOW once the water is 2 °C below its setting. Its mimic's LED readout is a backlit 16 × 2 character display: the water and its setting, and its state, trips and warnings in turn. The plant room's water mimic and the wall plate by the tub follow it, tested together (`jacuzzi-systems.test.ts`).
- Dev1's racks gallery now has two reusable engineer's storage cupboards, one with openable solid steel doors and one with openable glazed doors. Both contain working nine-drawer parts banks, open-front bins, shelves, cases and a tool chest.
- Noclip camera authoring from the Esc menu: fly through equipment with WASD, Space up, Shift down and Ctrl faster; touch has UP/DOWN controls and gamepad uses A/B for altitude. Room envelopes bound flight, apparatus interactions and doorway handovers are disabled, and leaving flight restores the walking position. A live coordinate readout and Copy Camera Link support authoring shots without replacing the Continue bookmark. Prompts 038 and 054 describe noclip and the later isolated attract sequence with scenario presets, background loading and fades through black.
- The title screen links to the GitHub source beside its build details. The repository is now `trebizondoculpepper/the_pool`; repository and GitHub Pages links follow its new name.
- The title screen loops the owner's theme, Treblo's Suspended Circulation from Ripple Equilibrium, through the master volume control; it starts after the first browser audio gesture and stops on entering a room.
- Continue restores the saved simulation and the last room, position, viewing direction and zoom. The view is saved periodically, on Save, on quitting to the title and when hiding or closing the page.
- The spa plant rooms' drains are seen as well as heard: while the drain runs, water falls from the drain pipe's open end, behind the cabinet, across its air gap into the tundish on the floor gully, with a splash where it lands (`falling-water.ts`).
- Dev37 · Valve test rig (`?room=dev37`), for trying valves and their sounds: an ideal mains-water source piped in DN80 PVC to an ideal sink through a butterfly valve on the new spring-return electric actuator (`drive: 'electric'`: gear motor while it travels, relay click, end-stop thud), with a Kessler-Moody flowmeter on the line. A Penfold panel has illuminated OPEN and SHUT buttons (lit for the command), SUPPLY, OPEN, SHUT (from the valve's limit switches) and FLOW lamps, and a readout of flow and opening; a Voltara heavy-throw supply isolator cuts the actuator's power, and its spring drives it shut. Flow is in proportion to the opening (`gallery/logic/valve-test-rig.ts`); the room starts fresh and is not saved.
- Dev37 has one of each kind of valve (the owner, 2026-10-24): beside the DN80 butterfly line, a second line through a 35 mm ball valve on the same spring-return electric actuator (`createActuatedBallValve`, new), and on the butterfly line a bypass under its valve through a 54 mm direct solenoid valve, in parallel, worked from the panel's SV1 OPEN and SHUT and snapping shut, with its click, when the supply is thrown off. Each line has its own panel and throw switch; the throw switches now move both ways (their handles were bound to a plain getter, which their computed never re-read), as dev29's selectors now do too.
- The jacuzzi's music is heard in its plant room, through the wall from the hall: the same programme, about 30 dB down.
- The Jacuzzi's upper bare-concrete walls now have flush fibre-optic pinpoints. A Penfold box beside its plant-room water mimic controls the saved illuminator: on/off with a running lamp, brightness, 0–10 twinkle speed (0 is steady), and white/rainbow colour. Dev30's fibre booth also has a white/rainbow selector and a brighter, sharper twinkle.
- Jacuzzi music: a standard 12U cabinet on its plant room's north wall, beside the water-circuit mimic, with reusable `music_source` (1U character LCD and NEXT) and `amplifier` (3U on/off and volume) components, a working supply indication and power/audio feeds. Its six owner-supplied Still Water Garden tracks play through two small waterproof speakers near the hall’s west-side corners above the tile line, aimed inward at the jacuzzi, with short feed wires and concrete grommets. Both speakers share one synchronized audio stream; the live plant room has no speaker. Transport and amplifier settings are saved in world version 24; the transport continues between rooms and pauses when the plant-room power supply fails. Dev36 provides a private music-effects prototype using the same assembly. `duty_managers_office` is now listed as a standalone shell.
- Dev30 has a fibre-optic concrete wall booth: 88 warm-white pinpoints flush with a raw board-marked wall, scattered irregularly and driven by its Lumenhaus dimmer and a twinkle-wheel selector.
- The jacuzzi's simulated water quantity now drives percent full and visible height through a reusable basin profile. Its two tapered basins meet at the seat: about 20 % of the water reaches the seat, and the other 80 % fills the wider upper basin. Dev35's FILL % control runs from empty to full; the water surface stays continuous in height as it widens over the step.
- The jacuzzi fills through its jets: while water comes in, each jet out of the water throws a gentle arc that lands on the seat (water below the seat) or on the surface, with rings where it meets water; once the water covers a jet, its arc is gone. Dev35's FILL % knob is now a target the tub fills or drains toward, quickly enough to watch.
- The jacuzzi's safety interlock: a level switch 30 mm under the overflow lip. Until the tub is full the jets and bubbles cannot be started, from the wall plate, the cabinet or Dev35's panel, and if the level falls while they run they cut out and stay off until started again.
- POOL-03's jacuzzi plinth now meets the west wall at its rear, with steps on its other three sides; Dev35's TUB EFFECTS panel is mounted on a short post directly in front of the supplied viewpoint, with the tub visible beyond it.
- Dev35's jacuzzi water lab has a TUB EFFECTS panel (the owner, 2026-10-24): its water's level from empty to full in 5 % steps, with an LED readout, and illuminated JETS, LIGHT and BUBBLES buttons, each pressed on and off, setting the tub directly past its controller. The tub's water falls inside its wall and then its seat's ring as the level drops; its jets stream only under water, and its bubbles rise only from sources under water, up to the surface.
- Every green build of `main` is pushed to itch.io (`salimfadhley/the-pool`, channel `html5`) by CI's new itch job, with butler, logging in with the `BUTLER_API_KEY` repository secret, and tagged with the build's version.
- Pool ladders now mount with Use at either end. Forward climbs and back descends without requiring a particular view angle; reaching the deck or pool floor returns to normal walking. PR_A and the pool hall, donut pool and arched pool ladders use the shared control scheme.
- The nearest closed-door room's code is prefetched before opening. Scene construction waits until the door opens, and an already visited nearest room stays cached when its door closes.

- The jacuzzi's jets and air in the world (world version 22): its plant room cabinet's JETS and AIR are illuminated push buttons (from a selector and a lamp each), pressed to start and to stop, lit while each runs; the plate on the wall by the tub (START for ten minutes, STOP) works the same state, and the tub's jets, bubbles and sounds show it, so the cabinet's lamps follow the jets however they were started. They run only while the jacuzzi circulates.
- Dev35 · Jacuzzi Water Lab (`?room=dev35`) reuses the existing jacuzzi tub and control plate in an isolated room with a private, unsaved controller, for water-effect experiments without world-model bindings.

- Motor starters in the world ([044](doc/prompts/044-plant-room-motor-panels.md), world version 21): the Olympic's and the lounge's circulation pumps (P-101A/B, P-201A/B), duty and standby, each with HAND · OFF · AUTO, START and STOP in AUTO, a trip that holds until RESET, and a current. The pools circulate while one runs; the standby starts whenever the duty is not running. A pump run for a minute with its valves shut against it trips on its thermistors. The plant rooms' local control panels work them: their selectors, a new RESET per pump, their emergency stops and their mimics' START and STOP, with lamps and ammeters showing them; the control room's main pool board works the same starters. The panels' LOCAL · REMOTE key switch is gone, as both boards are the one world's. The air handling units' hall humidity now feeds the control room's readings.
- The air handling units are now in the world (`game/plant/air-handling.ts`, world version 20): each unit's dampers, fans, heat pump, filters, trip, isolator and HAND-OFF-AUTO are saved and run whichever room the player is in, fed from the air plant's supply. Each hall has a relative humidity, which its units hold in the mid-fifties and which climbs toward 80 % when they stop. A hall's air heating, and its pool's evaporation, follow its units' running airflow. Switching off the Olympic's nine units leaves its hall about 2 °C colder and at 77 % humidity after two hours, and 11 °C colder after a day. The units' controllers and the air plant's mimic, including each hall's OCCUPIED and UNOCCUPIED buttons, show and work the world's units. A dev room's exhibit units still keep their own state.
- Dev34 · Stair Tower (`?room=dev34`): a wide room four storeys tall, with decks at each upper storey along both its west and east walls and a straight stair outside each, three flights on in the same direction from the floor to the top deck; the west in painted mild steel with safety-yellow rails, the east in stainless with black nosings. Both are built by the stair helper (`stair-run.ts`), taken out of the air plant, which now uses it too; the helper takes the wall its decks stand against, names for a second stair, and a finish.
- The boiler house re-planned (the owner, 2026-10-24): laid out by hand, the calorifier and pressurisation unit brought from the far north corners to the south-east, by the circulator and the low-loss header, so its heating flow and return are 80 m of pipe instead of 94; its boilers', header's, circulator's and gas train's feeds come down their walls, not over the flues.
- The air handling plant re-planned (the owner, 2026-10-24): the stair to the Olympic stacks' gantries now runs outside them, one straight run of two flights with a landing at the middle gantry and another at the top, nothing overhead and the floor under the lower gantry clear for the lowest units; each flight has its own handrails; a walker can climb it all the way (a solid meant for people under the first flight had stopped anyone on it). The room is narrowed to its contents, 9.5 × 25 m from 17.5 × 28.
- Plant Room 1's eleven filter, pump-discharge and balance-tank valves are now solenoid actuated and commandable from its Penfold mimic. Their actual travel and spring return on loss of power or air live in the saved world, and the mimic shows the open and shut limit states. The lounge pool circulation follows the pump and filter paths through those valves. The filter pipes now meet the curved shells radially, their outlet bends drop below the front observation area, and the small filters' sight glasses sit clear of the nozzle bank.
- Dev33 · Footbath Water Level (`?room=dev33`): the footbath hall on its own, its neighbours not wired in, with a Penfold panel on its west wall whose LEVEL knob sets its water from empty to full in 5 % steps, an LED readout showing the per cent.
- The foot spa's make-up seen coming in ([041](doc/prompts/done/041-footspa-makeup-jets.md)): while fresh water is entering it (make-up valve open, its float calling, the wet hall able to supply), a thin clear stream from each south-wall inlet arcs down to the surface with small rings spreading where it lands; with the nozzles under water, only the rings (`inlet-streams.ts`, `footbathMakeUpEntering`). Its nozzles are 3 cm lower, and full now stands just below the grilles, 3 cm under the deck.
- A shallow pool's water no longer shows empty early: empty is its own floor, not the hall's 0.2 m minimum depth, so the footbath's bottom quarter is no longer hidden under its tiles.
- The valve motors' pitch spread is widened to ±10 Hz of the 271 Hz whine (from ±6 Hz), so they are more easily told apart.
- Every knob turns down or up by which half of it is clicked, in every room: the pool halls, the racks, architectural and corridor-lab rooms and the dev and plant corridors gave their knobs no direction, so they could only turn up. A test now fails any walkable room that works parts without asking `knobDirection`.
- Dev32 (`?room=dev32`) is an isolated foot spa trial: the installed Lidoluxe cabinet works against a square-top translucent ideal pool whose width-to-height ratio is A4's. Its front LCD shows live water temperature and litres; the water block rises as it fills. Ideal mains water, heating water and 230 V sources, two receiving sinks, a valve test panel and a live water quality sign are bound one-for-one to typed port-graph nodes. Every physical graph edge is drawn with the pipe helper and checked for clashes.
- The foot spa's drain-down is heard: while its water runs to drain (`footbathDischarging`: lined up to drain, pumped, not tripped, water left), a gurgle plays at its tundish over the floor gully in the plant room. It is a new machine tone, `drain-gurgle`, synthesised from bubbles: many small rising notes as the water drags air under, the trap's occasional deep glug, and the splash and trickle of the falling water.
- Each motorised valve takes its own time to turn (`motorStroke`), within 10 % of the 6 s standard, by its place in its room.
- Each motorised valve's gear motor is a few hertz off the others (`motorDetune`, by its place in its room, within ±6 Hz of the recorded 271 Hz whine), so several running together are each heard apart, and a valve always sounds the same.
- A gear-motor sound for motorised valves, resynthesised from a recording of a small DC gear motor (`gear-motor` in `machine-tones.ts`, in dev7's listening room): its 271 Hz whine and harmonics as measured (the fifth, 1354 Hz, loudest), the gear shaft's once-a-turn swell, a slower gear stage and the brushes' hiss. The foot spa's motorised valves now travel over a 6 s stroke at a steady speed (`plant/logic/motorised-valve.ts`): the motor is heard running up as a valve sets off, steady while it moves, and running down as it arrives; its actuator's lamp shows amber between its limits.
- All of the foot spa plant's water and heating valves are motorised ball valves, each with a rotary actuator and open/shut lamp. A wider Penfold panel higher on the east wall (`plant/views/valve-control-panel.ts`) has two valves per row, with illuminated SHUT and OPEN pushbuttons bound to the world. The make-up's chain wheel is gone; the floor before the panel stays clear.
- Dev31 · Effects Playground (`?room=dev31`): the Encabulator's effects room and hallucination room combined into one room, their two consoles side by side, so the end-state effects (channels breathing apart, π drifting, warp, precision failing, phaser, divergence) and the hallucinations (apparitions, ghosting, geometric patterns) can be played together. Copied files are listed in `doc/reuse-inventory.md`.
- The packaged spa sets carry their own mimic (the owner, 2026-10-23: the foot spa is too small for a board of its own): along the top of the cabinet's control panel, its circuit printed (spa, surge vessel, pump, filter, UV, heat exchanger), LEDs for the pump, the UV, the heat and, on the foot spa, its drain and make-up, and one fixed-purpose LED readout of the water's temperature with its legend on Dymo tape (`plant/views/cabinet-mimic.ts`). Beside it the pools' thermostat: ▲ and ▼ show its setting (its SET lamp lit) and step it by half a degree. Each pool's setpoint is the world's (`pool/setpoint`, 26 to 40 °C; WORLD_VERSION 18), and the thermal model heats to it: the jacuzzi's water warms or cools to a new setting over hours.
- Plant Room 1's first smaller layout trial: a 12 × 11 × 8 m shell with the pool door north and shared plant doors south, matching machine placement and pipe wall endpoints. The footbath now has two visible floor drain grilles set into open sumps.
- Plant Room 1's two sand-filter outlet rises now leave each vessel to its free side before turning to the overhead header; the inlet and outlet pipework has no remaining measured clashes. A 12 × 10 m shell trial failed working-access and routing checks, so the 12 × 11 m room remains the live layout.
- The packaged cabinets' pump is heard (the owner, 2026-10-23): the footbath's and the Jacuzzi's cabinet hum with their pump's motor while it runs, running up and coasting down as the pump starts and stops; at full volume while either door is open more than about 20 degrees, fading evenly in decibels to 20 dB down as the last one shuts (`door-attenuation.ts`, by the doors' actual swing).
- The packaged cabinets' rooms' pipe routing (the owner, 2026-10-23): the heights the jacuzzi's and the footbath's cabinet runs cross the room at searched (`CABINET_LEVELS`), the lowest that keep every run's level lengths over head height, clear of the doors, the cabinet and every other run: about 7 % cheaper in each (the jacuzzi's £2,206 to £2,045, the footbath's £1,389 to £1,289). Each water run's isolating valve now stands up its rise behind the cabinet, staggered, clear of the elbows (it had sat on the stub's short first length, meeting the elbow); the cabinets' stubs end plain. The ten new valves are registered pipework valves (`installed-valves.ts`, PL3-JZ1-IV1 to IV7, PL4-PW1-IV2 to IV4), worked through the world; the footbath's named return valve moves up its rise too.
- The Jacuzzi's plant room optimised the same way (the owner, 2026-10-23): of 108 places along its walls that keep its doors and its mimic's floor clear, the cheapest is on the west wall, 1.55 m south of where the packer had put its cabinet; set there by hand, 0.9 m off its wall for a working space behind it, its services cost £2,206 against £2,221 before, though their metres go up (the dearer runs shorter).
- The footbath's plant room optimised (the owner, 2026-10-23): every place along its walls the spa set's cabinet could stand, at three gaps behind it, priced by its services' runs (`cabinetPipes` now takes a trial placing). Of the 117 that keep the doors' clear space and the isolator's place, the cabinet's own spot on the west wall is the cheapest (£1,376, 30.7 m); the north wall's best costs about 23 % more, and the east wall has none. So it stays, moved 0.9 m off its wall, from 0.6 m, for £12 more pipe, to give a working space behind it for the valves on its back stubs.
- Dev30, lighting styles (`?room=dev30`): six booths, each a stretch of wall and ceiling lit one way, each on its own Lumenhaus dimmer: a direct/indirect halo panel (prompt 040), cove lighting, wall grazing on blockwork, a shadow-gap slot across the ceiling and down the wall, a fibre-optic starlight ceiling (its projector's twinkle and colour wheels switchable) and a luminous stretch ceiling. Light washes (`architectural/views/light-washes.ts`) lay each fitting's light on the surfaces round it without a dynamic light apiece; the dimmer's law, the fade, the twinkle and the colour wheel are tested logic (`architectural/logic/architectural-lighting.ts`). A new maker, Lumenhaus, architectural lighting.
- Dev30's second six booths (`rooms/plant/lighting-styles-small.ts`): smaller fittings (recessed downlights and wall-washers; opal globe pendants; up/down wall cylinders), and fittings for tiled and curved walls (tile-flush glass lights and oval eyelid bulkheads on a tiled wall; a flexible LED neon line and a bendable grazer on a curved tiled wall; a bowed opal sconce wrapping a tiled round column). Light washes may now lie on curved surfaces and pool on floors; glazed wall tiles as a texture (`architectural/views/glazed-tiles.ts`).
- The balance tank hall's three visible inlet drops now carry two-sided, pipe-mounted identification plates: Olympic pool overflow 1 and 2, and mains make-up with its Type AA air gap.
- Disturbed water (`components/plant/views/disturbed-water.ts`): a tank's water surface stirred by what falls into it (the owner, 2026-10-23). Close to each source it is churned white, froth broken into boiling patches, thickest where the stream lands, with steep fast ripples; the rings spread out and die away, so further off the water is calm with only a faint drift of small waves. Each source's strength (0 still, 1 a full stream) scales it. No caustics: a dark tank's water, its foam given a little light of its own. The balance tank hall's water is it, stirred by the make-up filler and the pool's returns. Falling water now runs down: a bright, nearly solid column whose surface is displaced in and out by waves running rapidly down it, wobbling more towards its foot, its streaks travelling down as it narrows, drawn over the water it lands in, which it gives its source; and it is heard (`water-pour-sound.ts`): a fluttering splash, the low rumble of its plunge and a hiss of spray, louder and heavier the bigger its pipe, from where it lands, stopping with its room.
- The Olympic's balance tank is the world's (prompt 035, item 2): the world's water model has the tank as built (Dev28's: 9 × 6 m, its weir at 4.0 m, LOW LOW at 1.0 m, a 30 m³/h make-up), and keeps its make-up's selector (`olympic-tank.ts`, WORLD_VERSION 17): OFF; AUTO, opening below 2.9 m and shutting at 3.05 m; or HAND, filling to the weir. The tank hall draws its water's depth, its float switches, its level transmitter and its filler pouring or still from the world; the balance tank panel (Dev28's, without the test rig's bather load) stands on the Olympic plant room's west wall beside the pumps, showing the tank's level, volume, flows and floats and working its make-up and the pumps. A backwash draws the tank down; LOW LOW trips the pumps. The booster set's demand takes the Olympic's own call. Wall items facing east or west are now placed on those walls.
- The Olympic runs on one model (prompt 035, item 1): its filter battery is in the world (`world.olympic`, each bed's dirt; WORLD_VERSION 16), and the pools' step takes the Olympic's circulation from it, what its valves let through the filters' outlets and on through the contact tank and UV, and its waste, what a backwash sends to drain, drawn from the balance tank (in place of a timed midnight backwash). Each bed fouls in service (a working pool's dirt: doubled resistance in about a day and a half) and washes out in a backwash. The filters' gauges and sight glasses, the flowmeters and the plant reading's filter pressure differences all read it. The pump head is set so five filters in service give the design 620 m³/h. Unattended, the Olympic's beds now foul and its circulation falls until someone backwashes.
- A sand filter's sight glass is fitted with the bed's surface at rest across its middle (the owner, 2026-10-23): yellow sand below, showing its condition (its crust as it fouls, its unevenness when it channels), and black water above, in a vessel with no light of its own. In a backwash the bed lifts and churns and the water over it clouds a pale brown, clearing back to black as the dirt goes. It redraws only while what it shows is changing.
- A character LCD draws its characters only while they are big enough on screen to read, by the printed labels' rule (`labelOpacity`); further off it shows its glass alone and redraws nothing as its reading changes. The flowmeter's display is halved, to about 100 by 30 mm, well inside its head. A sand filter's service hatch is drawn as a bolted manway cover with a lifting handle, not a plain disc.
- Dev0's gallery has plant fittings on two of its empty plinths (`rooms/plant-gallery.ts`): the solenoid-operated butterfly valve on a DN150 spool, with COMMAND, POWER and AIR selectors and ENERGISED, OPEN and SHUT lamps, and the electromagnetic flowmeter, with a PUMP selector, a centre-zero FLOW knob that drives it either way, and a FLOWING lamp.
- Radial nozzles (the owner, 2026-10-23): a vessel may have its nozzles normal to its shell, each pipe pointing at its axis (`Item.nozzles: 'radial'`), its pipe running out along its radius until it turns onto the room's square lines. The Olympic's filters have them; the face pipework now stands clear of every sight glass and service hatch seen square on, and of the outlet's pressure gauge. An upright flowmeter's head is turned so its display sits along its long side.
- The plumber's options (the owner, 2026-10-23): what drives an automatic valve (`actuator`: motor or solenoid, beside the existing `Actuation`), and where flowmeters go (`flowmeters`: the ports whose lines take one, on the line's side of the isolating valve). A flowmeter is now a fitting the plumber places and the inline fitting drawer draws (`mag-flowmeter.ts`, its display on its head's end on an upright pipe). The Olympic plant room's plumbing takes them as `OlympicOptions` (`OLYMPIC`: all automatic, solenoid, five-valve faces, meters on the filters' outlets and the pumps' discharge), and a three-valve face and manual valves remain options.
- The Olympic's filter battery re-plumbed to five valves a filter, Dev26's (V1 inlet, V2 outlet, V3 backwash in, V4 backwash out, V5 rinse, and the vessel's drain), tagged `PL2-F<n>-V<k>`, 15 new valves in the registry (`valve-0072` to `valve-0086`); the backwash header and channel DN300; the outlet header, the pump manifold and the side-stream stubs 0.3 m higher to clear the new valves' actuators. A flowmeter on each filter's outlet riser and on the pumps' discharge, reading the battery's flows from the valves' states in the world (`game/plant/olympic-battery.ts`, through the five-valve model's new `faceFlows`), so either of its mimics will show the same. No pipe crosses a filter's sight glass or service hatch, tested.
- The Olympic plant room's valves are solenoid-operated in the game: drawn as actuated butterfly valves (`operator: 'solenoid'`), bound to the world's valve states. The room before and after its redo scored alike (`gallery/logic/olympic-comparison.ts`): the morning's room 89.8 but failing a gate (runs across open floor below headroom), the new 89.2 passing every gate, in a third less floor.
- Dev29, solenoid-operated valves (`?room=dev29`): a butterfly valve on a spring-return pneumatic actuator with a NAMUR solenoid pilot and limit switch box (`components/plant/views/actuated-butterfly-valve.ts`, its working in `logic/actuated-valve.ts`: opens on its command with power and air, fails safe shut on losing either, strokes slower the bigger it is, manual override), at DN300 and DN150, and the direct solenoid valve, each on a Penfold test station (COMMAND, POWER, AIR; ENERGISED, OPEN, SHUT lamps).
- The tank hall (`?room=tank_hall`): the Olympic pool's balance tank hall in the building, joined to the Olympic plant room through its west equipment doors, seen through and walked through without a break. It is Dev28's hall without its plant bay (`buildHall(kit, { bay: false })`); Dev28 stays as the dev room.
- The Olympic plant room installed in the game (prompt 033, stage 3): 22 × 14 m, its pool door east onto a cantilevered landing and gantry, its stair down the south wall, its machines placed by the plan and its pipework from the pipeline, its busbar ring drawn, 14 machines and 28 valves registered. The plant room shell is now drawn from a plan of any shape (`gallery/logic/shell-plan.ts`), the square rooms unchanged; walking knows whether you are on a deck or the floor. Runs never cross open floor below headroom; the battery's outlet header is overhead and its backwash header in a floor trench, so every valve is within reach. The shared support planner no longer moves a support on a small pipe further than its spacing allows.
- The Olympic plant room's plan plumbed, routed and scored by the pipeline (prompt 033, `gallery/logic/olympic-plumbing.ts`): the pump and filter battery blocks, the backwash falls and the pool's overflow and main drain across the north-west corner laid by rule; the treatment, the heat exchanger's side-stream and heating, the make-up, chemicals, ozone and power routed by the voxel router to each wall's role; every valve solenoid-operated from the panel; power by a busbar ring round the walls, each machine on a short drop from a fused tap-off, with its local isolator within reach. Acid and hypochlorite lines never run over a walkway: they come in through the south wall and stay against it, dosing onto a spool of the return main laid along the wall past the dosing pumps (the UV moved beside them); the sample line from the pump manifold to the dosing controller is routed. 51 runs, 36 fittings, every gate passed (76.6). The heat exchanger moved beside its side-stream's stubs on the battery's outlet header; the plan drawing now shows the plumbing as routed.
- The Olympic plant room's proposed 2D plan (prompt 033, stage 2), in a room of 22 × 14 m rather than the 22 m square (the machines need about 218 m², not 484): the pool door in the east wall onto a landing in the south-east corner, the stair down the south wall heading west, the main plant's doors in the south wall under the landing, the balance tank hall's in the west; the landing and a 1.2 m viewing gantry along the east wall cantilevered from the walls, with no legs. Every machine placed as the owner arranged it, at true size, against the room's doors, landing and stair, tested for clashes and access (`gallery/logic/olympic-plan.ts`), and drawn with each wall's role and the main lines' ways (`doc/plans/olympic-plant-room.svg`).
- The Olympic plant room cleared for its redo (prompt 033): an empty large hall with its three doorways, north to the pool, south to the main plant, and new equipment doors in the north half of its west wall to the balance tank hall, just south of the strip kept for a battery of five filters along the north wall (`NORTH_BATTERY`) (`plantRoomPlan(size, { tankHall })`). Its hand layout, pipework, valves and asset IDs are removed; the room as it was is `retired0`. Old saves drop the removed valves' states on loading.
- Dev28, the Olympic pool's balance tank hall (`?room=dev28`, prompt 032): a 216 m³ cast concrete tank under a steel grating gantry, blockwork above, lit by wall battens, its panel and make-up valve in a bay of the adjacent plant room behind a partition, entered through the standard equipment doors with their card reader, with a working level model (bathers' displacement, channel drain-back, auto make-up, weir spill, LOW LOW pump trip, `game/plant/balance-tank.ts`). New components for it: a stainless tank ladder, a tethered float switch, an ultrasonic level transmitter, a staff gauge, a solenoid valve, falling water, a lifebuoy, a Penfold balance tank panel, a grating deck, and board-marked concrete for rooms with no pool water. A dev room's exhibit may bar the way as it works (a shut door's leaves, `LiveExhibit.solids`). Dev rooms may set their own finish (blockwork walls, no floor, their own lights) and starting spot (`FINISH`, `START`).
- Retired rooms: a plant room's machine layout and pipes and ducts frozen as a reference for planning variants (`gallery/logic/retired-rooms.ts`). `retired0` (`?room=retired0`) is the Olympic pool's plant room as it was on 2026-10-23, drawn from its snapshot in the Olympic room's shell, and tagged `retired0` in git.
- Dev27 (`?room=dev27`), heat exchange (scenario 4, prompt 027): one gas condensing boiler (Hamwarmy, its own controller firing it: purge, ignition, modulation, lockout) on unlimited natural gas and 230 V, through a gas valve and an isolator. Its closed primary circuit runs through a twin-head circulator to a three-way valve over the shell-and-tube exchanger, the valve's bypass joining the return. Pool water from an experimental source goes through the exchanger to a sink. The exchanger is counterflow by effectiveness (ε–NTU); the boiler's output goes into its water's thermal mass, with lags on the exchanger and every thermometer (`game/plant/pool-heating.ts`). A PI controller works the valve to hold the pool outlet at its set point, and the energy balances. New digital thermometers (Kessler-Moody RTD transmitters with LED readouts) on five points and two flowmeters feed a Penfold mimic board. The board draws the whole process with each reading in place, the valve's position, the duty and the gas burnt, and has the burner, lockout and pump lamps, AUTO/MANUAL, the set point, and OPEN and CLOSE.
- The README links to the game and to each demonstration room.
- `doc/room-layout-guide.md`: one guide for agents to the room-layout pipeline (sizer, regions, placement, blocks and variants, plumber, router, scorer, bound room), with a recipe for improving a room's layout, the owner's rulings, the experiment rigs, and the known problems. `AGENTS.md` points to it.
- Every sand filter's sight glass is live, driven by a shared bed model (`plant/logic/filter-bed.ts`): the bed's dirt (from the water it is exposed to, or from its pressure difference where that is all a model gives), its pitting as a function of the time since it was last washed, and which way the water goes through it (down, filtering; up, lifted). It needs to know nothing of backwashing: water coming up through it is a wash and starts its clock again. Fitted to the plant rooms' filters, Dev19's, Dev26's Olympic filter (which starts ten days unwashed, dirty and pitted) and the small multiport filter.

### Fixed

- Touch panels no longer lose a touch that strikes their frame first (found with the CX-7, mission 105): a screen lies in its frame's face, coplanar with it, and a ray could hit the frame at the same distance, so the use went nowhere. The touch-surface kit now takes a use on the frame within a screen's opening (a curved strip's point unbent first) as a touch on that screen at that point (`frameTouch`, `unbend`), for the bridge's consoles, dev47, dev48, dev49 and the CX-7 alike; the CX-7's own workaround is gone. Nothing looks different.
- The lounge's sand filters' sight glasses follow the world's filters (the owner, 2026-10-28): each filter's own flow from its valves, so a backwash shows up through the glass, with its own dirt, fissuring and unsettled bed, as the Olympic's do; before, they showed service flow whatever the valves.
- Walking between a pool hall and its loop corridor no longer seems to zoom at the hand-over (the owner, 2026-10-28: into or out of the jacuzzi, and the footbath). The two rooms' cameras matched exactly (field of view, zoom, eye and heading, checked frame by frame across the hand-over); what changed was the corridor. Each corridor is drawn in both rooms it joins, and each copy was tiled as its own hall is: the jacuzzi's 5 cm blue mosaic in its scene, the other halls' 12.5 cm white in theirs, so the walls' tiles jumped 2.5 times in scale as the walker was handed from one copy to the other. The copies of a corridor also tiled from the world's origin, so even where the halls' tiles were alike the grout shifted where a corridor's two copies lie off the grid or turned (by stairs, or the gallery's). Corridors now have their own tile (`CORRIDOR_TILE`), laid from each corridor's own start and heading (`corridorTileFrame`; the tile material's new `origin` and `turn`), so both copies match tile for tile; and a hall's end wall stands clear of its doorway's reveal, which is the corridor's lining (they overlapped there, the hall's tile showing in its own scene and the corridor's in the next), a skin of the hall's tile over the lining's ends on the hall's face. A walker handed through a doorway also keeps its zoom (`src/main.ts`), rather than taking the zoom it last had in the room beyond.
- The bridge's RESET (the CONTROL ROOM MODE plate) is no longer undone when the game is open in more than one tab (the owner, 2026-10-27: after RESET the bridge's consoles were still lit, seen from the corridor). The cause was the save slot's second tab: a tab that does not hold the slot applied its own actions only until the holding tab's next save (within ten seconds), and the holder, another tab left in the game, still had the bridge visited, its displays on. A follower tab's actions now go to the holder (`BroadcastChannel`), which applies and saves them, so every tab's actions are kept; and a follower takes the slot up when the holder closes (`src/toolkit/save/world-store.ts`, `world-store-tabs.test.ts`).
- Placed with its eye's height given (a link's `y`, a saved place on reload, a doorway's hand-over), a walker stood on the floor under that eye rather than the one it last stood on: placed on the bridge a storey up, it had stood on the floor below and could not walk down the stair it was over.
- A saved value in a field that is null by default was dropped on loading (its type is not the null's): a starter's START or STOP given in AUTO (a pump started from the bridge or a mimic stopped again on reload), a chemical line's outlet being served. `fillDefaults` now keeps a plain value there (tested).
- The engineer's office's 240 V socket ring runs above the signs over its doors, no longer across the corridor door's opening; the south wall's socket stands beside that door rather than on its leaf; the desk socket's drop runs down beside the room's title card, not across it (the owner, 2026-10-26).
- The loading dock's yard tarmac now keeps a fixed clip at the outside wall, so moving into the yard cannot make it show through the extended building wall.
- The balance-tank hall’s lights and batten diffusers now follow the Olympic plant lighting circuit and the main lighting switches, including the shared emergency-lighting behaviour.

- The balance-tank overflow inlet has a hollow bellmouth and a recessed bore instead of overlapping end faces; the two submerged suction inlets also have open flared mouths.

- PL2-UV1’s control panel faces north into the Olympic plant room; its inlet/outlet routing and power conduit follow the rotated ports. The four heat-exchanger connections have longer straight spools so their valves and flanges clear the drawn elbows, including heating insulation.
